- **Current issue.** There is **no overflow (⋯) menu anywhere in the product** — [`RecordHeader`](../../app/shared/record-layout/RecordHeader.tsx) renders only inline primary/secondary buttons, and the Card's built-in `overflowAction` slot is passed by none of the seven modules — so there is no conventional home for secondary/destructive actions. As a result removal is scattered across four placements plus three absences: Notes = record-header "Delete" + Deleted filter (the good pattern, ADR-042); Project = archive/restore **buried in the Settings sub-tab** with no header or collection entry ([`ProjectSettingsTab.tsx`](../../app/modules/projects/ProjectSettingsTab.tsx)); Task = cancel via a drawer status `<select>`; **Area, Goal and Diary entries have no removal UI at all** despite the spine/entity kernel supporting soft-delete ([`spine-repository.ts`](../../app/kernel/spine/spine-repository.ts), [`entity-repository.ts`](../../app/kernel/entities/entity-repository.ts); mutate routes reject every non-rename intent). "How do I delete this Project or Area?" has no good answer. Confirmed in the 2026-07 audit ([`area-record-light.png`](assets/ui-audit-2026-07/area-record-light.png), [`project-record-light.png`](assets/ui-audit-2026-07/project-record-light.png)).
- **Re-audited 2026-07-27 — the shape has changed, the debt has not.** Verified against `main` at `b1a2f65`:
  - **Still no overflow menu anywhere.** `RecordHeader` renders only inline primary/secondary buttons, and a repository-wide search finds `overflowAction` referenced *only* in [`Card.tsx`](../../app/shared/card/Card.tsx)/[`card/types.ts`](../../app/shared/card/types.ts) — **no module passes it**. [DS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-12--record-header-overflow-menu--card-overflow-action) remains unstarted, so [PX-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-04--lifecycle--destructive-action-consistency) is still blocked.
  - **A de-facto convention emerged without the primitive.** Five modules now house lifecycle in a record **Settings tab**: Projects, Areas ([AREA-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-area-05--area-archive--safe-deletion-lifecycle)), People, Assets and Reviews (`*SettingsTab.tsx` / `ReviewRecord.tsx`). That is more consistent than the four-way scatter the original entry described, but it is convention by imitation, not a shared component — and it is still not where a user looks first.
  - **Three placements still disagree.** Notes = record-header "Delete" + a Deleted filter (ADR-042); Meetings = an inline "Archive meeting"/"Restore meeting" button in the record body ([`meetings/routes/detail.tsx`](../../app/modules/meetings/routes/detail.tsx)); Task = cancel via a Drawer status `<select>`.
  - **Two absences remain.** **Goals and Diary entries still have no removal UI at all**, though the kernel supports soft-delete. Areas gained theirs in AREA-05, so the original "Area, Goal and Diary" list is now "Goal and Diary".
- **Desired future state.** Implement the documented Record Header overflow (⋯) slot and adopt the Card `overflowAction`; house one shared lifecycle action set (Archive/Delete/Restore) there, consistently across modules; converge the five Settings-tab implementations and the Notes/Meetings/Task placements onto it; add the missing Goal and Diary lifecycle; gate hierarchy deletes on the existing `SpineHasActiveChildrenError` with an explanatory confirm. See §7 of the 2026-07 audit. **Reversible removal first** — permanent deletion stays behind the shared exact-title dangerous-action dialog, never a cascade.
- **Resolved 2026-07-28 by [DS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-12--record-header-overflow-menu--card-overflow-action) + [PX-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-04--lifecycle--destructive-action-consistency)** ([ADR-053](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-053-the-shared-overflow-menu-and-one-record-lifecycle-vocabulary)). The structural hole is closed: ONE shared overflow (⋯) menu ([`app/shared/overflow-menu`](../../app/shared/overflow-menu)) renders on both the Record Header and the Card, and every record's lifecycle actions live there. The wording is **derived** from `ENTITY_IDENTITY` ([`app/shared/record-lifecycle`](../../app/shared/record-lifecycle)), so the five Settings-tab implementations, the Notes header flow and the Meetings inline button now say the same words and drive the same handlers. **Both absences are closed with no migration**: Goals gained reversible delete/restore plus a `?state=deleted` view, and Diary entries gained the same. Meetings' bespoke settings section became the shared DS-10b surface. Task removal stays **Cancel** by explicit decision (ADR-053 §8), documented rather than silently omitted.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-12--record-header-overflow-menu--card-overflow-action) (the shared overflow primitive) and [PX-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-04--lifecycle--destructive-action-consistency) (the lifecycle adoption that depends on it).

### ☑ DEBT-30 — Shared EntityLink renders no entity icon, so related-record identity drifts — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28**
- **Current issue.** The one shared relationship component, [`EntityLink`](../../app/shared/entity/EntityLink.tsx), renders title text only — no `EntityIcon`/accent (identity is only in its `aria-label`). Consumers therefore drift: the Task/Project Links tabs hand-compose an `EntityIcon` beside it, but the Project overview summary rows show bare text Area/Goal links ([`ProjectOverview.tsx`](../../app/modules/projects/ProjectOverview.tsx)). The same "linked record" concept looks iconned in one place and generic in another — undercutting "the value is in the links."
- **Re-audited 2026-07-27 — the predicted collision has now happened, and the fork has doubled.** `EntityLink.tsx` still contains no icon reference at all. Entity identity itself is complete — [`identity.ts`](../../app/shared/entity/identity.ts) maps all ten entity types (`area`, `goal`, `project`, `task`, `note`, `meeting`, `person`, `asset`, `diary`, `review`) to one frozen icon + accent. But **subtype** icons have forked twice, not once: Diary's [`diary-icons.tsx`](../../app/modules/diary/diary-icons.tsx) and Assets' [`asset-icons.tsx`](../../app/modules/assets/asset-icons.tsx) each maintain a private module-local map (the Assets file explicitly cites Diary as its precedent). And the collision the original entry only *predicted* is now real: `diary-icons.tsx` maps the `meeting` **subtype** to `MeetingIcon`, which since [MEET-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-meet-01--meeting-record) is the **Meeting entity glyph** — so a Diary entry of subtype "meeting" is visually indistinguishable from a Meeting record.
- **Desired future state.** `EntityLink` gains an optional leading `EntityIcon` (default on); hand-composed icons are removed. A shared **subtype-icon registry** (distinct from entity identity) is promoted alongside `ENTITY_IDENTITY`, with Diary and Assets migrated onto it as its first two consumers, so subtype icons never fork or collide with entity icons — and the Diary `meeting` subtype is given a glyph distinct from the Meeting entity. See §5 of the 2026-07 audit.
- **Resolved 2026-07-28 by [PX-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-05--identity-adoption-entitylink-icon--subtype-icon-registry).** `EntityLink` now carries its leading `EntityIcon` by default (opt-out only where the surrounding row already shows the type); the hand-composed icons are gone, and the two byte-identical relationship rows collapsed into one shared `EntityRelationshipRow`. A shared subtype-icon registry ([`app/shared/entity/subtype-icons.tsx`](../../app/shared/entity/subtype-icons.tsx)) replaced the two private maps, and the collision was fixed **at its root** rather than reassigned: the shared icon set gained dedicated subtype glyphs so **no** subtype wears an entity glyph, enforced by a test.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PX-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-05--identity-adoption-entitylink-icon--subtype-icon-registry).

### ◐ DEBT-31 — Cross-module presentation drift: Today/Diary forks, terminology and capitalisation — P2 — **presentation half RESOLVED 2026-07-28**
- **Current issue.** Today does not use the shared [`EmptyState`](../../app/shared/empty-state/EmptyState.tsx) (each section renders a bare inline `<p>` with no icon or CTA — [`TodayDashboard.tsx`](../../app/modules/today/TodayDashboard.tsx)) and its primary "Quick capture" action is a bespoke, currently-inert button rather than the shared create pattern. Diary ships **two** primary entry points (header button + a floating FAB), forks the shared Card (a hand-rolled `dh-diary-entry` timeline node) and uses the Inspector rather than the shared Record Layout. Create labels and count nouns drift in capitalisation ("New Area"/"New Goal" title-case vs "New project"/"New task"/"New note" lower-case vs "Quick capture"/"New entry"), empty-title copy drifts ("No tasks here" vs "No projects yet" vs "Your diary is empty"), the Links tab is "Key links" on Projects but "Links" on Tasks, and Diary uses a curly apostrophe where others use ASCII. Confirmed across the 2026-07 audit's collection screenshots.
- **Re-audited 2026-07-27 — Today is now the sole `EmptyState` holdout.** Every other product surface has adopted the shared component: Areas, Assets, Diary, Goals, Meetings, Notes, People, Projects, Reviews and Tasks all import `~/shared/empty-state`, and `/today/waiting` does too — but `TodayDashboard.tsx` and `landing/widgets.tsx` contain **zero** `EmptyState` references. Quick Capture is also confirmed still inert: `onCapture` sets the notice *"Quick Capture is not connected yet. Your draft has not been saved."* and writes nothing ([`TodayDashboard.tsx`](../../app/modules/today/TodayDashboard.tsx)). That message is honest — it is not a lying CTA — but the app's most prominent primary action still does not create a record.
- **Desired future state.** Today adopts the shared `EmptyState` and create pattern (and Quick Capture is wired to real creation); Diary keeps its excellent timeline visual but reduces to one primary entry point per viewport and, where feasible, expresses the timeline node as a Card variant so it inherits shared behaviour; a copy/capitalisation convention is added to [`DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`](../design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md) and swept across all collections; tab labels converge ("Links"). See §8–§10 of the 2026-07 audit.
- **Resolved 2026-07-28 by [PX-06](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-06--cross-module-polish--copy-convention), except the two items that were never in its scope.** Today adopted the shared `EmptyState` (a new `size="compact"` variant sized for a widget) — it was the last holdout. The copy convention is now **executable**: create/empty/count labels derive from `ENTITY_IDENTITY` ([`app/shared/entity/copy.ts`](../../app/shared/entity/copy.ts)), so drift is structurally impossible rather than merely discouraged; the written rule lives in `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` and every collection was swept to it, apostrophes included. Diary dropped its `+` glyph and now shows exactly one primary entry point per viewport. Tab labels converged on `Linked`, and the Person record's `Timeline` became `Activity` in its correct position. **Still open, by design:** Quick Capture remains inert pending [TODAY-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-today-07--quick-capture-wiring), and the Diary timeline node remains a **deliberate, documented fork** — expressing it as a Card variant risked losing the best scanning surface in the product for no user-visible gain, so the visual was preserved intact.
- **Today's half was re-decided by the 2026-08 Today redesign, and the files named above no longer exist.** Today does not render the shared `EmptyState` any more, deliberately: on a screen whose whole point is a short quiet day, an icon + headline + description + button makes "nothing is due" the largest object on the page. Its two empty cases are now single lines — "Nothing planned today. Capture anything new with the + button." and the rail's "All clear" — and that rule is written into [`DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` → The Today screen](../design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md#the-today-screen). Quick Capture is likewise gone from Today: the global `+` is the one capture affordance, which is what TODAY-07 was asking for. The Diary timeline-node fork is what remains of this entry.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PX-06](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-px-06--cross-module-polish--copy-convention) (empty-state, copy, Diary entry-point and tab-label polish) and [TODAY-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-today-07--quick-capture-wiring) (making Quick Capture functional).

### ☐ DEBT-32 — Today personalisation is per-device, not synced — P3
- **Current issue.** TODAY-08 remembers the Today widget arrangement (hide/move/pin/collapse) in `localStorage`, so it is per-device and does not follow the owner across devices. This is a deliberate first cut ([ADR-045](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-045-today-as-the-command-centre--the-personalisable-widget-landing-surface-over-real-cross-module-reads) §2): the arrangement is a cosmetic UI preference with no entity content, so it was kept out of the server-derived workspace rather than over-coupling a nicety to the security boundary.
- **Desired future state.** When a workspace/user preference store exists (SET-01 territory), a synced arrangement can adopt the SAME pure `landing/layout.ts` model unchanged — only the persistence seam (`useTodayLayout`) swaps from `localStorage` to the preference store. No widget or model change required.
- **Related roadmap item.** A future Settings/preferences item (SET-01 and beyond).
- **SET-01 note.** SETTINGS-01A introduced the owner/workspace preference store but deliberately did **not** migrate Today arrangement into it; the device-local boundary remains a product decision until synced widget layout is explicitly prioritised.
- **THEME-01 note (2026-07-31).** The theme preference has now moved INTO that store for exactly the reason this entry defers: a personal choice that does not follow the owner between devices is a broken one. That makes the device-local arrangement newly inconsistent rather than merely deferred — recorded as [DEBT-55](#-debt-55--today-widget-arrangement-is-still-device-local-while-the-theme-is-not--p3), which also notes that THEME-01 has proven the migration shape end to end.
- **⚠ The code this entry describes no longer exists (verified 2026-08-16 by [TODAY-11](../design/TODAY_11_COMMAND_CENTRE_2026_08.md)).** The 2026-08-07 Today redesign removed the widget system outright — `useTodayLayout` and `landing/layout.ts` are gone, and `grep -rn "useTodayLayout" app` returns nothing. There is no arrangement in `localStorage`, because there is no arrangement. This entry and DEBT-55 both now describe a *capability the product deliberately dropped* rather than a persistence seam waiting to be swapped, and the two must be re-decided together: **either** Today regains a personalisation system (a feature, with its own roadmap item, not a seam swap) **and** it is synced from the start, **or** both entries close as "not applicable — Today is one composed screen". TODAY-11 deliberately did neither: rebuilding Today to `MOCKUP 5.png` is a composition change, and inventing a widget registry inside it would have been exactly the creep that pass was told to avoid.

### ◐ DEBT-33 — Settings changes are not yet represented in Activity — P3 — NARROWED 2026-08-08
- **Original issue.** SET-01 persists owner/workspace preferences, but preference mutations do not append Activity rows. The accepted Activity model is entity-subject based, and forcing Settings into a fake entity subject would pollute the audit model. Preference values can also be sensitive or identifying, so arbitrary before/after payloads are not acceptable.
- **The blocking half is resolved (2026-08-08, with SET-03).** The "deliberate workspace/owner-scoped audit shape for non-entity system changes" this entry asked for now exists, and it is not a second audit log: `buildWorkspaceActivityWriteModel` validates a SUBJECT-LESS event, `WorkspaceEventRecorder` appends it to the SAME `activities` table with the same trusted actor, and it surfaces in the workspace feed while appearing in no record's Timeline. `buildActivityWriteModel`'s "at least one subject" rule is untouched, so a subject-less event has to be asked for by name. Payloads carry structural metadata only — counts and enumerated literals — exactly as this entry required. See [`ACTIVITY_TIMELINE.md → Workspace-scoped events`](../development/ACTIVITY_TIMELINE.md#workspace-scoped-events-set-03-2026-08-08).
- **What remains.** Only the adoption: `security.signed_out` and `security.local_data_cleared` are recorded, and ORDINARY preference changes (timezone, date format, navigation visibility, module defaults, AI policy) still are not. That is now a small, mechanical extension rather than a modelling problem.
- **Closing condition.** A representative preference change appends a structural workspace-scoped event, with a kernel test proving the payload carries no preference VALUE that could identify the owner.
- **Related roadmap item.** A future Settings hardening item building on [SET-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-set-01--app--workspace-settings--core-preferences). **Related ADR.** [ADR-082](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-082-a-nonce-based-content-security-policy-one-header-authority-and-a-security-surface-that-refuses-to-overclaim) decision 9.

### ☐ DEBT-34 — Reviews period context and Today integration are bounded first cuts — P2
- **Current issue.** REVIEWS-01 presents bounded live Tasks/Diary/Meetings context through existing repositories and ships the clean Reviews module seam, but it does not yet provide a complete unbounded period aggregation service, immutable source-record snapshots, Projects-updated-in-period facts, or a Today card for "Start/Continue current weekly Review." The Review remains correct and durable because authored reflection and links are stored, but context is a live helper rather than a complete historical snapshot.
- **Desired future state.** Add a dedicated Reviews period-context service that composes source repositories with explicit pagination/summary semantics for Tasks, Projects, Diary and Meetings; add a modest Today contribution that shows the active current-period Review or Start weekly review action; and, if the owner needs historical source labels frozen, design a small snapshot mechanism rather than copying entire source records into Review tables.
- **Related roadmap item.** [REVIEW-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-review-03--insights--alignment) for derived period facts/snapshots and a future Today refinement for the current-week entry point.
- **Partially resolved 2026-08-05 by [REVIEW-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-02--weekly-review--delivered-2026-08-05), with evidence.** The named gap "**Projects-updated-in-period facts**" is closed: the guided flow adds a bounded Project review projection over `ProjectRepository` + PROJ-02's `ProjectHealthRepository` (Area/Goal context, workflow status, derived health, open/overdue/waiting counts, Tasks completed in the period, last activity, a deterministic next action), with a documented selection and ordering rule, an ASSERTED per-step query budget measured against real D1, and two tests proving the counts are flat with respect to workspace size. The context also stopped being unbounded-or-silent: where a list is bounded the surface states the truth ("4+") and links to the canonical destination, and the Inbox total is the authoritative grouped aggregate rather than "how many were loaded". See [`REVIEWS_MODULE.md → Project review projection`](../development/REVIEWS_MODULE.md#project-review-projection) and [ADR-072](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-072-the-guided-weekly-review--one-review-two-presentations-a-canonical-step-model-and-the-smallest-possible-persisted-workflow-state) decisions 7 and 8.
- **Narrowed again 2026-08-08 by [REVIEW-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-03--insights--alignment), with evidence.** The named gap "**derived period facts**" is closed: the Review now derives, from the owner's own canonical data, what completed in the period (exact, from the append-only Activity stream, for **any** period past or present), where that completed work landed by Project / Goal / Area, what was already outstanding when the period began, how Project health moved since the previous Review, and a bounded trend over recent Reviews of the same type. A **small explicit snapshot mechanism** was designed rather than avoided — but deliberately NOT the "immutable source-record snapshot" this entry originally imagined: `review_insight_snapshots` stores **derived facts only** (ids, states, counts), never a copy of a record body, precisely so a renamed Project still renders under its live title. Every read is a bounded, grouped aggregate under an **asserted 14-statement budget** proven flat with respect to workspace size, so "a complete unbounded period aggregation service" is no longer the desired state — an unbounded aggregation is the thing the budget exists to prevent.
- **Deliberately still open in this entry.** The Today "Start/Continue this week's Review" card. REVIEW-02 built the *flow* and REVIEW-03 built the *evidence*; neither touched Today, and a Today entry point is a Today read-model change rather than a Reviews one. Immutable source-record snapshots stay out of scope on purpose: the REVIEW-03 audit concluded that freezing source labels would make a Review disagree with the records it points at, which is worse than a label moving.

### ☐ DEBT-35 — Assets: deferred capabilities (attachments, reminders, logbooks, ingestion, AI) — P3
- **Current issue.** ASSET-01 ships a deliberately complete-but-bounded first version: no real file attachments, no external object storage (R2), no OCR, no barcode/QR scanning, no recurring reminders or automated warranty/renewal alerts, no service-history logbook, no depreciation/tax or insurance-claims workflow, no receipt/email ingestion, no external subscription sync, no AI extraction, and no household sharing beyond current workspace rules. These are intentionally absent (no dead UI ships for any of them), but they are the natural next capabilities users will expect. The kernel already stores the queryable warranty/renewal/service dates and exposes `AssetRepository.list({ view: "expiring" | "service_due" })` as the bounded read seam a future Today widget / reminder engine will consume.
- **Desired future state.** ASSET-02 adds history & renewals (maintenance log, value-over-time, calm renewal reminders reading the existing dates) and a Today "expiring soon / service due" widget over the existing read seam; a later attachments story adds real object storage (R2) with OCR/barcode as optional enrichment — all reusing the shared seams, never a bespoke fork, and each documented with an ADR where it introduces a genuine new decision.
- **Related roadmap item.** [ASSET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-asset-02--history--renewals--done); [ASSET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-asset-03--mobile).
- **Still open after [ASSET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-asset-03--mobile-assets) (2026-08-08).** ASSET-03 made creating an Asset from a phone fast — the global `+` offers Asset and the type picker became a phone control — and deliberately added **none** of the capabilities in this entry. No scanning, no OCR, no lookup, no attachments, no AI classification, no reminders: a phone camera next to a capture form is exactly where those would have crept in, and they stay recorded here rather than half-built.
- **Scope boundary (2026-07-27).** OCR, barcode/QR scanning, receipt or email ingestion, financial depreciation/tax workflows and external subscription sync stay in **this** entry as future debt. They are explicitly **not** part of [ASSET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-asset-02--history--renewals--done), whose scope is service/maintenance history, warranty and renewal tracking, value changes, calm due/expiry surfacing and the Today read seam.

### ☑ DEBT-36 — Global search coverage is incomplete: several shipped modules register no provider — P2
- **Original issue.** DS-08 discovered search providers from the module registry, but only **five** manifests contributed one: Assets, Meetings, People, Reviews and Tasks (plus the fixture-backed Today provider). **Notes, Projects, Areas, Goals and Diary declared no `searchProviders` at all**, even though all five shipped real, workspace-scoped records. Global search therefore could not find a note, a project, an area, a goal or a diary entry by title — while simultaneously returning **fabricated** project/note/meeting hits from the Today fixture provider ([DEBT-17](#-debt-17--today-search-provider-is-fixture-backed-not-over-real-records--p1)). Command-palette coverage remains separate: `commands.ts` exists for Assets, Meetings, People, Reviews, Settings, Tasks and Today, but **Areas, Goals, Projects, Notes and Diary register no commands**, so there is no "Open Notes" or "New project" anywhere in the palette.
- **Impact before resolution.** The product's central promise — one searchable, cross-linked model — was not met for half the shipped modules, and the results a user *did* get were partly invented. This was the single largest gap between what DalyHub claimed and what a user could do in Search.
- **Desired state.** Each shipped module registers a real, repository-backed search provider returning canonical record targets (following the [`tasks/search.ts`](../../app/modules/tasks/search.ts) and [`reviews/search.ts`](../../app/modules/reviews/search.ts) precedent). The Today fixture provider is retired once nothing depends on it. No new search architecture — the registry seam already exists.
- **Narrowed (2026-07-28, NOTES-03) — the Notes half closed first.** Notes registered a real, repository-backed provider over **title, full Markdown body, headings and tags** (the first provider that searches a record's *body*, not just its header fields), plus five navigation commands. Each result states where it matched and shows a readable, syntax-free excerpt. The remaining gap before X-01 was Projects, Areas, Goals and Diary, plus the still-registered Today fixture provider ([DEBT-17](#-debt-17--today-search-provider-is-fixture-backed-not-over-real-records--p1)).
- **Related roadmap item.** [X-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-x-01--global-search-maturity); [NOTES-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-notes-03--organisation--search) ☑ for the Notes half; [DS-08](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-08--shared-search)/[DS-09](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-09--command-palette--quick-actions) for the shared seams.
- **Resolved (2026-07-29).** X-01 completes Search provider coverage for every
  shipped record type: Areas, Goals, Projects, Tasks, Notes, Diary, Meetings,
  People, Assets and Reviews. Areas/Goals/Projects/Diary gained real providers
  through their module manifests; Tasks now uses a richer bounded projection; the
  existing Notes/Meetings/People/Assets/Reviews providers remain real. Today is no
  longer a Search provider. `test/unit/module-registry/discovery.test.ts` asserts
  the exact production provider set, and `test/kernel/search-route.test.ts` seeds
  real records across all ten providers through the actual authenticated `/search`
  route.

### ☑ DEBT-37 — On-hold tasks appear on Today but are excluded from `/tasks` active planning views — P2
- **Current issue.** [TASKS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-01--first-class-tasks-module) added `on_hold` as a first-class workflow status, and the `/tasks` `active` system view deliberately excludes it alongside completed, cancelled, Someday/Maybe and waiting ([`app/kernel/tasks/task.ts`](../../app/kernel/tasks/task.ts), ADR-043 §11) so a paused task stays out of a Matrix quadrant or Time Sector. But Today's planning read applies only `commitment_state <> 'someday' AND status <> 'cancelled'` ([`d1-task-repository.ts` `listPlanningTasks`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-task-repository.ts)), so **an on-hold task still appears in Today's planning buckets** — and Today's `PlanningTaskItem` projection ([`planning-view.ts`](../../app/modules/today/task/planning-view.ts)) carries no `status`/`commitmentState`, so it renders indistinguishably from ordinary active work with no "On hold" label.
- **Impact.** The same task is "parked" on `/tasks` and "today's work" on `/today`. It contradicts [DEBT-28](#-debt-28--task-priority-is-invisible-where-triage-happens-and-status-resolves-three-different-ways--p2)'s resolution, which retired `taskDisplayStatus` precisely so every surface would resolve state identically — Today is the one surface that still does not run `taskDisplayState` at all.
- **Desired future state.** Decide the intent once and apply it in one place: either exclude `on_hold` from `listPlanningTasks` exactly as `/tasks` does, or widen the Today projection to carry the status and render the shared "On hold" state so the card is honest. Do not add a third state vocabulary.
- **Resolved (2026-07-31) by [TASKS-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-04--daily-driver-tasks-inbox-inline-editing-and-basic-recurrence).** The intent was decided ONCE and applied in one place: `listPlanningTasks` now excludes `on_hold` alongside Someday/Maybe and cancelled ([`d1-task-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-task-repository.ts)), so a paused Task is not "today's work" on one surface and "parked" on another. No third state vocabulary was added and Today's projection is unchanged in shape. Proven by [`test/kernel/task-inbox-parent.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/task-inbox-parent.test.ts), which seeds one Task per state (active, in progress, waiting, on hold, Someday/Maybe, cancelled, completed) all scheduled for the same day and asserts the planning bands and the `/tasks` active view AGREE on every one of them — plus that a recurring successor shows on Today as ordinary active work and that completions stay visible so "completed today" still works.
- **Half of it came back, in the other direction, and TODAY-10 closed it (2026-08-12).** TASKS-04 applied the decision to `listPlanningTasks` (which Today reads) and left the canonical `/tasks` **date** views alone, so the same Task was "parked" on Today and "today's work" on `/tasks?system=today` — the original defect with the surfaces swapped, and invisible until TODAY-09 made the two surfaces' agreement an explicit contract. Measured on the heavy fixture with one on-hold Task due today: `/tasks?system=today` said "14 Tasks"; Today's own figure, which links straight to that view, said 12. The intent was NOT re-decided — this entry's decision stands, and `notParked` in [`d1-task-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-task-repository.ts) now applies it to `today`, `upcoming` and `overdue` alongside the `waiting` exclusion those three already carried. `inbox` is deliberately untouched: it is about filing, not dating, and has never excluded waiting either. Proven by the set-equivalence block in [`test/kernel/today-route.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/today-route.test.ts) and by [`e2e/today-focus.spec.ts`](../../e2e/today-focus.spec.ts).
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-04--daily-driver-tasks-inbox-inline-editing-and-basic-recurrence), [TASKS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-01--first-class-tasks-module) / [TODAY-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-today-04--planning); [TODAY-10](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md) for the follow-on.

### ☑ DEBT-38 — Notification toasts occlude bottom-anchored record actions — P1
- **Resolved (2026-07-27).** The DS-10 notification region no longer takes pointer input anywhere except its own controls. In [`app/styles/feedback.css`](../../app/styles/feedback.css), `.dh-feedback`, `.dh-feedback__toolbar` and `.dh-toast` are all `pointer-events: none`; only `.dh-feedback__dismiss-all`, `.dh-toast__action` and `.dh-toast__close` opt back in. Toasts stay fully visible and their Undo/Retry/Cancel/Dismiss controls stay operable; every other pixel of the region passes the click through to the page. One shared fix in the one shared feedback layer — no Reviews-only CSS, no test-only override, no forced or coordinate clicks anywhere in the suite.
- **What the original entry got half-right.** It identified the container/opt-in structure correctly but attributed the failure mainly to the toast stack. Measured on this branch (a probe over the real Reviews Settings tab at 1280×720): the *Restore review* button occupied y 425–470 and the **dismiss-all toolbar** occupied y 445–467 across the full 384px column, so `document.elementFromPoint` at the button's centre returned `div.dh-feedback__toolbar`. The toolbar is a full-width flex row containing one small right-aligned button — pure empty area absorbing clicks. The toast bodies are the second offender: the region grows upward from `bottom: 16px`, so five stacked toasts blanket y≈287–704 and can cover the same control. Both are fixed by the same rule set.
- **Hover-pause narrowed, deliberately and documented.** Auto-dismiss now pauses on focus anywhere in the region, or on hovering a toast's *controls*, rather than on hovering any part of a toast. A pointer crossing a click-through surface is interacting with the page beneath, not reading the toast, so pausing on it would prolong the very obstruction this fixes. Errors remain sticky, so nothing that must be read expires. Recorded in [`DESIGN_SYSTEM.md → Feedback`](../design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md#feedback-platform) and [`FEEDBACK_AND_INSPECTOR.md`](../development/FEEDBACK_AND_INSPECTOR.md).
- **Evidence.** Two guards, both of which fail on the pre-fix CSS (verified by reverting it): [`test/unit/feedback/notification-pointer-events.test.ts`](../../test/unit/feedback/notification-pointer-events.test.ts) checks the rule set at source; `e2e/feedback.spec.ts` → *"the notification stack never intercepts clicks meant for the page"* hit-tests the **whole** region with `document.elementFromPoint` on a 4px grid at **both** 1280×720 and 390×780, asserting no non-control pixel is hit-testable, that every control still is, and that Undo and Dismiss-all still work with ordinary clicks. End to end, `e2e/reviews.spec.ts` (*archive → restore*) passes, including on repeat runs — see [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02).
- **Related roadmap item.** [DS-10](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-10--inspector-settings-and-feedback-states) (the shared feedback layer); unblocks [REVIEWS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-reviews-01--dalyhub-reviews-foundation) verification.

### ☑ DEBT-39 — `[[Wiki Links]]` create no EntityLink, and the resolver scans the whole workspace — P2
- **Current issue.** REL-01's wiki links are resolved at **navigation** time: [`wikilinks.ts`](../../app/platform/markdown/wikilinks.ts) rewrites `[[Title]]` to `/notes/resolve?title=…` and [`notes/routes/resolve.tsx`](../../app/modules/notes/routes/resolve.tsx) finds a matching entity and redirects. Two consequences follow.
  1. **No backlink is created.** Because no EntityLink row is written, a wiki-linked record has no idea it was referenced — the reference is invisible from the target's side and absent from its Linked Items and Activity. This is the substantive part of what [NOTES-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-notes-02--linking--backlinks) still owes, distinct from the *presentation* gap in [DEBT-08](#-debt-08--ad-hoc-cross-entity-links--p2).
  2. **The resolver is an unbounded read.** It pages through **every** entity in the workspace with a deliberate "no page cutoff" (documented in the route), stopping early only on a note match. That is a correct-but-unbounded scan on a hot navigation path, contrary to the [performance expectations](../../AGENTS.md#16-performance-expectations) every other read honours with bounded, cursor-paginated queries.
- **Resolved (2026-07-28, NOTES-02).** The decision this entry asked for was made and recorded as [ADR-054](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-054-note-knowledge--a-wiki-link-is-a-persisted-reference-and-knowledge-relationships-stay-entitylinks): **a wiki link in a saved note is a persisted reference.** Both halves are closed.
  1. **A relationship is now written.** `reconcileNoteReferences` ([`note-references.ts`](../../app/platform/entity-links/note-references.ts)) makes the note's typed `note.references` EntityLinks exactly match the `[[…]]` in its body on every content save, so a referenced record shows the reference in its Backlinks and both records' Timelines carry the kernel's `entity_link.*` events. Titles are matched ONCE, at save time, and the relationship is persisted by **stable id** — renaming the target keeps it, which is why "no fragile title-based matching" and "renaming does not break links" are both true. Duplicates collapse to one row; a reference inside code is never a relationship; removing the last mention unlinks; re-adding restores the SAME link id rather than duplicating. Reconciliation only ever touches `note.references` links whose SOURCE is that note, so nothing a user created by hand is disturbed, and it never fails the save (the Markdown source is the canonical record).
  2. **The unbounded read is gone.** Title resolution is `NoteQueryRepository.resolveReferenceTargets` — ONE bounded, chunked, indexed `lower(title) IN (…)` query with a total, stable tie-break (a Note wins, then the earliest-created record) — instead of paging every entity in the workspace with no cutoff.
- **Evidence.** `test/kernel/notes-knowledge.test.ts` (real Workers/D1) covers each row of that table, plus cross-workspace and self-reference refusal and reconciliation idempotency; `test/unit/markdown/note-document.test.ts` covers the code-block, malformed-input and duplicate rules purely; `e2e/notes-knowledge.spec.ts` drives a wiki link becoming a visible backlink in a real browser, and a plain title mention creating nothing.
- **Related roadmap item.** [NOTES-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-notes-02--linking--backlinks) ☑; [REL-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-rel-01--universal-relationship-system-shared-linked-items).

### ☐ DEBT-40 — Two migrations share the number `0013` — P3
- **Current issue.** `migrations/0013_create_area_details.sql` ([AREA-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-area-05--area-archive--safe-deletion-lifecycle), merged in #65) and `migrations/0013_create_person_details.sql` ([PEOPLE-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-people-01--person-record), merged in #66) were authored in parallel and both claimed `0013`. Wrangler applies migrations in filename order and tracks them by name, and the two slices are independent, so there is **no known defect today** — `area_details` simply applies before `person_details`, deterministically.
- **Impact.** Low now, latent later. The numbering is the only ordering contract the repository has; once it is not unique, "apply in order" stops being verifiable by inspection, and a future pair of same-numbered migrations with a real dependency between them would order by alphabetical accident. It also makes "which migration is `0013`?" ambiguous in every doc and ADR that cites a number.
- **Desired future state.** Do **not** rename an applied migration (both are live in production). Instead: add a CI check that fails on a duplicate numeric prefix, and record the convention — claim the next number at PR-open time, and renumber before merge if another PR took it. Cite `0013_create_area_details` / `0013_create_person_details` by full filename, never by number alone.
- **The check now exists (V2 release closure, 2026-08-01) — [`test/unit/migrations/migration-numbering.test.ts`](../../test/unit/migrations/migration-numbering.test.ts).** It runs in the ordinary unit suite, so it gates every PR through the existing CI job with no new workflow. It asserts three things: the `NNNN_snake_case_description.sql` naming, that no number is issued twice, and that the sequence has no gap so "apply in order" stays verifiable by inspection. The historical collision is grandfathered **by exact filename, as a pair** — not by number — so it cannot be used to excuse a second one, and a *third* file numbered `0013` still fails. Neither live migration was renamed.
- **Why it stays ☐ rather than ☑.** The check closes the "latent later" half. The entry's other half — the ambiguity of *"which migration is `0013`?"* in every doc and ADR that cites a number — is a documentation property the check cannot enforce, and both files are still live in production. It closes when the citation convention has been swept through the docs that name a number.
- **Related roadmap item.** [FND-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-fnd-02--data-kernel-entities--storage) (the migration convention); [SETUP_AND_CI](../development/SETUP_AND_CI.md).

### ☑ DEBT-41 — The E2E suite is unreliable on `main`, so "CI is green" claims are unverifiable — P1 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-02**
- **Resolved (V2.0.1 closure, 2026-08-02).** The entry's own closing condition —
  *"the complete gate reports reliably **and** current `main`-equivalent CI is
  green"* — is now met on evidence: after the V2 release closure (#97) merged,
  `main` @ `a06e41c` ran the full 14-shard configuration and the **CI Gate
  passed** (run 30726027912, 2026-08-02). The entry was deliberately held open
  so it could not be closed by the change that hoped to fix it; that change has
  now merged and the trunk is green.
- **One factual correction, recorded rather than silently edited.** The
  narrative below (and the CI workflow comment, corrected in V2.0.1) claimed run
  30698894216 "reproduced" the shard-4-ceiling shape of run 30693899680. It did
  not: that run's failure was shard 10/10 failing ordinarily after ~7.5 minutes
  with its report uploaded, and shard 4/10 succeeded. The 10 → 14 split decision
  stands on 30693899680 alone.
- **The standing discipline survives the entry.** "Treat a red trunk as
  stop-the-line, no item marked ☑ while `main`'s CI Gate is failing" is a rule
  of [`AGENTS.md §14`](../../AGENTS.md#14-testing-philosophy)-adjacent practice
  now, not a debt — and V2.0.1 adds enforcement teeth: the production deploy
  itself refuses a release commit without a successful CI Gate
  (`scripts/deploy-production.mjs`, `test/unit/deploy/release-preflight.test.ts`),
  and branch protection instructions + a checklist verification item exist in
  [`SETUP_AND_CI.md`](../development/SETUP_AND_CI.md).
- **The two V2 release blockers, found here and fixed here (V2 release closure, 2026-08-01).** This entry's discipline — *"treat a red trunk as stop-the-line"* — is what turned up both. `main` was red on the two most recent runs, and neither cause was a product defect:
  1. **A stale assertion on a surface the product had deliberately deleted.** `e2e/today.spec.ts:89` asserted a `[data-widget="focus"]` panel with a `Focus` toggle. [UX-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ux-01--daily-driver-ux-ui-and-product-polish) **removed** the "coming soon" Focus panel from Today — correctly, for the same reason POLISH-01 removed Weather and the calendar placeholder — but the spec was not updated with it, so from #95 onward `main` failed on a widget whose absence was the intended outcome. Fixed by taking the widget under test from the RENDERED catalogue (`[data-widget]` and its title) instead of naming one, so the test covers the personalisation behaviour it was written for and stops encoding which widgets happen to exist. **This is the failure mode this entry exists to catch, in its least dramatic form:** a green product and a red gate, where the test was wrong.
  2. **The shard budget was exceeded again — the third time, with the same shape.** Run 30693899680 (`main` @ `ed666ca`) shard 4 of 10 reached Playwright's 900s `globalTimeout` with **102 passed, ZERO failed and 1 test never run**; run 30698894216 (`main` @ `6b50930`, the X-04 merge) reproduced it. MEASURED per-shard test time on 30693899680: 6m24 / 10m22 / 8m11 / **15m01+** / 4m28 / 4m21 / 4m14 / 4m11 / 13m41 / 7m46 — about **79 minutes** of tests against a ten-way split, so the mean was ~8 min and the worst shard was over the ceiling. NOTES-05, ASSET-02, UX-01 and X-04 each added specs. Split to **fourteen** shards, which is the same remedy and the same reasoning as the 3→5, 5→7 and 7→10 splits: **split finer rather than raise the ceiling**, because raising the ceiling only moves the line the growth is measured against and drags `timeout-minutes` up with it. Intra-shard `workers` stays 1, deliberately — a shard's tests share one dev server and one local D1, and some mutate owner-level state, so concurrent workers would trade a budget problem for a correctness one.
- **A THIRD defect, found by running the complete suite locally rather than trusting the shards.** `people.spec.ts:168` ("no WCAG violations, 44px touch targets and no overflow from 320px up") failed, and unlike the two above it **reproduced deterministically with nothing else running**. It was not a product defect and not contention: the test performed a record creation, a touch-target check, **eighteen navigations** (nine viewports x collection + record) and an axe scan inside ONE 30-second per-test budget. It fitted on a fast CI runner and did not fit on a slower machine — a reliable-looking test one bad runner away from failing, which is precisely this entry's subject.
  - **Fixed by splitting the test, not the assertion.** Same viewports, same overflow assertion on both surfaces, same touch target, same axe scan — repackaged as one test per viewport, which is how [`responsive.spec.ts`](../../e2e/responsive.spec.ts) already packages this matrix. Measured after the split, on the same slow machine: **13 tests, all green, each ~15s** — half the budget, against a combined test that exceeded it. It also distributes, because Playwright shards by test COUNT and one 30-second test is indivisible across runners.
  - **This is the same remedy as the shard split, one level down.** The budget only ever has to cover the worst UNIT, so make the unit smaller rather than the budget bigger.
  - **The sibling test, and a correction to this entry's first draft.** `project-health.spec.ts` has a test of *exactly* the same shape — the same nine-viewport matrix, the same 18 page loads, plus two axe scans. It was first recorded here as an unfixed fragile neighbour. **That was wrong, and reading it settled the question:** it already carries `test.setTimeout(90_000)` with a comment saying MOBILE-01 grew `RESPONSIVE_VIEWPORTS` from seven checkpoints to nine, that the loop therefore does 18 loads instead of 14, and that *"the budget is raised to match the added coverage; every assertion is unchanged."* It is not fragile and it needs no fix.
  - **So the repository already had a precedent for this exact pattern, and it was to raise the per-test budget.** That is worth stating plainly, because the shard work in this entry has repeatedly rejected *"raise the ceiling"* — and the two are not the same thing. Raising a **shard or global** ceiling pins the worst shard against whatever the new ceiling is and hides a growing suite. Raising **one test's** timeout to match coverage that genuinely grew is a different, legitimate move, and the sibling documents exactly why it was taken.
  - **Splitting was still chosen for People, deliberately.** Both fixes preserve every assertion. The split additionally distributes: Playwright shards by test COUNT, so one 90-second test is indivisible across runners while nine ~15s tests are not, and a failure names the viewport that broke instead of the whole matrix. If `project-health` ever needs touching for another reason, splitting it the same way would be an improvement — but it is not failing, and changing a passing test in a release closure is scope this needs no part of.

  - **The real shape of it, found by measuring instead of guessing (2026-08-01).** After three separate specs failed on 30-second timeouts, the full local run's per-test durations were tabulated rather than re-diagnosed one at a time. **23 tests take 20 seconds or more against the 30-second default**, and the pattern is unmistakable: **every spec whose author had already hit this had already sized its own budget** — `tasks.spec`, `tasks-collection`, `people-timeline`, `people-relationship`, `meetings-people-history`, `project-health` and `tasks-journey`'s own accessibility block all carry a local `setTimeout`. The specs still failing were simply the ones that had not failed *yet*. This is not flakiness; it is a default sized for short tests being applied to multi-step journeys.
  - **Sized, with the measurement recorded, before they fail rather than after:** `tasks-journey`'s full-journey block (22.5s / 27.2s measured), `areas-goals-mobile`'s mobile block (**55.1s**, 23.2s, 21.7s), `projects-mobile`'s block (35.1s, 25.4s, 20.0s), `assets-ownership`'s five-theme sweep (31.9s) and `notes-knowledge`'s (28.9s). No assertion changed in any of them.
  - **A correction to this entry's own earlier reading.** `areas-goals-mobile:111` was recorded as "proven environmental — passes in isolation". That was incomplete: with a budget it actually fits, the test takes **55.1 seconds**. It was never an environmental blip, it was over budget and occasionally lucky. Passing in isolation proved only that isolation is faster, not that the test was sound.
  - **The distinction that makes this legitimate, stated once.** Raising the **`globalTimeout` / shard budget** is the move this entry has rejected three times, and rightly: it pins the worst SHARD against a moving line and hides a growing suite. A **per-test** timeout is a different thing — a bound on how long ONE interaction may take — and 30 seconds was never sized for a journey that creates a record, opens a Drawer, edits it and walks four lifecycle states. Sizing that to the work is what the sibling specs already did.
  - **Deliberately left, and recorded rather than swept:** the remaining bare tests in the 20-27s band (`assets.spec:62`, `people.spec:95`, `settings.spec:69`, and `areas-goals-mobile`'s two shorter cases, now covered by their block). They have not failed, and a release closure is not the place to touch nine more passing specs. If one fails it needs no diagnosis — the table above already names it.
  - **`cards-filters.spec.ts` — the history-race half, and an honest limit on the verification.** Two tests failed on CI (`:182` "filters survive refresh and Back/Forward" and `:328` "keeps filters after close"), both asserting URL state after a HISTORY transition, and `:328` is the `cards-filters.spec.ts:320` this entry named as historically flaky before line numbers shifted. The observed URL was the bare `/design/cards-filters` with no query at all — it had gone back one entry too far, past the filter. Both acted on history without first asserting the state they were navigating away from: `addFilter` pushes asynchronously, and the Drawer's close is `navigate(-1)`, so pressing Escape (or Back) while the previous push is still in flight pops the wrong entry. **The same mechanism, and the same correction, as the Meetings double-pop this entry already fixed.** Preconditions are now asserted before each history action.
  - **Stated plainly: that fix is reasoned, not verified.** `cards-filters.spec.ts` passes **12/12 locally, three runs in a row**, and did so before the change too — the race does not open on this machine. The correction is a strict improvement (waiting for state that must exist before acting on it) and matches a documented prior fix for the identical mechanism, but only CI can confirm it.
- **What this means for the entry's status.** It is **narrowed again, and its closing condition is unchanged**: *"the complete gate reports reliably **and** current `main`-equivalent CI is green"*. The second half still cannot be asserted from a PR branch — it closes when `main` itself is green after the release closure merges. That is deliberate: this entry must not be closed by the change that hopes to fix it.
- **Narrowed, not closed (2026-07-27).** Every specific problem this entry named is fixed and the gate now reports honestly, but the entry's own closing condition is *"the complete gate reports reliably **and** current `main`-equivalent CI is green"* — and the second half cannot be asserted from a PR branch. It closes when `main` itself is green after this merges.
- **What was actually wrong with the gate.** Two faults, not one. The Playwright matrix had **no budget headroom** (run 30310393566: slowest shard 14.0 min of tests, 14m34s of job, 26 seconds inside `timeout-minutes: 15`), *and* both artefact-upload steps were conditioned on `failure()` — which is **false for a cancelled job**. So the single case the artefacts existed for was precisely the case that skipped them. Fixed by: a **5-way** matrix; a Playwright `globalTimeout` of 15 min set *below* the job's new 20-minute backstop, so an overrun is a Playwright failure **with** a report and traces rather than a GitHub cancellation; uploads keyed on `always() && steps.e2e.outcome != 'success'`; `strategy.job-total` deriving the `--shard=N/TOTAL` denominator from the matrix list; and a gate that prints a `job → result` table. Recorded with the measurements in [`SETUP_AND_CI.md`](../development/SETUP_AND_CI.md).
- **Measured after the change** (run 30314062657, five shards, all green): tests 8m40s / **11m09s** / 5m24s / 5m03s / 7m53s. Reproduced on run 30317758251 with the whole gate green — jobs 9m32s / **10m34s** / 5m46s / 5m22s / 10m16s — so the numbers are a property of the split, not one lucky runner. The worst shard uses 74% of the Playwright ceiling and 60% of the job budget, against 97% of the old budget. Recorded honestly: the *spread* widened (5.0–11.2 min at five shards vs 11.7–14.0 at three) because Playwright shards by test count and finer splits expose more per-test variance. `playwright-report/results.json` now carries per-test durations so the split can be revisited from data.
- **The three test failures, and what each really was.**
  1. `meetings-follow-up.spec.ts` — **not** the "genuine product or fixture defect" around the Agenda/Settings tabs this entry predicted. The failing page snapshot shows the browser on `/new/meeting`: it had navigated *off* the record. A DS-03 Drawer close is `navigate(-1)`, which does not land synchronously, and the spec's helper pressed Escape again after a fixed 120ms — popping a second time, past the record. Fixed in the shared provider (close idempotent per history entry, released when the browser leaves that entry) and the helper. A genuine user-facing defect, since a repeated Escape carried the user off the page.
  2. `reviews.spec.ts:89` — [DEBT-38](#-debt-38--notification-toasts-occlude-bottom-anchored-record-actions--p1), now closed.
  3. `assets.spec.ts:85` — the strict-mode locator, correctly called as a test defect and scoped.
- **Evidence for the flaky ones.** Because a green run was explicitly not accepted as evidence for a timing-dependent failure, the previously-flaky specs were re-run with `--repeat-each=3`: `reviews`, `cards-filters`, `assets` and `meetings-follow-up` passed **87/87**, covering `reviews.spec.ts:89`, `cards-filters.spec.ts:320` and `assets.spec.ts:62`. `cards-filters.spec.ts:320` and `assets.spec.ts:62` each failed only once historically and no independent cause was found for either beyond the two shared defects fixed here; if either recurs, it needs a fresh diagnosis rather than being attributed to this entry.
- **A fifth defect, found *by* the repaired gate.** `projects-mobile.spec.ts:166` failed once on run 30314899655 — a test the audit never saw, on a spec nobody had touched. It did not reproduce locally (6/6 green). It was diagnosed from the **uploaded trace**, which the old `if: failure()` condition would have discarded: the "Open" task-filter link was clicked at t=559904ms and "Add task" at t=559932ms — **28ms later, with no wait between them**. The second click superseded the in-flight filter navigation, so "Add task" carried `?tasks=completed`, and the *open* task created next was correctly absent from a completed-only list. Fixed by waiting on the filter having actually applied (URL settled **and** the completed task gone) — a stronger assertion than before, not a weaker one. This is the gate working as intended: a real race, previously invisible, now diagnosable from artefacts alone.
- **What remains.** (a) `main` has not yet run this configuration — the closing condition. (b) Shard 2's 11m09s is the number to watch; it is the one shard with less than a third of the Playwright ceiling to spare. (c) The standing discipline this entry asked for still applies: treat a red trunk as stop-the-line, no item marked ☑ while `main`'s CI Gate is failing, and roadmap status entries say which commit their verification refers to.
- **New evidence (2026-07-28, from the PEOPLE-03 change).** A full local `pnpm exec playwright test` run on the PEOPLE-03 branch finished **728 passed / 11 failed**. Attributing them took three steps and is worth recording, because two of the three ways of reading the result were wrong:
  1. **One was a real defect the suite was right to catch** — `people-timeline.spec.ts:264` (axe). The shared DS-05 viewport carried `aria-label` on a div with **no role** whenever it was empty, loading or errored (`aria-prohibited-attr`, serious). It passed in isolation and failed in the full run purely because other specs' fixture cleanup decided whether the timeline had articles. Fixed in [`ActivityStream.tsx`](../../app/shared/activity-feed/ActivityStream.tsx) (always a labelled region: `feed` with articles, `group` otherwise) with a four-state regression test. **A non-deterministic spec was hiding a genuine WCAG violation** — the failure mode this entry exists to prevent.
  2. **One was genuine cross-spec interference** — `tasks.spec.ts:54` failed in the full run and passes in isolation. The suite shares one local D1, so fixture cleanup in one spec can change another's outcome.
  3. **Nine fail deterministically, in isolation, and are PRE-EXISTING.** Re-running the same five spec files at the branch point (`4b3e022`) reproduced **exactly the same nine**, by file and line: `forms.spec.ts:78`, `notes.spec.ts:352`, `task-drawer.spec.ts:55` and `:185`, `touch-targets.spec.ts:128`, `waiting.spec.ts:46`, `:65`, `:98`, `:124`. They are not caused by PEOPLE-03.
- **Method note, because it cost a wasted run.** The first baseline attempt used a `git worktree` with `node_modules` symlinked from the main checkout: Vite's `fs.allow` list rejects paths outside the project root, so the client bundle never loaded and **all 46 tests failed for that reason alone** — a result that looks like catastrophic breakage and means nothing. Compare against a base commit **in the main checkout** (detached HEAD), not a worktree, unless dependencies are installed there properly.
- **What this adds to "what remains".** The nine deterministic failures above are a concrete, reproducible work list for closing this entry — they need diagnosing on their own merits, not another green-run claim.
- **Related roadmap item.** [FND-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-fnd-01--repository--toolchain-scaffold) (the CI gate) and the [IMPLEMENTATION_WORKFLOW](IMPLEMENTATION_WORKFLOW.md) Definition of Done.

### ☑ DEBT-42 — Repository-backed searches can fail the whole query on a long user input — P2
- **Current issue.** D1 caps a `LIKE` pattern at **50 bytes**; a longer pattern fails the ENTIRE statement with `LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex: SQLITE_ERROR`, not just that predicate. Every repository-backed search builds its pattern as `%<user input>%`, so **any query over ~48 characters — a pasted title, a copied sentence — returns a 500 rather than results.** Found empirically while building the Notes search (a full note title as the query, reproduced end to end), not by inspection. [`d1-note-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-note-repository.ts) now bounds the escaped needle itself, so a long Notes query degrades to matching its opening characters. **The same exposure remains** in `d1-person-repository.ts`, `d1-asset-repository.ts`, `d1-meeting-repository.ts`, `d1-review-repository.ts` and `d1-task-repository.ts`, each of which builds `%…%` from unbounded user input.
- **Impact.** A user pasting a name or a title into People, Assets, Meetings, Reviews or Tasks search gets a broken surface, not zero results — and the failure mode is a server error, which is the least honest possible answer to "I searched for something long".
- **Desired future state.** ONE shared helper for building a bounded, escaped LIKE pattern (the Notes implementation, promoted), used by every repository-backed search, with the truncation documented at the search surfaces. Truncation must never leave a trailing unpaired escape character, which SQLite rejects in its own right.
- **Related roadmap item.** [X-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-x-01--global-search-maturity); [DS-08](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ds-08--shared-search).
- **Resolved (2026-07-29).** X-01 promoted the bounded LIKE helper to
  [`app/platform/storage/d1/like-pattern.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/like-pattern.ts)
  and reused it across repository-backed Search projections, including the new
  Areas/Goals/Projects/Diary projections and the widened Tasks projection. The
  helper escapes `%`, `_` and `\`, bounds by UTF-8 bytes under D1's 50-byte pattern
  limit and trims trailing escape characters. `test/kernel/search-projections.test.ts`
  proves a 500-character Diary search degrades calmly instead of throwing.

### ☑ DEBT-43 — `project-activity.spec.ts` asserts a stale Project tab set — P3
- **Current issue.** Two tests in [`e2e/project-activity.spec.ts`](../../e2e/project-activity.spec.ts) fail on `main`: *"opens Activity from the project record and shows the real timeline"* asserts the tab strip is exactly `Tasks / Linked / Activity / Settings`, and *"is keyboard operable: reach and open the Activity tab"* presses ArrowRight a fixed number of times to land on Activity. [PROJ-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-proj-03--knowledge) added a **Knowledge** tab to the Project record, so the strip is now five tabs and both assertions are one short.
- **Why it is recorded here.** Found while verifying [MEET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-meet-03--people--history-integration), and **reproduced against clean `origin/main` (`b342142`) before being attributed** — it is not a MEET-03 regression and was not dismissed as flaky. It is a stale test, not a product defect: the Project record is correct and the Knowledge tab is intended.
- **Impact.** Two red E2E tests on trunk, which is exactly the "is the gate trustworthy?" problem [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02) exists to prevent.
- **Resolved 2026-07-28.** Fixed while returning trunk to green rather than deferred again: a red trunk is the one condition under which the next person cannot tell their own breakage from someone else's, and this entry was the only thing left holding it red. The strip now asserts all five tabs, and — per the desired state below — the keyboard test arrows to Activity **by name**, bounded by the live tab count, so adding a sixth tab cannot silently break it a second time. Test-only; no product code touched.
- **Desired future state.** The spec asserts the current five-tab strip, and the keyboard test navigates by tab **name** rather than a fixed number of key presses, so a future tab addition cannot silently break it again.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PROJ-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-proj-03--knowledge); [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02).

### ☐ DEBT-44 — A held meeting appears on the timeline of a linked NON-attendee — P2
- **Current issue.** [MEET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-meet-03--people--history-integration)'s `meeting.held` names the Meeting as an Activity subject (so the Meeting's own Timeline carries it, and the Person timeline can link back to the canonical record). But a Person who is linked to that Meeting — by a `link.related`, or as an attendee added AFTER it was marked held — has the Meeting in their PEOPLE-02 anchor set, and `listForEntities` returns every event naming an anchor. So they see the line `<the person who marked it held> · Meeting held — <meeting>` on their relationship history even though their id is **absent** from the recorded attendee subjects.
- **Verified, not assumed (2026-07-28).** Raised by automated review on [#81](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/81) and reproduced with a throwaway integration test against the real loader before being accepted: both a late-linked attendee and a `link.related` bystander receive the event, and the item's `subjects` array contains only the Meeting (`isAnchor: false`) — no Person.
- **Impact, stated honestly.** It is the PEOPLE-02 anchor contract working exactly as designed: a linked record's own events appear on your timeline, and `meeting.created` / `meeting.updated` already behave this way. The line is a true statement about the Meeting and disappears if you unlink. **But** "Meeting held" reads as *"we met"* in a way that "Meeting created" does not, so on a relationship history it is true-but-misleading for someone who was never an attendee — and it sits awkwardly beside MEET-03's own documented rule that a late-added attendee is "not retroactively added".
- **What is NOT affected.** The durable, Person-scoped fact is subject membership, and the [PEOPLE-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-people-03--stay-in-touch-signals) contact seam reads `activity.listForEntity(personId)`, which returns only events naming that Person. So a non-attendee can never become a "last meaningful contact" through this. The defect is presentational, not a corruption of the contact signal.
- **Why it was not fixed in MEET-03.** The suggested remedy — filter `meeting.held` on the People read path — would put Meetings knowledge inside the People module, which MEET-03 explicitly forbids and which an architectural test asserts against. The architecturally clean fix is a **generic** People-timeline rule ("an event that names Person subjects appears on a Person's timeline only if that Person is one of them"), which carries no Meetings knowledge but **changes PEOPLE-02 semantics for every module's events** — e.g. a third party's `entity_link.created` would stop appearing. That is its own roadmap item, not a late addition to a shipped one (AGENTS.md §13, one item per PR).
- **Desired future state.** Decide the rule deliberately as part of [PEOPLE-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-people-03--stay-in-touch-signals) or a dedicated PEOPLE item: either adopt the generic subject-membership rule (with tests and a documented PEOPLE-02 semantic change), or accept the anchor contract and make the distinction legible in the UI.
- **Related roadmap item.** [MEET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-meet-03--people--history-integration); [PEOPLE-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-people-02--relationship-timeline); [PEOPLE-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-people-03--stay-in-touch-signals).

### ☑ AUDIT-IDENTITY-01 — Production actor names and unrecognised activity events — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-04**
- **Was.** Two related identity gaps in the shared FND-05 Activity stream as it appeared in production: **actor names** were not resolved to a real person, so every event the owner performed read `Someone · …`, and **unrecognised activity event types** fell through to the generic descriptor and were badged `Unrecognised event` rather than being caught as a registration gap.
- **Root cause, established against the real code (not assumed).** FND-09 already recorded a truthful actor on every event (`actor_type='user'`, `actor_id` = the stable Access `sub`). What the schema had no row for was the IDENTITY behind that subject — there was no users table, no workspace membership and no link to the owner's Person record — so the presentation layer had nothing to resolve and used a hard-coded placeholder. The unrecognised-event half was separate: each surface carried its own partial descriptor map, so the workspace feed did not know about types other modules had fully registered.
- **Resolved by IDENT-01** ([ADR-071](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-071-actor-identity--workspace-membership-read-time-name-resolution-and-one-canonical-rule)): a `workspace_members` identity record keyed by the SAME subject the events already carry (migration 0028, additive, no history rewritten); one canonical resolution rule in `app/kernel/identity` used by every surface; membership provisioned once per authenticated request; cross-module descriptions built from the FND-06 module registry plus one shared curated set, with a test that fails if any registered or kernel-persistable type lacks a renderer. `Someone` no longer exists in the product, and a regression test fails the build if it returns.
- **Historical records.** Repaired additively by `scripts/repair-activity-identity.mjs` (dry-run by default) — see [`IDENTITY_AND_ACTORS.md`](../development/IDENTITY_AND_ACTORS.md). Anything it cannot attribute from real evidence stays `Unknown user` rather than being given a misleading name.
- **Related roadmap item.** [FND-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-fnd-05--shared-activity-model); [FND-09](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-fnd-09--app-shell--authentication); [ACTIVITY_TIMELINE.md](../development/ACTIVITY_TIMELINE.md).

### ☑ DEBT-45 — A captured record is not linked to the context it was captured from — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-08**

- **Resolved by [PEOPLE-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-people-04--mobile-people) / [DIARY-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-diary-02--day-context-links).** Every closing condition below is now met, and each is met by evidence rather than by inspection:
  - **Every promised contextual capture creates the canonical relationship.** Person → Task is `task.relates_to` (related work, never delegation, no invented structural parent); Person → Note and Person → Diary are `link.related`; Person → Meeting is the canonical `meeting.attendee`, so Meetings keep their own attendee authority instead of it being forked into a generic link. Proven per type against real Workers/D1 in `test/kernel/capture-context-matrix.test.ts`.
  - **Full-form hand-off preserves context** — the specific gap this entry stayed open for. Context travels as a `?ctx=` search parameter (`CAPTURE_CONTEXT_PARAM`) to `/tasks?drawer=new-task`, `/notes?drawer=new-note`, `/new/meeting` and `/diary?inspector=new`; each renders the SAME shared context chip and submits to the SAME canonical create route. It survives a refresh and is CONSUMED once used, so a later capture on the same page does not silently inherit a finished context.
  - **Context is server revalidated.** The URL value is never authoritative: it is re-parsed through the shared contract parser and the create route re-resolves the anchor's id and type in the authenticated workspace. A tampered parameter, a claimed type that does not match the stored record, a source deleted between opening and submitting, and a cross-workspace anchor each produce NO relationship — and the cross-workspace case discloses nothing about whether the foreign record exists.
  - **Duplicate submissions do not duplicate relationships.** EntityLink creation is idempotent; a retry, or a Person named by both the attendee picker and the context, yields exactly one relationship. Asserted, not assumed.
  - **Failure behaviour is honest.** ADR-060's compensation is preserved unchanged: a relationship failure rolls the created record back, and a failed rollback returns the created id and says the record exists but is not linked.
  - **Mobile and desktop use one contract.** The same `CaptureContextContract`, the same chip component, the same hand-off parameter and the same create routes. Journey 4 of `e2e/people-diary-context.spec.ts` drives the desktop flow specifically to prove there is no mobile-specific divergence.
  - **The route/integration/E2E/a11y proof exists.** `test/kernel/capture-context-matrix.test.ts`, `test/unit/capture/full-form-handoff.test.ts`, `test/unit/capture/handoff-forms.test.tsx`, and `e2e/people-diary-context.spec.ts` (four journeys plus axe at 320px and 390px and a touch-target assertion on the chip's Remove control).
- **The one thing NOT claimed.** Bundle-size impact still has no measurement. The query behaviour was inspected and is bounded — the new day-context read is two bounded, indexed statements plus one bounded linked-items read, with no N+1 and no "load the day of every entity type" — but this entry does not claim a benchmark it did not run. That is a measurement gap, not a behavioural one, and it is recorded here rather than rounded up.
- **Status (was): partially addressed by ADR-060 (2026-07-29), not closed.** The shared capture-context contract, relationship matrix, context chip and canonical-route reconciliation now exist. People quick actions, Project record overflow actions, Meeting record overflow actions and the Task Drawer linked surface pass context into the shared Quick Capture sheet. The four canonical Quick Capture routes (`/tasks/new`, `/notes/new`, `/meetings/create`, `/diary/new`) revalidate the source context server-side, create the canonical record, then create the canonical EntityLink / attendee link / structural parent relationship with compensation on relationship failure.
- **Current issue (was).** The original no-link gap was closed for the implemented Quick Capture entry points, but the debt was not fully resolved: full creation routes did not preserve context when launched outside the sheet; focused route/integration tests and the requested Playwright/mobile/axe matrix had not been added; and performance impact had not been measured beyond type/unit execution. All but the bundle-size measurement are now addressed, as recorded above. *(Area/Goal/Note/Diary record headers still do not each expose every contextual action. That is deliberately NOT treated as a closing condition: this entry is about a captured record being LINKED to its context, not about how many surfaces offer a capture entry point. The Person, Project, Meeting and Task surfaces this entry names all do, and adding an entry point elsewhere is now a one-line change against a proven contract.)*
- **Why it was not fixed in MOBILE-01.** A correct fix is a *link-intent contract*: capture would need to carry an optional anchor (entity id + type + link type) through the sheet to each panel, and each panel would need to create the EntityLink through the Universal Relationship System after its module's create succeeds — including deciding what happens when the create succeeds and the link does not. That is a real shared-contract design touching four modules and the relationship system, not a mobile layout adjustment (AGENTS.md §13, one item per PR). Approximating it — for example creating the link only for Tasks — would have made the behaviour inconsistent across capture types, which is worse than not having it.
- **Desired future state.** Every promised contextual capture type creates canonical relationships; context is server-validated; retries do not create duplicates; partial failures are reported honestly; mobile and desktop use the same contract; Project Knowledge and Person relationships update correctly; full-form handoff preserves context; and the full route/unit/Playwright/mobile/accessibility proof exists.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PEOPLE-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-people-04--mobile-people) (☑ — it was ◐ for exactly this reason); [DIARY-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-diary-02--day-context-links) ☑; [REL-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-rel-01--universal-relationship-system-shared-linked-items).

### ☐ DEBT-46 — Diary's timeline node is still not the shared Card — P3 (deliberately downgraded)
- **Status: outstanding, downgraded and re-scoped by [MOBILE-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-mobile-01--fast-mobile-first-daily-experience) (2026-07-28).** This is the Diary half of [DEBT-01](#-debt-01--duplicate-card-implementations-per-module--p2), split out because the two halves no longer have the same answer.
- **Current issue.** The Diary day timeline renders a hand-rolled `dh-diary-entry` node rather than the shared DS-04 Card, so it does not inherit the Card's swipe accelerator, selection model or compact phone preset.
- **What changed.** [DIARY-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-diary-03--mobile) named this as a fork to resolve "here or in PX-06". MOBILE-01 deliberately did **not** resolve it. A day timeline node is a genuinely different presentation from a Card — it is positioned against a time rail and reads as a chronological event, not as a record in a list — and forcing it into a Card to close a debt entry would produce worse product on the very surface the mobile work exists to serve. The other half of DEBT-01 (Meetings) was already resolved by UX-01 adopting shared Cards.
- **Desired future state.** Either a Card *variant* that can render against a time rail without the Card growing timeline knowledge, or an explicit, documented acceptance that a timeline node is its own shared primitive. Decide it deliberately; do not close it by conversion.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DEBT-01](#-debt-01--duplicate-card-implementations-per-module--p2); [DIARY-01B](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-diary-01b--diary-responsive-day-timeline-workspace).

### ☑ DEBT-47 — An open autosave editor does not adopt a server-side change to its field — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-20**
- **Status: contract SHIPPED 2026-08-01, adoption partial.** Originally surfaced by [MOBILE-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-mobile-01--fast-mobile-first-daily-experience) (2026-07-28). Pre-existing behaviour of the shared autosave field; MOBILE-01 is the first workflow that makes it visible, so it is recorded here rather than left to be discovered.
- **Current issue.** `useAutosaveField` seeds its draft from `initialValue` once and then owns it, so a change written to the same field by anything else — another tab, another device, or the Meeting capture bar's **Note** type — does not appear in an editor that is already mounted. The write itself is correct and canonical: the note IS appended to `notesMarkdown` through the same `intent=update` the editor autosaves through, and it is there on the next load. But during the meeting the user captures a note, hears "Note captured", and sees the notes editor unchanged.
- **Why it was not fixed here.** The safe-looking fix — adopt `initialValue` whenever it changes — would overwrite a draft out from under someone who is typing, which is exactly the data loss the current design refuses. A correct fix is a *reconciliation contract* on the shared hook: adopt an external value only when the field is genuinely clean (no pending debounce, no unsaved edit, no in-flight or failed save), and decide deliberately what happens when it is not (keep the draft and say so, or offer the newer version). That is a change to a shared DS-06 contract used by every autosave surface in the product, not a mobile adjustment — one item per PR (AGENTS.md §13).
- **Effect on the tests.** `e2e/mobile-capture-journeys.spec.ts` asserts the captured note after a reload, which is what is genuinely true today, rather than asserting a live update the product does not make. The comment there points back to this entry.
