### ☑ DEBT-63 — "Load more" could silently drop a page — P1 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-01**

- **Filed as a test-timing curiosity. It was a product defect.** The first draft of this entry recorded two keyset-pagination component tests failing "only under heavy machine load", noted CI was green, and deliberately declined to widen their waits without a reproduction. Then the same family failed on CI on a dedicated runner (`NotesCollection.test.tsx`, run 30715617556), which falsified the "only under load" reading and forced a real diagnosis.
- **The diagnosis, and the experiment that settled it.** Reproduced locally at ~1 run in 12 by running the Notes and Projects collection suites together. The decisive step was widening the wait to **8 seconds**: it still failed. That rules out slowness — the page was never delivered at all. Tracing the shared hook then showed the mechanism exactly, and there were **two** independent drop paths:
  1. **The mount-time scope reset.** `useEffect` runs after paint, so a `findBy*` (which resolves during render) followed by a click could land *before* React flushed the mount effect. The reset then ran for the first time with a request already stamped, cleared `requested.current`, and the page that arrived was discarded as belonging to a previous scope.
  2. **The premature failure branch.** `fetcher.load()` does not synchronously leave the idle state, so `state === "idle" && data === undefined` has two meanings — "settled with nothing" and "has not started yet". The branch treated them alike, so it fired in the window between stamping the request and the fetcher going `loading`, set the retry state and cleared the request; the real page was then dropped.
- **What the owner saw.** Click "Load more"; the spinner clears; nothing is added; the cursor does not advance. A second click works. **All eight collections share this hook** (Areas, Goals, Notes, Projects, People, Assets, Meetings, Reviews), so it was product-wide.
- **The fix.** Reset only on a genuine scope CHANGE (compared by value, seeded with the mount scope) rather than whenever the effect happens to run; and gate the failure branch on a `started` flag that is only set once the fetcher has actually left idle. Neither weakens the DEBT-45 rule this hook exists to encode — a scope that genuinely changes still discards an in-flight request, which is exactly what must happen.
- **Measured before and after,** running the Notes + Projects collection suites together: **1 failure in 12** before; **5 in 30** with only the first fix (which is why both were needed, and how the second was found); **0 in 40** with both. The full unit suite then passed **3/3 consecutive runs (3128 tests)**.
- **Regression coverage, stated honestly.** The two collection suites that reproduced it are the guard: they exercise the path and went from intermittently red to 40/40 green. An attempt to add a dedicated repeated-journey test to `useKeysetPagination.test.tsx` was **removed rather than shipped** — it passed 5/5 against the *pre-fix* hook, so it guarded nothing, and a check that cannot fail is worse than no check because it reads as protection. The race only opens in the larger collection component trees, not in the isolated harness.
- **The lesson worth keeping.** `test/unit/load-more/useKeysetPagination.test.tsx` already carried a widened 5-second wait with a comment blaming "a fully-parallel suite run". That widening was masking this defect. **An intermittent test that is fixed by waiting longer has not been diagnosed** — the 8-second experiment is what turned a suspected flake into a found bug, and it cost one command.
- **Related roadmap item.** [UX-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ux-01--daily-driver-ux-ui-and-product-polish) (which introduced the shared hook); [DEBT-45 (pagination)](#-debt-45--keyset-paginators-can-consume-a-revalidated-fetcher-page-after-a-scope-reset--p3--resolved-2026-08-01), whose rule this preserves.

---

### ☑ DEBT-64 — A test asserted an eventual focus outcome synchronously — P3 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-01**

- **Current issue.** `test/unit/tasks/TasksQuickAdd.test.tsx` awaited the quick-add field being CLEARED after a save, then asserted `document.activeElement` **synchronously** on the next line. Those are two different eventual outcomes: the field is cleared by a state update, but it is refocused by an **effect** gated on the save no longer being in flight, so focus returns one effect later. The test therefore raced a behaviour the component always eventually gets right, and failed on CI (run 30716784330) while the product was correct.
- **Impact.** Test-only. The refocus itself works, and its purpose — "the next task is one keystroke away" — is intact; only the assertion's timing was wrong. But it is a required check, so it is a red gate for a green product, which is the failure mode [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02) exists to name.
- **Resolution.** Await both outcomes. Verified 15/15 on the file and **4/4 consecutive full unit-suite runs (3128 tests)**.
- **Swept, and deliberately narrow.** The whole unit suite was scanned for the same shape — a synchronous `document.activeElement` assertion following an `await`. Three other candidates were examined and all three are **correct**: `InspectorProvider.test.tsx` asserts *inside* its `waitFor`, and the two `SettingsLayout.test.tsx` cases are negative assertions that follow an already-awaited positive one, where "focus is not somewhere else" is immediate once "focus is here" has been established. Nothing else was changed.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-04--daily-driver-tasks-inbox-inline-editing-and-basic-recurrence) (the quick-add refocus behaviour); [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02).

### ☐ DEBT-61 — An export is not a point-in-time snapshot, and a busy workspace can produce a slightly incoherent one — P3

- **Current issue.** [X-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-x-04--export--data-portability) reads the workspace through a SEQUENCE of bounded statements. Each statement sees a consistent database, but the sequence does not: a write committed while the export is running is visible to the collections read after it and absent from those read before. A task created mid-export can therefore land in `entities` with no `taskDetails` row, or a link can name an entity that had not yet been read — which the snapshot validator would reject, failing the export rather than shipping an inconsistent file.
- **Why it was accepted.** D1 exposes no cross-statement snapshot for this read pattern through the Workers binding, so the alternatives were to claim a guarantee that does not exist, or to state the real one. X-04 states it — in `meta.consistency`, `manifest.json`, the archive `README.md` and the vault's `Export Information.md` — and mitigates it by reading collections **sequentially rather than concurrently**, which narrows the window. For a single-owner product where an export is a deliberate, occasional action, the practical exposure is close to zero: it requires a write to land in the second or so between two specific collection reads.
- **Desired future state.** If D1's Sessions API (or an equivalent read-snapshot mechanism) becomes usable from the Workers binding for a multi-statement read, bind the whole export to one snapshot and change `meta.consistency` to say so. Until then the honest statement is the correct behaviour, not a workaround.
- **Evidence.** [`build-snapshot.ts`](../../app/platform/export/build-snapshot.ts) (the sequential read and the reasoning), `SNAPSHOT_CONSISTENCY` in [`workspace-snapshot.ts`](../../app/kernel/export/workspace-snapshot.ts), and the validator's referential-integrity checks in [`snapshot-validation.ts`](../../app/kernel/export/snapshot-validation.ts).
- **Related roadmap item.** [SET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-set-02--backup--restore) — a scheduled backup runs unattended and hits this window more often than a hand-pressed export does, so it is the item that should decide whether the mitigation is still enough.

### ☐ DEBT-62 — The whole export is assembled in memory, so it is bounded rather than unbounded — P3

- **Current issue.** X-04 builds the snapshot in memory and then builds the archive from it, so peak memory is roughly the workspace's text plus the compressed archive. Both are bounded — 50,000 rows per collection (`SNAPSHOT_COLLECTION_MAX_ROWS`) and 64 MiB of archive content (`ZIP_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES`) — and exceeding either is **reported**, in `limitations` and the manifest, rather than silently truncated. But a workspace that outgrows those bounds cannot export in one file at all.
- **Why it was accepted.** A streamed archive still needs every entry's CRC and size before the central directory can be written, and the vault's link rewriting needs every record's filename before any file can be written — so streaming would not remove the need to hold the data. A realistic personal workspace is orders of magnitude below both ceilings, and the honest ceiling with a clear error beats an isolate the runtime kills.
- **Desired future state.** If a workspace ever approaches a ceiling, split the export into parts (per module, or per date range) with a manifest that names the set — which is also the shape a large restore would want. Do not raise the ceilings without measuring the Worker's real memory headroom first.
- **Evidence.** `SNAPSHOT_COLLECTION_MAX_ROWS` in [`snapshot-repository.ts`](../../app/kernel/export/snapshot-repository.ts), `ZIP_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` and `ZipTooLargeError` in [`zip.ts`](../../app/platform/export/zip.ts), and the 507 response in [`export.tsx`](../../app/modules/settings/routes/export.tsx).
- **Related roadmap item.** [SET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-set-02--backup--restore).

### ☐ DEBT-65 — `MeetingRepository.list` performs an items read per returned row — P3

- **Current issue.** [`d1-meeting-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-meeting-repository.ts)'s `list()` maps each page row through `#items(id)` — one extra prepared statement per meeting on the page — even for consumers that never render the items (the collection cards, the Review period context). Found during V2.0.1 while fixing the Meetings search provider; the new `searchMeetings` projection deliberately avoids it, but `list()` keeps the N+1 shape.
- **Impact.** A 30-row collection page issues ~31 statements instead of ~2. On D1 this is latency, not correctness, and the page is bounded at 50 — so it is friction, not a defect.
- **Desired future state.** Batch the item read (one `IN (…)` query over the page's ids, grouped in memory), or let callers opt out of items entirely, mirroring how the People module's relationship facts read avoided its N+1 (proven by a query-count test).
- **Closing condition.** A query-count assertion on `list()` (the `countedDb` harness in `test/kernel/search-projections.test.ts` already exists for exactly this) showing a constant number of statements per page.
- **Related roadmap item.** [MEET-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-meet-04--mobile) (the next Meetings slice).

### ☑ DEBT-66 — A custom recurrence rule is displayed truthfully but cannot be authored or edited outside quick capture — P3 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-08 (TASKS-07)**

- **Current issue.** The recurrence model accepts any interval 1–99 over day/weekday/week/month/year plus weekday-pinned weekly rules, and quick capture can create them ("every 3 weeks", "every Monday"). Since V2.0.1 the quick-edit panel ([`TaskQuickEditPanel.tsx`](../../app/shared/task-record/TaskQuickEditPanel.tsx)) presents such a rule as its own labelled option (via the shared `taskRecurrenceLabel`) and re-committing it is a guaranteed no-op — the rule can no longer be misrepresented or silently flattened. But there is still no surface for AUTHORING a custom interval or weekday set: the panel's predefined list is the only editor, and the full Task record shows recurrence read-only.
- **Impact.** An owner who wants "every 3 weeks" must phrase it at capture time; changing an existing rule to a custom interval means re-capturing.
- **Desired future state.** A small custom-repeat editor (interval + unit + optional weekdays) on the Task record or quick-edit panel, posting to the existing `set_recurrence` route — which already validates the full model.
- **Closing condition.** A custom interval can be set and changed from the Task record, with unit coverage mirroring `TaskQuickEditPanel.test.tsx`'s preservation cases.
- **Resolution (TASKS-07 / [ADR-085](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-085-the-tasks-daily-driver--the-matrix-removed-editing-moved-onto-the-row-bulk-made-structural-and-recurrence-given-a-second-scheduling-mode)).** The seven-option `Repeat` select was replaced by the shared [`TaskRecurrenceEditor`](../../app/shared/task-record/TaskRecurrenceEditor.tsx): presets for the ordinary choices, and **Custom…** for any interval 1–99 over days/weeks/months/years, a weekday set for a weekly schedule, the anchor date when the Task has both, and the new fixed / after-completion scheduling mode. The composed result is stated in plain language BEFORE it is saved, through the same `taskRecurrenceLabel` every read-only surface uses. The translation is pure and unit-tested (`recurrence-authoring.test.ts`), and `presetOf` is the strict inverse of `ruleForPreset`, so a custom rule is reported as Custom and never coerced to the nearest preset. Because the editor lives in `TaskQuickEditPanel`, the `/tasks` row, Review Inbox and the guided Review all gained it at once.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-04--daily-driver-tasks-inbox-inline-editing-and-basic-recurrence) · [TASKS-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md#-tasks-07--recurrence-20--delivered-2026-08-08) ☑.

### ☐ DEBT-67 — Seven curated themes is more choice than one owner needs — P3

- **Current issue.** THEME-02 added the Modern pair, taking the registry to **seven** curated themes plus the `system` appearance mode ([`theme-preference.ts`](../../app/kernel/preferences/theme-preference.ts), [`tokens.css`](../../app/styles/tokens.css)). Three of them — Eucalypt, Coastal and Ember — are *character* themes: single light palettes that differ from Daly Light mainly in hue. There is now visible overlap, most obviously between **Daly Light**, **Modern Light**, **Coastal** and **Eucalypt**, all of which are light themes with a cool-to-neutral surround and a restrained blue-green or green accent.
- **Impact.** Cost is carried in three places, none of them the owner's data: a settings list long enough that the choice takes thought rather than a glance; a maintenance tax where **every new colour token must be authored eight times** (seven themes plus the duplicated `prefers-color-scheme` dark block) and pass the full contrast harness in each; and CI time in the whole-registry sweeps (`assets-ownership.spec.ts` now walks seven themes, and the THEME-01 surface matrix is seven × six). It is friction and drag, not a defect — every theme is complete, distinct and AA-clean today.
- **Desired future state.** A registry of roughly **four**: one default pair and one alternative pair, or a default plus two genuinely distinct characters. The obvious candidates for retirement are the character themes whose distance from a shipped default is smallest, but **that is a product judgement, not a mechanical one, and it is deliberately not being made here.** Removing a theme takes a choice away from an owner who may already be on it, and this change existed to add a theme, not to delete four.
- **How a removal would have to be done, when it is decided.** The registry entry, the `tokens.css` block and the TS colour map come out together; the `theme` CHECK is narrowed by a further rebuild migration; and `parseThemePreference` already degrades a retired value to the default rather than leaving the document unstyled — there is an E2E test for exactly that case, so a removal has a proven safe landing. It should also be announced in the release notes rather than discovered by an owner whose theme silently changed.
- **What the DS-14 foundation learned, and it is evidence for this entry rather than against it (2026-08-03).** The foundation authored **21 new colour tokens** — `surface-page`, the neutral absence pill pair, `divider-subtle`, and six Area accents each with a tint pair — **eight times**, and recomposed the light neutral ramp of **five** themes to satisfy the elevation contract. The tax this entry predicts is real and was paid in full. It was also **survivable**, and specifically because it was mechanical: the values are generated from stated rules and asserted by a test that enumerates the registry, so seven themes cost seven rows of a table rather than seven independent judgements. That is the honest shape of this debt — it is drag on every token-level change, not a coherence failure, and it does not get worse per theme so much as it gets *longer*.
- **This was deliberately NOT acted on by DS-14.** A restyle that quietly deletes four themes to make its own work smaller is a product decision taken for an engineering reason. No theme was exempted from the contract and none was removed; the recommendation stands here, undecided, for whoever decides it on its merits.
- **Closing condition.** Either a decision recorded in an ADR to keep all seven (with the maintenance cost accepted explicitly), or a consolidation shipped with the removal path above and the acceptance matrix updated to the reduced set.
- **Related roadmap item.** [THEME-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-theme-02--the-modern-visual-system); [DS-14](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ds-14--whole-application-visual-overhaul) (paid the tax, did not act on the recommendation).

### ☑ DEBT-68 — Logging out does not clear the device's offline data automatically — P2 — RESOLVED 2026-08-08

- **Original issue.** DalyHub's sign-out was a Cloudflare Access sign-out: the session ended at the edge, and there was no client-side hook that ran before it did. So the PWA milestone's local-data clearing is an explicit control (`/settings?section=offline` → *Reset offline data*), not something that happens on logout. After logging out, a previously synced snapshot and any queued captures remain in IndexedDB on that device until the owner clears them, the browser evicts them, or a different identity signs in (which deletes the previous namespace's snapshot but deliberately keeps its queued captures).
- **Impact.** On a shared or borrowed device, "I logged out" does not mean "my data is off this machine". The Settings surface states this plainly and the behaviour is documented, so nothing is misrepresented — but the owner has to take a second action they may not think to take.
- **Resolved (2026-08-08, with SET-03).** Sign-out is now a path DalyHub owns end to end (`app/shared/account-security/use-sign-out.ts`). Before the browser is handed to `/cdn-cgi/access/logout` it clears the offline snapshot, recent searches, the diagnostics ring and DalyHub's cached application files — and, when nothing is queued, the offline database itself. A `security.signed_out` event records what happened.
- **The one thing it deliberately does NOT clear, and why.** Offline captures that have never reached DalyHub exist nowhere else. The closing condition below asked for "no `dalyhub-offline` database", and taking that literally would mean destroying unsynchronised work as a side effect of leaving a device — so the resolution splits local data into three classes (public assets / reproducible personal data / unsynchronised work) and clears the first two. A device with nothing pending is left with nothing at all, which is the outcome the condition was reaching for; a device with pending captures keeps exactly those, and the Account & security surface says so before anyone clicks. Destroying them is available as its own control, behind a typed confirmation.
- **No preference was added.** The condition imagined an optional "clear offline data when I sign out". Clearing reproducible personal data is now the unconditional DEFAULT, so a preference to enable it would be a setting whose only value is to turn a security improvement off.
- **Evidence.** `test/unit/account-security/use-sign-out.test.tsx` drives the hook and asserts the ORDER (clear → record → leave) and the class-3 preservation in both branches, plus that a failed history write and an unclearable store both still sign the owner out. `e2e/account-security.spec.ts` covers the surface, its confirmations and its wording.
- **Original closing condition.** Signing out through the DalyHub control leaves no `dalyhub-offline` database and no `dalyhub-*` cache on the device, with queued captures either synced first or explicitly confirmed as discarded. **Met, with the deliberate amendment above recorded rather than glossed.**
- **Related roadmap item.** [SET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-set-03--account--security--substantially-delivered-2026-08-08).

### ☐ DEBT-69 — Offline capture receipts accumulate with no prune — P3

- **Current issue.** `offline_capture_receipts` (migration `0027`) is written once per replayed offline capture and never deleted. Its purpose is to survive the retry window — minutes, not years — but nothing removes an old row. `created_at` is indexed for exactly this, and the table is bounded by how many captures the owner actually makes offline, so this is small and slow-growing rather than dangerous.
- **Impact.** Unbounded row growth in a table with no reader after its retry window. At a realistic capture rate it is measured in kilobytes a year; the debt is that "unbounded" is still unbounded.
- **Desired future state.** An age-based prune (delete receipts older than a documented horizon) attached to whatever scheduled maintenance DalyHub gains first — the V2.0.1 backup workflow is the obvious host.
- **Closing condition.** Receipts older than the horizon are removed, with a kernel test proving a receipt inside the horizon still prevents a duplicate.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PWA/offline](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md#11-known-limitations) (follow-on).

### ☐ DEBT-71 — A replayed capture whose Worker died mid-creation cannot be resolved automatically — P2

- **Current issue.** Offline capture replay claims an idempotency key in `offline_capture_receipts` *before* the module creates anything, and finishes the receipt with the created id afterwards. D1 has no interactive transaction that can span the module's own creation code, so a Worker that dies between those two writes leaves a receipt whose outcome is undecidable: the create may have committed just before the process ended, or never run at all. DalyHub retires the key rather than guessing (`capture-receipts.server.ts`), so no duplicate is ever created — but the owner is asked to check whether the capture arrived, and the queued capture is marked as needing attention rather than syncing.
- **Impact.** Rare (it needs a Worker to die inside a one-round-trip window) and never destructive: the capture's text stays on the device and nothing is duplicated. The debt is that a resolvable situation is handed to the owner.
- **Desired future state.** Make the creation itself idempotent so there is no second write to lose: derive the created record's primary key from the idempotency key, so replay is an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` against the record table and the receipt becomes an optimisation rather than the guarantee. That is a change to how the three modules mint ids, which is why it was not folded into the PWA milestone.
- **Closing condition.** A kernel test kills the request between create and receipt completion, replays the same key, and asserts exactly one record exists and the replay reports its id — with no owner-facing "check whether this arrived" message.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PWA/offline](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md#11-known-limitations) (follow-on).

### ☐ DEBT-70 — Hydrated offline rendering is not covered by automation — P2

- **Current issue.** `e2e/pwa-offline.spec.ts` runs against the development server, because it is the only Playwright server with an authenticated session (the production-mode server is deliberately fail-closed, which is its own test). A Vite dev server has no hashed bundles to precache, so an offline reload renders the cached shell DOCUMENT but cannot hydrate it. The suite therefore proves the service worker's runtime behaviour and asserts the snapshot and queue directly in IndexedDB, but not that the fully hydrated offline application renders from the precached production bundle.
- **Impact.** The single most user-visible offline behaviour — "I opened DalyHub on a plane and it worked" — rests on the manual device checklist rather than on CI.
- **Desired future state.** A third Playwright server: the real production build served with the development authenticator, so the precached production bundle can be exercised offline. It needs a build directory that is not shared with the fail-closed preview server.
- **Closing condition.** A Playwright test loads the production bundle authenticated, goes offline, reloads, and asserts the offline snapshot renders from IndexedDB with hydration complete.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PWA/offline](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md#10-testing) (follow-on).

### ☑ DEBT-72 — Card-on-tint is a phone-native pattern and its behaviour at 1440px and above is unproven — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-03**

- **Current issue.** [DS-14](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ds-14--whole-application-visual-overhaul) restyles every DalyHub surface to **card-on-tint** — a tinted page canvas with cards raised above it ([`DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md §1`](../design/DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md), [ADR-068](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-068-ds-14--the-card-on-tint-direction-its-elevation-contract-two-density-presets-derived-area-colour-and-a-single-commit-rollback)). That pattern is **native to the phone**, where it earns its keep: a narrow viewport shows one or two cards, the tint reads as a surround, and the card reads as the thing you are holding. It is not established that it survives being scaled up. At 1440px and above the same rules produce many cards on a large field of tint, and the risks are specific and nameable — the canvas becoming a large expanse of flat colour rather than a surround; a grid of cards reading as a scattered board instead of one contained workspace, which is the property [`DESIGN_SYSTEM.md → Surface & boundaries`](../design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md#surface--boundaries) exists to hold; and the ΔL\* ≥ 3 separation being comfortable on a 6-inch panel and either invisible or banded across a wide, bright display.
- **The gap is in the verification, not only in the design.** [`DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md §10`](../design/DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md) sets DS-14's verification widths at **320, 375, 390, 430, 768, 1280, 1440** — six of the seven are phone or tablet, and the widest is 1440. The repository's existing responsive regression ([`e2e/responsive.spec.ts`](../../e2e/responsive.spec.ts), DS-11) already sweeps **320 / 375 / 390 / 768 / 1024 / 1440 / 2560**, so DS-14's own matrix stops short of a width the product is already tested at. Overflow-free at 2560px is not the same claim as *coherent* at 2560px, and neither is asserted for card-on-tint today.
- **Impact.** The owner's daily driver is a desktop. If the pattern does not hold at width, it is discovered after seven PRs have restyled every module rather than after one, and the discovery is aesthetic — meaning no test fails and nothing goes red. This is the single largest unproven assumption in DS-14.
- **Resolution evidence.** The rejected staged delivery model is not an active
  product process. The wide-desktop risk is handled as implementation evidence:
  real screenshots and responsive checks at desktop widths, plus automated surface
  hierarchy assertions for card, page and raised layers. If the pattern fails in
  review, the fix belongs in the visual PR that introduced the defect, not in a
  deferred owner-process gate.
- **Status: RESOLVED 2026-08-03 — answered inside DS-14.** The answer arrived during implementation, and **it was a real defect, not a false alarm**: an uncapped collection row at 1440px puts the record's title at one end and its status pill at the other with roughly a thousand pixels between them — the "stretched mobile application" this entry's third named risk describes, reached by a route the entry did not predict (row geometry rather than tint expanse).
- **The fix, and why it is the general one.** A content measure on **collections**, not only on prose: `.dh-collection__content` is capped at `--dh-width-wide` and aligned to the inline START rather than centred, because the navigation rail is on the left and a centred column drifts away from it as the viewport grows. That is [ADR-069 decision 7](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-069-ds-14-applied--the-group-is-the-card-the-shared-boundary-is-the-region-and-what-a-whole-application-restyle-cost-in-one-pr). The other two named risks did not materialise: the group-is-the-card decision means a wide viewport shows ONE card containing many rows rather than a scattered board of many cards, which also removes the large-flat-tint concern — the tint is a surround, not a field.
- **What is still not claimed.** The measured ΔL\* (3.41–4.72, [`THEME_ACCEPTANCE_MATRIX.md §9.2`](../design/THEME_ACCEPTANCE_MATRIX.md)) is verified numerically and reviewed in captured screenshots at 1280 and 1440 in all seven themes, light and dark. That is a screenshot and automation claim, not a claim about personal taste after extended use.
- **Desired future state.** Card-on-tint verified at desktop widths on real content, with the evidence in [`THEME_ACCEPTANCE_MATRIX.md`](../design/THEME_ACCEPTANCE_MATRIX.md) as a DS-14 section, and DS-14's verification widths extended to include 2560 so its matrix is not narrower than the suite the product already runs.
- **Closing condition — met.** The wide-desktop failure mode was identified and fixed inside DS-14; the DS-14 acceptance section is written; `responsive.spec.ts`'s 320–2560 sweep passes over every restyled surface. The assumption was tested, which is what this entry was raised for.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DS-14](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ds-14--whole-application-visual-overhaul).

### ☑ DEBT-106 — `main`'s E2E suite is broadly red: 36 journeys across 19 spec files fail for reasons unrelated to the change that found them — P1 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-09**

- **Resolved (2026-08-09) by the [E2E regression audit](E2E_REGRESSION_AUDIT_2026_08_09.md).** A complete Playwright run was taken against `aa83b8a` before anything changed, every failure was classified, and the shared causes were fixed at the shared component. The audit document carries the numbers, the classification and the repair for each; what belongs here is the closing evidence and the three places this census sent the next reader the wrong way.
- **Three diagnoses in the census below are WRONG, and the record says so rather than being quietly rewritten.** Each cost real time to re-derive:
  - *"`projects.spec.ts` — the roll-up progress bar is missing, not merely wrong."* It is not missing. RECORD-01 folded it into the record's summary band and renamed it from "Roll-up progress" to **"Tasks"**; the failure snapshot shows `progressbar "Tasks"` on the page the assertion timed out against.
  - *"`today.spec.ts:93` — the overdue day list renders four rows against a documented cap of three. It is the cap, not the fixture volume."* The cap is correct and shows three. The **"+n more overdue" row carried the same class as a task row**, so the test counted the link that says how many were left out. The row now carries the `dh-day-row--more` modifier the test was already filtering on — a modifier that had existed nowhere in the application.
  - *"`people.spec.ts` — the card's `Archived` status `<span>` intercepts pointer events aimed at the card's own `Restore` button. A shared-Card layering problem."* It is the **cold action rail**: `opacity: 0` and `pointer-events: none` until the pointer arrives over the card, which is deliberate (UIQ-002). Playwright's visibility check ignores `opacity`, so the button reads as "visible, enabled and stable" and the hit test returns whatever happens to sit under the transparent rail — the status chip here, the card's `<h2>` title on the Notes Deleted view. The product is correct; the V2.2 programme had already diagnosed and fixed the same thing for `tasks-daily-driver.spec.ts`, and its helper is now shared as `clickCardAction`.
- **Two rows of the table were already closed by the work that recorded them, and the audit's run confirms it on `main`:** all eight `meetings-follow-up.spec.ts` journeys and all five `tasks-daily-driver.spec.ts` journeys pass.
- **One row is a product regression, and is NOT closed by this entry.** The four `assets-ownership.spec.ts` failures are Today having lost its Assets section — see [DEBT-111](#-debt-111--today-lost-its-assets-section-so-an-obligation-with-no-linked-task-reaches-the-owner-nowhere-outside-the-assets-module--p2). The journeys now assert the obligation where the product genuinely surfaces it; the missing surface is tracked there.
- **What was actually wrong, in one line each:** one shared select field putting one accessible name on two nested elements · one meeting form doing the same · fifteen fixtures able to open the local D1 and only ten able to survive contention · one shared section hard-coding its heading levels into a real WCAG `heading-order` failure · one `count()` used as a branch condition before its panel had rendered · and a long tail of assertions describing surfaces the last ten merges deliberately replaced.
- **Closing condition met.** A full Playwright run on `main` plus this change is green, with no spec excluded, no `.skip`/`.fixme` added, no browser dropped and no retry raised. The only skips are the opt-in screenshot-capture specs (`CAPTURE_SCREENSHOTS=1`), which were skipped before this change too.

<details>
<summary>The original census, kept as it was taken</summary>

- **The heading counts what is red NOW (36); the census below counts what was red when it was taken (41).** V2.2 fixed five of them and the struck-through table row says which. The census is left as it was measured rather than quietly rewritten, because its value is that it was a real before/after run against a named CI job.
- **Status: raised 2026-08-08 by [PEOPLE-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-people-04--mobile-people) / [DIARY-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-diary-02--day-context-links); widened 2026-08-08 by [AUDIT-FIX-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-07--atomic-compound-mutations-one-long-form-editor-and-the-dead-code-p3--delivered-2026-08-08) into a full census. Every failure below was reproduced at a clean `origin/main` with the branch checked out from under it.**
- **Current issue.** AUDIT-FIX-07's CI run (`31258582194`, head `7b0910d`, base `main` at `808a9b6`) failed **42** Playwright tests across 10 of 18 shards. Each was then re-run locally against detached `origin/main` (`808a9b6`) with the branch's work stashed. **41 of the 42 failed identically on `main`.** Exactly one — `e2e/forms.spec.ts` *the long-form field is the shared writing surface* — was AUDIT-FIX-07's own, and is fixed in that PR. The 41 are, by spec file:

  | Spec | Failing journeys |
  |---|---|
  | `assets-ownership.spec.ts` | 4 (`:211`, `:364`, `:459`, `:691`) — plus `:289` which also fails on `main` locally but was green in that CI run |
  | `command-palette.spec.ts` | 3 (`:109`, `:208`, `:234`) |
  | `creation-controls.spec.ts` | 2 (`:133`, `:186`) |
  | `csrf.spec.ts` | 1 (`:162`) |
  | `global-capture.spec.ts` | 1 (`:359`) |
  | `meetings-follow-up.spec.ts` | 8 (all) — **fixed by AUDIT-FIX-07, see below** |
  | `mobile-capture-journeys.spec.ts` | 1 (`:200`) |
  | `mobile-modules.spec.ts` | 1 (`:201`) |
  | `notes.spec.ts` | 1 (`:540`) |
  | `project-health.spec.ts` | 2 (`:66`, `:110`) |
  | `project-settings.spec.ts` | 1 (`:213`) |
  | `projects.spec.ts` | 2 (`:67`, `:413`) |
  | `projects-mobile.spec.ts` | 1 (`:185`) |
  | `reviews-guided.spec.ts` | 1 (`:455`) |
  | `settings.spec.ts` | 2 (`:69`, `:138`) |
  | ~~`tasks-daily-driver.spec.ts`~~ | ~~5 (`:117`, `:145`, `:243`, `:275`, `:307`)~~ — **fixed**, see below |
  | `today.spec.ts` | 1 (`:93`) |
  | `tooltip.spec.ts` | 3 (`:81`, `:95`, `:108`) |
  | `visual-system.spec.ts` | 1 (`:134`) |
  | `people.spec.ts` | 1 — raised by PEOPLE-04/DIARY-02, not in this CI run's shards |

- **Two causes are diagnosed; the rest are reproduced but not yet explained.** Do not read the table as one defect:
  - **The Today `h1` is a greeting, not the word "Today".** [#131](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/131) replaced the Today screen and its `h1` now renders *"Good evening, Local"*. `csrf.spec.ts:162` waits for `getByRole("heading", { level: 1, name: "Today" })` and times out; the page itself is fully rendered and correct in the failure snapshot. The `creation-controls` and `command-palette` failures are on the same surface and are likely the same cause, but that was not confirmed test-by-test.
  - **`getByLabel("Add action item")` is a strict-mode violation.** The item input and its submit button carry the same `aria-label`, so the label matches two controls. This one **is fixed here** — AUDIT-FIX-07 rewrote the eight `meetings-follow-up.spec.ts` journeys to ask for the textbox by role, because that spec is the only browser-level regression coverage of the meeting→Task conversion path AUDIT-13 rewrote, and shipping that rewrite with its own E2E coverage disabled would have been the worse call. The **product-side** duplicate accessible name is untouched and still worth fixing.
  - `people.spec.ts` — in the Archived People collection the card's `Archived` status `<span>` intercepts pointer events aimed at the card's own `Restore` button, so the click never lands. A shared-Card layering problem.
  - **Three more diagnosed 2026-08-08 by the [V2.2 Tasks daily-driver programme](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md)**, which re-ran every module's list surface because it changed the shared Card. Each was re-run in isolation, then against a **deleted `.wrangler/state`** so the local D1 was migrated and seeded from scratch, then on detached `origin/main` with that same fresh database — failing identically at every step, so none is drift and none is V2.2's:
    - `settings.spec.ts:69` — the spec's `choose()` helper calls `selectOption` on `getByLabel("Owner timezone")`, which resolves to **two** elements: the field's wrapping `role="group"` and the typeahead `role="combobox"` input inside it. It fails strict mode before it can attempt the write. The control became a combobox and `selectOption` only works on a native `<select>`, so this needs rewriting as click → fill → choose option, not a locator tweak.
    - `projects.spec.ts:67` and `:413` — both fail the same way: `getByRole("progressbar", { name: "Roll-up progress" })` matches nothing at all on the Project record, so the first times out reading `aria-valuetext` and the second cannot assert `100% — 60 of 60 tasks complete`. The roll-up progress bar is missing, not merely wrong.
    - `today.spec.ts:93` — the overdue day list renders **four** rows against a documented cap of three (`.dh-day-list--overdue .dh-day-row:not(.dh-day-row--more)`), on a freshly seeded database. It is the cap, not the fixture volume.
  - Everything else in the table is reproduced on `main` and nothing more. No cause is claimed for it here.
- **Five rows of the table are struck through, because V2.2 fixed them.** All five `tasks-daily-driver.spec.ts` journeys drove a row's action rail COLD — clicking a control that is `pointer-events: none` until the pointer arrives over the row, which is a state no person can reach. They pass on the V2.2 branch, which reaches them through one `rowAction` helper that scrolls, clears the sticky header and hovers first. Two `tasks-journey.spec.ts` failures of the same kind were repaired rather than recorded: *Today and Projects project the same task* filled a date input with the UTC day instead of the owner's calendar day (ADR-022), planning the task for yesterday whenever the suite ran late in the owner's day; and *no horizontal overflow across views after creating a task* carried a 90s budget whose comment described sixteen navigations while the loop had grown to thirty-six. All of these are in the Tasks suite that programme owns, so fixing them was in scope where the twenty other spec files were not.
- **What the two `settings.spec.ts` rows cost V2.2, stated plainly.** `:69` aborts at the timezone step, which is *before* the assertion V2.2 updated in the same test (the Default Tasks view now offering "Time Sectors" rather than the removed "Matrix"). That one assertion is therefore not exercised end to end. The behaviour underneath it is covered — `test/kernel/app-preferences.test.ts` and `test/unit/preferences/app-preferences.test.ts` cover the stored value and the resolution of a stored `matrix` default, and `e2e/tasks-collection.spec.ts` → *choosing a DEFAULT Tasks view does not change the Today dashboard* drives the same preference from the Tasks side and passes — but the Settings-page path itself is unproven until this entry is closed.
- **Why the rest is recorded rather than fixed.** Thirty-six failures across nineteen spec files and a dozen modules are not one defect, and folding them into an atomicity/editor PR would make that PR un-reviewable (AGENTS.md §13, one item per PR). The one exception above is argued on its own merits, not waved through. The rest are recorded with their reproduction so the next agent starts from evidence rather than from a red build.
- **Severity: P2 → P1 (2026-08-08).** This was filed as P2 when it described six tests. At 41 it is a different thing: the E2E gate no longer distinguishes a regression from the baseline, which is precisely the condition [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02) was raised and closed for. Every PR from here has to run its own before/after census — as this one did — to say anything true about its effect on CI.
- **Related.** [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02) (closed, and re-opened in substance by this entry) and [DEBT-76](#-debt-76--a-chromium-renderer-segfault-fails-playwright-shards-non-deterministically-and-retries-0-turns-it-into-a-red-build--p2) — this entry is about *deterministic* failures, not the non-deterministic segfault DEBT-76 covers.
- **Desired future state.** `main`'s E2E suite is green, or every remaining failure is diagnosed on its own module's terms and explained. Until then `main`'s E2E suite is not green, and no item should claim it is.
- **Closing condition.** A full Playwright run on `main` is green, or each remaining entry in the table above carries a diagnosis and a decision.

</details>

### ☐ DEBT-73 — Area colour is derived from rank, so the owner cannot choose it and a permanent deletion repaints later Areas — P3

- **Current issue.** [ADR-068 decision 5](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-068-ds-14--the-card-on-tint-direction-its-elevation-contract-two-density-presets-derived-area-colour-and-a-single-commit-rollback) derives an Area's accent rather than storing it: rank the workspace's Areas by `(created_at, id)` and take `area-accent-((rank mod 6) + 1)`. Nothing is persisted — `area_details` ([migration `0013`](../../migrations/0013_create_area_details.sql)) holds one nullable `archived_at` and states outright that it is not a second identity model, and there is no colour column on `entities`. Derivation was chosen because it needs **no migration**, and because the alternatives are worse: a hash of the id collides with ≈91% probability at five Areas over a six-colour ramp, and a stored colour needs both a migration and an owner-facing picker that [`DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md §9`](../design/DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md) explicitly puts out of scope.
- **Impact.** Two properties derivation cannot give. **The owner cannot choose an Area's colour** — if Health comes out orange and that reads wrong to the person whose life it is, there is no way to change it. And **a permanent Area deletion repaints every Area created after it**, because rank depends on the set. That is bounded on purpose (rank is computed over every `area` row regardless of lifecycle state, so archiving and soft-deleting change nothing, and only the irreversible typed-confirmation delete shifts anything) but it is still an Area changing colour as a side effect of a different Area being removed. Beyond six Areas the ramp wraps and two Areas share an accent; that is tolerable only because [`§10`](../design/DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md) forbids colour as the sole carrier of meaning — the dot always has an accessible name and always sits beside the Area's own title.
- **Desired future state.** An **optional** stored colour: a nullable accent column on the Area detail slice, defaulting to null, with the derived value used whenever it is null. That keeps every existing Area's colour exactly as it is on the day it ships, makes owner choice additive rather than a reset, and pins a chosen colour against the deletion shift. It belongs wherever Area settings next get owner-facing surface, not inside a restyle.
- **How it would be done, so the path is not re-derived.** One additive migration on `area_details` in the precedent that table already sets (nullable column, no backfill, absence means the default); the accent resolver gains a stored-value branch ahead of the rank computation; the picker offers only the six ramp accents so [`§2`](../design/DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md)'s colour discipline holds and no owner can select a role colour; and the DS-14 invariant test covers the stored values by construction, because it already asserts every `area-accent-*` pairing in every theme.
- **Closing condition.** An Area's colour can be set and cleared from the Area record, a cleared value falls back to the derived one, a stored value survives another Area's permanent deletion, and the accent contrast assertions still pass in all seven themes.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DS-14](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ds-14--whole-application-visual-overhaul) (follow-on), landing with [SET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-set-03--account--security--substantially-delivered-2026-08-08) or whichever item next opens Area settings.

---

### ☐ DEBT-74 — An Area's identity dot stops at the Areas collection — P3

- **Current issue.** The Area identity accent is resolved by `listAreas`, which ranks over every `area` row in the workspace ([ADR-068 decision 5](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-068-ds-14--the-card-on-tint-direction-its-elevation-contract-two-density-presets-derived-area-colour-and-a-single-commit-rollback), implemented in [`d1-area-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-area-repository.ts)). That rank exists **only on that query**, so an Area referenced from another module's row — a Project card's "Area: DalyHub V2", a Task row's Area breadcrumb, an Asset's Area — shows the Area's name and no dot.
- **What this entry is NOT any more.** It was originally raised as a correctness defect: the accent came from the index into the *active* collection, so archiving an early Area recoloured every Area after it. That was fixed — the rank is now a window function over all `area` rows regardless of lifecycle state, and [`area-lifecycle.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/area-lifecycle.test.ts) archives the earliest Area and asserts every other rank is unchanged. What is left is propagation, which is a smaller thing, hence **P2 → P3**.
- **Impact.** Small and bounded. The name is the identification; the dot is a shortcut for a reader who has already seen it, which is exactly why [`§10`](../design/DS_14_OVERHAUL_BRIEF.md) forbids colour as the sole carrier of meaning. What is lost is the cross-module scan — noticing at a glance that three of today's tasks are all Health — never the ability to tell which Area something belongs to.
- **Desired future state.** Any surface that loads an Area's name also has its accent.
- **How it would be done, so the path is not re-derived.** The `area_ranks` CTE already exists and is index-served; the work is exposing it on the Area projections the other modules read (the Project/Task/Asset Area summaries), not deriving anything new. If [DEBT-73](#-debt-73--area-colour-is-derived-from-rank-so-the-owner-cannot-choose-it-and-a-permanent-deletion-repaints-later-areas--p3) lands first, a stored colour supersedes this entirely — a stored value needs no rank — so consider the two together.
- **Closing condition.** An Area's dot appears wherever its name appears, with the same accent as on the Areas collection, and no query-plan regression.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DS-14](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ds-14--whole-application-visual-overhaul) (follow-on).

---

### ☐ DEBT-75 — The service-worker byte budget has 2,185 B of headroom — P3

- **Current issue.** `sw.js` measures **21,815 B against the fixed 24,000 B budget** after DS-14 (it was 12,147 B before the self-hosted fonts and 22,514 B before the comment added in this work was trimmed). The budget is fixed and was not raised — [`e2e/pwa-budget.spec.ts`](../../e2e/pwa-budget.spec.ts) still asserts 24,000 — but the margin is now about 9%.
- **Impact.** None today; the gate is green. The risk is the failure MODE: this file is served verbatim, so its own comments cost budget, and the next person to add an explanatory paragraph to `sw-template.js` will fail a budget assertion for a reason that has nothing to do with what they were changing. DS-14 hit this twice — the foundation trimmed the header prose to fit, and this work trimmed a comment after measuring it.
- **Desired future state.** Either the worker's prose lives in `PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md` with only pointers in the file (the direction both trims already took), or the build minifies the worker so comments are free and the budget measures behaviour rather than documentation.
- **How it would be done, so the path is not re-derived.** Minification is the better answer and is a plugin change, not a budget change: `vite-plugins/service-worker.ts` already post-processes the template to substitute `__DALYHUB_*__`, so a minify pass belongs in the same step. The budget stays at 24,000 and starts measuring code, which is what it was meant to measure.
- **Closing condition.** Adding a paragraph of explanation to `sw-template.js` cannot fail the byte budget.
- **Related roadmap item.** None — housekeeping against [PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md §12](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md#12-measurements).

---

### ☐ DEBT-76 — A Chromium renderer segfault fails Playwright shards non-deterministically, and `retries: 0` turns it into a red build — P2

- **Current issue.** `chrome-headless-shell-1228` on the `ubuntu-24.04` runner image intermittently dies mid-run with `Received signal 11 SEGV_MAPERR 0000000001b0` (`cr2: 0x1b0`, identical stack every time). The test that happens to be executing then fails with `browser.newContext: Target page, context or browser has been closed` — **no assertion has failed and nothing is wrong with the code**, but the shard exits non-zero and `CI Gate` goes red because it aggregates `playwright`.
- **It is not new, and it is not any one branch's.** The same signature appears on **`main` at [`6aba3ae`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/commit/6aba3ae) (run 30802345484, shard 1, `/people` axe scan)** and on [PR #106](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/106) (run 30841044978, shards 3 and 5, `diary` and `people`). It was briefly and wrongly attributed to a scroll-driven animation in that PR; the `main` occurrence predates that code, which is what settles it. The crash favours axe scans and repeated-viewport-resize tests — long-lived pages doing heavy layout work — but has hit at least three different specs.
- **Impact.** **Four of the ten most recent `main` CI runs are red**, and a red `main` is corrosive in a specific way: it stops being information. Once a maintainer learns that failures are usually noise, a real regression rides in behind one, and the cost of the flake is paid by the change that follows it rather than by the flake itself. It also makes every PR's CI a coin toss on 14 shards — the more shards, the likelier at least one crashes.
- **2026-08-07 — a fourth occurrence, and the shard count has moved against us.** On [PR #124](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/124) (run 31134265194, head `3a0228b`) **shard 2/18** died during `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts:141` — the `/design/search` axe scan, a fifth distinct spec and again an axe scan, matching the pattern this entry already describes. The signature is identical to the letter: `Received signal 11 SEGV_MAPERR 0000000001b0`, `cr2: 00000000000001b0`, same `chrome-headless-shell-1228` stack. The shard reported **1 failed, 68 passed**, the other **17 shards were green**, and the test passes locally under `--repeat-each=3` (3/3) — so nothing in the diff is implicated. Two things are worth recording beyond "it happened again". First, **the matrix is 18 shards now, not the 14 this entry was written against**; since each shard is an independent chance to crash, every widening of the split has quietly raised the odds that a run is red for this reason alone, which means the split decisions and this entry are coupled in a way neither noted. Second, **re-running the failed job is not always available** — an automation without `actions: write` cannot clear a crash-only failure, so the only route back to green is a fresh push, which spends another full 18-shard run on a coin toss. That asymmetry is the practical cost of `retries: 0` here, and it is an argument for trying the browser-build upgrade **first**, as the remedy below already prefers.
- **2026-08-07 — the preferred remedy was tried and did NOT work; the crash follows the browser forward.** [PR #125](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/125) took the remedy this entry prefers — keep `retries: 0`, move the browser build — and upgraded `@playwright/test` 1.61.1 → 1.62.1 specifically because "the crash is specific to `chrome-headless-shell-1228`". It is not. On [PR #126](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/126) the identical signature appeared **twice, on two different commits and two different shards**, on the NEW browser: run 31152862102 shard 2/18 (job 92786092584, head `b449c7d`) and run 31154576594 shard 3/18 (job 92791337437, head `7b34772`). Both read `Received signal 11 SEGV_MAPERR 0000000001b0`, `cr2: 00000000000001b0`, in `chromium_headless_shell-**1234**` — one build number different, one byte of evidence identical. Both died inside `e2e/ai-assistance.spec.ts` during an axe scan, which is the sixth distinct spec and the same axe pattern this entry has recorded every time. Both shards reported **1 failed, 71 passed** with no assertion failure of any kind.
- **What that changes about the remedy.** The upgrade was the right thing to try first and it has now been tried; the crash is not a property of build 1228. So the branch of the plan that says "pin/upgrade the browser build, since the crash is specific to 1228" is **closed as ineffective**, and what remains is the `retries: process.env.CI ? 1 : 0` change this entry already describes — with the flaky-not-passed reporting that keeps the noise visible. That is a testing-contract change and belongs in its own PR with its own reasoning, not folded into a product change; PR #126 deliberately recorded this evidence rather than absorbing the fix.
- **Desired future state.** A shard that fails only because the browser died retries and passes, and a shard that fails because an assertion failed still goes red immediately.
- **How it would be done, so the path is not re-derived.** The repository sets `retries: 0` in [`playwright.config.ts`](../../playwright.config.ts) deliberately — "no retries that could mask flakiness" (ADR-008/ADR-016), and that instinct is right for **assertion** flakiness. A browser-process crash is a different class: it is not the product being non-deterministic, it is the harness dying. The narrow change is `retries: process.env.CI ? 1 : 0`, which leaves local runs strict; Playwright's HTML report marks a retried test `flaky` rather than `passed`, so the noise stays **visible** instead of being swallowed — which is the property that makes this different from simply weakening the gate. A stricter alternative is to keep `retries: 0` and pin/upgrade the Playwright browser build, since the crash is specific to `chrome-headless-shell-1228`; that is the better fix if an upgrade makes it go away, and it should be tried first.
- **Closing condition.** Ten consecutive `main` runs green, with any retried test still reported as flaky rather than silently passing.
- **Related roadmap item.** None — CI hygiene. Surfaced by [DS-14](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ds-14--whole-application-visual-overhaul).

### ☐ DEBT-77 — A project card cannot say what the next action is — P3

- **Current issue.** POLISH-02's brief asked Today's project cards to carry a **next action** alongside progress and health. They now carry health for every project (not only the ones in trouble), the open-task count and a progress meter — all derived from data the loader already reads — but not the next action, because there is none to read. [`listProjectHealthFacts`](../../app/kernel/project-health/project-health-repository.ts) aggregates *counts* per project (`overdueOpen`, `waitingOpen`, `upcomingDueOpen`, …), not the identity of any task, and [`listProjects`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-project-repository.ts) returns roll-ups rather than children.
- **Impact.** The owner can see that a project needs attention and how far along it is, but must open it to learn what to actually do. On a surface whose whole purpose is "what should I do now?", that is one click more than it should be for the six projects the section shows.
- **Desired future state.** Each "Continue working" card names its next actionable task — the highest-priority open, non-waiting task with the nearest scheduled or due date — as a row that opens that task in the same Drawer Today already hosts.
- **How it would be done, so the path is not re-derived.** This must NOT become six queries on the most-visited route in the product. The shape that fits the existing code is a single bounded, workspace-scoped statement over the six project ids — a `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY project ORDER BY <the Tasks smart-sort expression>)` filtered to rank 1 — added beside `listProjectHealthFacts` and read in the same `Promise.all`, returning a `Map<projectId, TaskListItem>`. The smart-sort expression already exists in [`d1-task-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-task-repository.ts); reuse it rather than inventing a second notion of "next", or Today and `/tasks` will disagree about which task is next.
- **Closing condition.** Every card in "Continue working" names its next action, the route's query count is unchanged (one additional statement, not N), and a test pins Today's "next" to the same ordering `/tasks` uses.
- **Related roadmap item.** [POLISH-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-polish-02--the-today-command-centre).
- **Still open, with a proven rule to reuse (2026-08-05).** [REVIEW-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-02--weekly-review--delivered-2026-08-05) needed the same answer for its Project check and derived one WITHOUT a per-project query: ONE bounded scan of the workspace's most actionable open Tasks under the canonical `smart` sort, bucketed by Project, first-per-Project wins — and where a Project's work falls outside that bound it says "No next action visible here" rather than claiming there is none. That is a deliberately cheaper approximation than the `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY …)` statement described above, and it does NOT close this entry: Today's cards still name no next action, and Today should use the exact ranked statement so it can never disagree with `/tasks`. Reuse the wording ("No next action visible here") and the never-invent rule; do not reuse the bounded scan for a surface that must be exhaustive.
- **Closing condition (unchanged).** Every card in "Continue working" names its next action, the route's query count is unchanged, and a test pins Today's "next" to the same ordering `/tasks` uses.

### ☐ DEBT-78 — Goals can state completion but not trend — P3 — **NARROWED 2026-08-09 by GOAL-02**

- **Narrowed, not closed.** [GOAL-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md) answered this for a
  Goal that carries a MEASUREMENT: a measurable Goal now records readings over
  time, states its recent pace against the pace it needs, and shows a trend line —
  all from one derivation (`evaluateGoalProgress`) that Today, the Goals gallery
  and the Goal record share, so they cannot disagree. What remains is the case
  this entry was originally written about: a Goal whose only progress signal is
  its CONTRIBUTING PROJECTS. "40% of Projects complete" still reads the same on a
  Goal that gained two this month and one that has not moved since March, and the
  approach below (a bounded Activity query over the contributing project ids) is
  still the right way to close it.

- **Original issue.** POLISH-02's brief asked the Goals widget for **trend, completion % and recent progress**. It now shows completion (from the contribution read the alignment evaluation already performs) and recent progress (the derived alignment label — "Recent action" / "No recent action"). It cannot show a trend, because nothing in the system stores a goal's completion *over time*: [`listGoalProjectContributions`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-goal-repository.ts) returns the current roll-up only, and there is no snapshot table or historical projection to difference against.
- **Impact.** The owner sees where a goal stands but not whether it is moving. "40% complete" reads the same on a goal that gained two projects this month and one that has not moved since March — and *is it moving?* is the question a Goal exists to answer.
- **Desired future state.** A goal states its direction over a meaningful window ("+2 Projects complete this month", or a sparkline), on Today and on the Goal record, from one shared derivation.
- **How it would be done, so the path is not re-derived.** Do NOT add a snapshot table first — the Activity stream is already the historical record ([ADR-005](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-005-shared-activity-model)), and a goal's contributing projects completing is a `project.completed` activity on a project linked to the goal. A bounded activity query over the goal's contributing project ids within the window gives the delta without any new write path or migration. The derivation belongs in [`~/shared/alignment`](../../app/shared/alignment) beside the existing evaluator, so Today and the Goal record cannot disagree.
- **Closing condition.** A goal's direction is stated in the same words on Today and on its record, derived once, with no new table and no per-goal query on the Today route.
- **Related roadmap item.** [POLISH-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-polish-02--the-today-command-centre).

## Debt raised by UIX-03 (Goals redesign, 2026-08-10)

### ☑ DEBT-149 — Sixteen E2E journeys fail on `main` for reasons unrelated to any current change — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 (HARDEN-05)**

- **Current issue.** Measured at `cd385cfd` by stashing FINISH-01's branch and re-running each failing spec on the clean tree, sixteen tests fail on `main` today:

  | Spec | Failing | What it looks like |
  |---|---|---|
  | `goals-outcomes.spec.ts` | 7 | the whole file |
  | `collection-header.spec.ts` | 5 | "the title keeps its row…" plus the four UIQ-021 menu-placement tests |
  | `projects-mobile.spec.ts` | 2 | the two long journeys; the first asks the lifecycle rail for a tab named "Open", and the rail labels that value "Active" |
  | `assets.spec.ts` | 1 | "create, edit, search, filter, archive, restore, delete" times out on `collection-sheet-type-vehicle` |
  | `spine-workspaces.spec.ts` | 1 | "records a measurement from the workspace and updates the trio and the chart" |

  This is the condition [DEBT-106](#-debt-106--mains-e2e-suite-is-broadly-red-36-journeys-across-19-spec-files-fail-for-reasons-unrelated-to-the-change-that-found-them--p1--resolved-2026-08-09) was raised for and closed on, arrived at again. It is P2 rather than P1 because it is a third the size and none of it touches data integrity or workspace isolation — but it has the same corrosive effect, which DEBT-106 named exactly: a suite with a standing red set means every author has to re-derive which failures are theirs, and the cost of that is paid on every change rather than once.

- **Why it was not fixed here.** FINISH-01 is an audit-completion pass over Analytics, People, Notes, Goals, Projects and the phone shell. Diagnosing sixteen failures across five specs it did not write — at least one of which (`projects-mobile`'s "Open" tab) looks like a spec asserting a label the product deliberately changed, and others of which look like fixture or timing problems — is a different piece of work with a different shape. Adopting it mid-package would have meant either doing it badly or stopping the audit to do it properly.

  What this pass did instead is the part that keeps the number honest: every failure above was **verified against the clean tree** before being called pre-existing, and each is named in the commit that met it. No failing test was skipped, weakened or deleted to make a phase look green.

- **The set is LARGER than the table above, measured again at `0fd18cc` (TODAY-TASK-01, 2026-08-17).** Twenty further journeys were reproduced on the clean tree by stashing the branch and re-running each one, and every one of them fails identically with and without the change:

  | Spec | Failing | What it looks like |
  |---|---|---|
  | `iphone-daily-driver.spec.ts` | 6 | the three shared-overflow-menu journeys, the two "never triggers iOS zoom" checks on `/notes` and `/tasks`, and "a task row's open link is comfortably tall" |
  | `today-mobile.spec.ts` | 3 | all three "touch accelerators on the Tasks collection" swipe-tray journeys |
  | `today-focus.spec.ts` | 1 | "bounds a large day…" — it asserts the Tasks pane subtitle reads "13 Tasks" and CONVERGE-01 §B changed that line to a state breakdown ("13 active"). A stale assertion about copy the product deliberately changed, on both sides of the diff |
  | `today-keyboard.spec.ts` | 1 | "a lower task drawer does not own shortcuts when help is stacked above it" |
  | `goal-measurement.spec.ts` | 2 | both ask the Goal's metrics for a "Start" reading; the seeded Goal has accumulated measurements from earlier runs of the same spec, so the record no longer shows one. Shared-database accumulation, which is the class HARDEN-02 named |
  | `tasks-collection.spec.ts` | 4 | the two filter journeys, the keyboard journey, and "sets a due date from the row without touching the planned date" |
  | `tasks.spec.ts` | 2 | "lists workspace tasks and opens one in the canonical Drawer" and "renders the shared priority + urgency signals on task cards" |
  | `tasks-v22-daily-driver.spec.ts` | 1 | "captures, edits, multi-selects and bulk-moves from a phone" |

- **One further test is a DURATION boundary rather than a failure, and it is the same accumulation.** `tasks-collection.spec.ts` → "is axe-clean in light and dark across the presentations" walks every presentation in both appearances and runs axe on each. It measures 2.0 minutes against the suite's 120-second `timeout` — i.e. it has no margin — and whether it clears the line depends on how many rows the shared workspace has accumulated by the time it runs. MEASURED at `0fd18cc`: it passed in isolation and timed out inside a nine-spec batch, on the same tree, twice each way. It is recorded here rather than "fixed" by raising a timeout, which is the one repair this register forbids.

  They are recorded here rather than fixed because they are the same condition this entry already names, in the same shape, and TODAY-TASK-01 is a Today/Tasks convergence rather than a suite triage. None of them was skipped, weakened or deleted, and each was verified against the clean tree before being called pre-existing.

- **Desired future state.** The set is triaged and closed the way DEBT-106 was — each failure attributed to a product regression, a stale assertion or a fixture problem, and fixed as whichever it turns out to be. Until it is, `main` cannot honestly be described as green, and the next audit-completion pass will pay the same re-derivation cost this one did.

- **Resolved by [HARDEN-05](HARDEN_05_GREEN_MAIN_2026_08.md) at `f994aa0`, against a set TWICE the size this entry recorded.** Measured before any edit, on a clean checkout, over all ten partitions with a fresh local D1 each: **73 failing / 1678**, across 26 spec files — not 16, and not the 36 the second table brings it to. CI run 32019461430 at the same SHA agrees (p01…p08 red, p09/p10 green) and its `p01` names exactly the same three failures the local `p01` produced, which is what makes the local measurement trustworthy for the other nine.

  The 73 collapse into **eight** root causes rather than 26 defects, and 64 of them are one shape: a spec addressing an anatomy the product deliberately replaced (DS-04's `TaskRow`, CONTROL-01's controls popover, REDESIGN-04's Goals workspace and metric trio, ADR-100's default table, NOTES-06's shared filter grammar, and four renamed controls). Nine were the product being wrong, each measured before and after — the task row's 19.6px open target on a phone, two fields below the 16px iOS-zoom floor, Today's 204px document at 195px, an unnamed open link on the Projects table, a `<span>` where `SelectField` should have had a `<label>`, and the shell precache over its ceiling (now **DEBT-151**).

  Two of this entry's own diagnoses were wrong, which is the argument for re-measuring rather than inheriting a list. `goal-measurement`'s two failures are recorded above as "the seeded Goal has accumulated measurements from earlier runs of the same spec"; the readings were correct throughout and REDESIGN-04 had moved the label. `assets.spec.ts`'s timeout on `collection-sheet-type-vehicle` is CONTROL-01 — at a desktop width there is no sheet — and it was taking the whole Asset lifecycle journey down with it.

  No test was skipped, deleted, weakened or retried to reach this: see the hardening record's §4.

### ☐ DEBT-150 — A plan row restates the date its own band already states — P3

- **Current issue.** Today's plan groups the day into three named bands — Overdue, Due today, Planned today — and every row inside a band draws the shared row's DATE cell, which on "Due today" reads "Today" on every row and on "Planned today" reads "Today" on every row. MEASURED on the seeded fixture at 393: eight rows, seven of them printing a date that the heading twelve pixels above them has already stated, in a metadata line that also has to hold the project and the priority.
- **Why it was not changed in [TODAY-TASK-01](../design/TODAY_TASK_01_ONE_TASK_ROW_2026_08.md).** It is not a Today defect. `/tasks` grouped by due state does exactly the same thing for exactly the same reason — the row shows its own date and the group states the bucket — so suppressing it on Today alone would be a Today-only rule on the row that pass exists to stop having Today-only rules on. And the cell is not a label: it is the EDITOR, so it cannot simply be hidden without taking the inline reschedule with it.
- **Impact.** Small and cosmetic. What it costs is width on a phone's metadata line and a little scanning noise on a desktop; nothing is wrong or misleading.
- **Desired future state.** A SHARED capability on `TaskList` — an "implied date" the list declares once — which lets a row draw its date cell in its EMPTY presentation (the control, no value) when the value merely restates the group. It has to be shared, it has to keep the control reachable and it has to keep the accessible name honest ("Due date: Today"), which is why it is a design decision rather than a `display: none`.
- **Closing condition.** A grouped day (Today) and a grouped list (`/tasks?group=due`) both stop printing the bucket's own date on every row, through one declaration on the shared list, with the date still editable from the row and still announced in full.
- **Related roadmap item.** A future Tasks/Today refinement; the mechanism belongs with DS-04's row anatomy.

### ☐ DEBT-120 — The Goals gallery is ordered by alignment, not by outcome — P3

- **Current issue.** `/goals` is ordered by the workspace-wide alignment rank (DEBT-23): neglected Goals first, then active, then completed. That is the ADR-040 Alignment view's own ordering and it is correct for the question ADR-040 asked. UIX-03 changed the question the screen answers — it is now an outcomes gallery — and under that reading the order is arbitrary: an unmeasured Goal with no recent Task activity can sit above a measured Goal that is behind its own target date.
- **Why it was not changed here.** The order is established in SQL BEFORE pagination and the keyset cursor is bound to it, so re-ranking means a second ranking expression, a second cursor scope and a status the database can compute — and the status is a kernel derivation over measurements, dates and the owner's calendar. UIX-03 gave the owner the lens instead (the All / On track / Needs attention / Completed views), which answers "which Goals need me?" without a query change.
- **Desired future state.** Decide whether `/goals` is the Alignment view or the outcomes gallery. If the latter, an outcome-first ordering needs its own ranking expression and cursor scope, designed together with whether alignment keeps a surface of its own.

### ☐ DEBT-121 — Status view counts describe the loaded page, not the workspace — P3

- **Current issue.** The Goals status views carry counts ("On track 3"), derived from the Goals loaded so far. A workspace with more Goals than one page shows counts for the page, beside a subtitle that already says "N Goals loaded".
- **Why it was not changed here.** A workspace-wide count per status means computing every Goal's derived status server-side for the whole workspace on every collection render — the status depends on measurement history, target dates and the owner's calendar day, so it cannot be a cheap aggregate. The per-page limitation is the same one ADR-040 §40.9 already discloses for the alignment recap, and the counts are consistent with the cards beneath them, which is the property that matters most.
- **Desired future state.** Either a bounded materialised status the collection can aggregate, or drop the counts if the workspace-wide figure is what owners read them as.

### ☐ DEBT-122 — `ExpressiveSummary` now has no consumer — P3

- **Current issue.** UIX-03 replaced the Goals gallery's expressive banner (a ring, two counts and a headline, all describing alignment rather than an outcome) with a compact status-view row. The Goals collection was `ExpressiveSummary`'s **last** consumer, so the component, its styles, its `MAX_SUMMARY_STATS` contract and its M3X "hierarchy Level 1" documentation now describe something no product surface renders.
- **Why it was not removed here.** It is a documented design-system component with a stated role in the M3X hierarchy, and `SupportingSurface` is defined in relation to it. Deleting it is a design-system decision — is "one dominant expressive surface per page" still a rule the product wants? — not a tidy-up to fold into a Goals redesign.
- **Desired future state.** Either a surface adopts it (the Reviews insights page is the obvious candidate), or the M3X hierarchy is restated without a Level 1 and the component, its styles and its docs are retired together.

## Debt raised by the 5 August 2026 end-to-end audit

> Added by the independent
> [End-to-End Audit — 5 August 2026](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md). Each entry
> carries its finding id (`AUDIT-NN`). The two P1 findings (AUDIT-01/02) are
> **product defects**, not debt, and live in
> [`ROADMAP_V2_1.md` → Immediate blockers](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#immediate-blockers--5-august-2026-end-to-end-audit)
> as AUDIT-FIX-01/02; the entries below are the debt those defects and the other
> findings created (testing, integrity, security, observability, documentation).
> The next free ID after this batch is **DEBT-89**.

### ☑ DEBT-79 — Asset permanent delete writes no audit event and destroys history — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-05**

- **Current issue.** [AUDIT-03] `d1-asset-repository.ts:842` `permanentlyDelete` runs a six-DELETE batch (`:930`) with **no `activities` insert**, hard-deleting `asset_events`/`asset_obligations` and removing `activity_subjects` pointers, so the workspace feed keeps no tombstone that the asset or its service/financial history ever existed. The Area purge (`d1-spine-repository.ts:1481`) does the opposite — a retained `area.deleted` event carrying `{areaId, title}`.
- **Impact.** Irreversible history loss with no audit trail that a destruction happened — weakens the "Activity is the audit trail" guarantee (AGENTS.md §17).
- **Desired future state.** The asset purge writes a guarded, subject-less `asset.deleted` tombstone with `{assetId, title}`, on the Area purge pattern.
- **Closing condition.** A kernel test counts exactly one `asset.deleted` event after a purge and asserts the workspace feed retains it.
- **Resolved (2026-08-05, AUDIT-FIX-03).** Reproduced first against real Workers/D1: purging an Asset with one history event, one obligation and three Activity rows removed every domain row, retained the three `activities` rows, removed all three subject pointers, and wrote **0** `asset.deleted` events — the type did not exist. The kernel now defines `ASSET_DELETED`, and `permanentlyDelete` appends one subject-less tombstone carrying `{assetId, title}`, inserted DIRECTLY after the `entities` DELETE and guarded on that statement's `changes()`, so a blocked, already-gone or race-losing purge appends nothing. The existing strengths — active-link precheck, commit-time guards on every destructive statement, child-first FK-safe ordering, typed `AssetStorageError`, idempotent already-gone result — are preserved unchanged. **Evidence.** Source: [`app/platform/storage/d1/d1-asset-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-asset-repository.ts) (`permanentlyDelete`, TEST-ONLY `deleteFault`), [`app/kernel/assets/asset-identifiers.ts`](../../app/kernel/assets/asset-identifiers.ts) (`ASSET_DELETED`), [`app/modules/assets/asset-activity.ts`](../../app/modules/assets/asset-activity.ts) + [`module.ts`](../../app/modules/assets/module.ts) (descriptor + manifest). Tests: five new real Workers/D1 cases in [`test/kernel/asset.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/asset.test.ts) — tombstone count/payload/subject-lessness with the workspace feed asserted to render it, second-purge idempotency, active-link blocking then release, a commit-time link race, and fault-injected rollback at both `after-entity` and `after-tombstone` — plus the shared invariants in [`test/kernel/permanent-delete-contract.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/permanent-delete-contract.test.ts).
