- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-03](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-03--asset-permanent-delete-writes-no-audit-event-and-destroys-history--p2). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-03--permanent-delete-integrity-asset--review-purge-p2).

### ☑ DEBT-80 — Review permanent delete: broken event ordering, non-idempotent — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-05**

- **Current issue.** [AUDIT-04] `d1-review-repository.ts:538` orders the append-activity statements before the deletes and lets the batch's own `deleteSubjects` remove the event's subject row; payload is `{}` (`:544`), so a surviving tombstone cannot name the review. This violates the recorder contract (`d1-activity-recorder.ts:44`, guard `WHERE changes() > 0`). A raced second purge fails on the subject-insert FK → `ReviewStorageError` instead of an idempotent `{deleted:false}`, and active links are silently destroyed.
- **Impact.** Unreliable/absent audit trail on review deletion; a concurrent double-delete errors instead of no-op'ing.
- **Desired future state.** Adopt the Area purge ordering + `{reviewId, title}` payload + idempotent second-purge + explicit active-link handling.
- **Closing condition.** Kernel tests assert tombstone presence, double-purge idempotency, and active-link behaviour.
- **Resolved (2026-08-05, AUDIT-FIX-03).** All five parts reproduced first against real Workers/D1: the tombstone was written with payload `{}`; its own subject row was deleted by the same batch; an ACTIVE link was silently destroyed; and two concurrent purges yielded one `{deleted:true}` plus `ReviewStorageError` ← `D1_ERROR: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed: SQLITE_CONSTRAINT`, while committing **two** tombstones for one destroyed Review. The purge is now delete-then-tombstone — `entity_links` → `activity_subjects` → `review_sections` → `review_details` → `entities` (`RETURNING`) → subject-less `review.deleted` with `{reviewId, title}` guarded on the entity DELETE's `changes()` — with the same "no active link" guard repeated on every destructive statement so the batch is all-or-nothing at commit. Active links now REFUSE the purge: `ReviewDeleteResult` gained `blockedReason: "has_links"` and `linkCount`, matching `AssetDeleteResult`; soft-deleted links do not block and are removed with the Review. Idempotency and race-safety come from the SQL guards, not from catching database errors — no `catch` suppresses a failure, and exactly one tombstone can exist per destroyed Review. **Evidence.** Source: [`app/platform/storage/d1/d1-review-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-review-repository.ts) (`permanentlyDelete`, TEST-ONLY `deleteFault`), [`app/kernel/reviews/review.ts`](../../app/kernel/reviews/review.ts) + [`review-repository.ts`](../../app/kernel/reviews/review-repository.ts) (result type + contract), [`app/modules/reviews/routes/mutate.tsx`](../../app/modules/reviews/routes/mutate.tsx) and [`ReviewRecord.tsx`](../../app/modules/reviews/ReviewRecord.tsx) (blocked-result handling and honest danger-zone copy), [`app/modules/reviews/review-activity.ts`](../../app/modules/reviews/review-activity.ts) (payload-driven tombstone descriptor). Tests: six new real Workers/D1 cases in [`test/kernel/review.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/review.test.ts) — successful-purge tombstone with feed rendering, second-purge idempotency, a genuine two-way concurrent purge asserting neither call rejects and exactly one tombstone lands, active-link blocking then release, soft-deleted links not blocking, and fault-injected rollback at both points — plus [`test/kernel/permanent-delete-contract.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/permanent-delete-contract.test.ts).
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-04](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-04--review-permanent-delete-nondeterministicempty-tombstone-non-idempotent--p2). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-03--permanent-delete-integrity-asset--review-purge-p2).

### ☑ DEBT-81 — No application-level CSRF defence — P2 — RESOLVED 2026-08-05

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-05] No `Origin`/`Sec-Fetch-Site`/`Referer` check existed on any mutation (`app/platform/request/request-boundary.ts:61` authenticated but did not check origin); no CSRF token was minted; there is no app session cookie — auth rides the Cloudflare Access `CF_Authorization` cookie whose SameSite is set by Cloudflare and asserted nowhere. ADR-016 reasoned only about token *forgery*, not session-riding CSRF.
- **Impact.** A compromised/XSS'd sibling subdomain of `daly.id.au` is same-site and could drive authenticated mutations; a future `SameSite=None` Access config would open plain cross-site CSRF.
- **Resolved (2026-08-05, AUDIT-FIX-04).** The absence was confirmed first: nothing in the request platform read either header. One canonical policy now runs ONCE at the shared boundary, so it covers every current and future mutation route without a single per-route check. `GET`/`HEAD`/`OPTIONS` are safe; everything else — including unknown and future methods, because the safe list is an allowlist — must carry an `Origin` that matches the request URL's own origin by exact scheme + hostname + effective port. There is no suffix match, no registrable-domain comparison and no wildcard subdomain, so **`same-site` is rejected as firmly as `cross-site`**: `https://other.daly.id.au` is same-*site* with `hub.daly.id.au` and is refused. `Sec-Fetch-Site` is read as corroboration only — `same-origin` or absent is accepted alongside an exact `Origin`, anything else is a rejection, and a hostile `Origin` claiming `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` is rejected as inconsistent. **Missing `Origin` fails closed** even when `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` is present: browsers attach `Origin` to every non-`GET`/`HEAD` request and DalyHub has no non-browser mutation client, so the compatibility case was deliberately not taken. The trusted origin is `new URL(request.url).origin` — authoritative because production disables `workers_dev`/`preview_urls`, so only the Access-protected custom domain routes here; no configuration value, no secret and no forwarding header (`X-Forwarded-Host`/`X-Forwarded-Proto`) is consulted. Ordering: authenticate → establish session → check provenance → reject, so a rejected mutation never provisions membership, never invokes React Router, never touches a repository, never records Activity and never mutates data; an unauthenticated request keeps its existing 401/403/503. The rejection is a generic `403 Request rejected.` with the baseline security headers, `private, no-store`, no reflected origin, no reason code, no CORS header and no redirect. `/health` keeps its exact-path public bypass for safe methods only. Offline replay is unaffected: it posts to the ordinary create routes with a plain same-origin `fetch`, so the browser supplies the provenance, and a 403 is classified `blocked` (not retried indefinitely). No migration, no token table, no cookie, no third-party dependency.
- **Evidence.** Source: [`app/platform/request/mutation-provenance.ts`](../../app/platform/request/mutation-provenance.ts) (the pure policy), [`request-boundary.ts`](../../app/platform/request/request-boundary.ts) (ordering + public-path method restriction), [`security-headers.ts`](../../app/platform/request/security-headers.ts) (`buildCrossOriginRejectionResponse`). Tests: 47 policy cases in [`test/unit/request/mutation-provenance.test.ts`](../../test/unit/request/mutation-provenance.test.ts); the eight boundary proofs in [`test/unit/request/request-boundary.test.ts`](../../test/unit/request/request-boundary.test.ts); real-runtime/real-D1 proof that a rejected mutation leaves no Task, no Activity and no membership row in [`test/kernel/request-boundary-csrf.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/request-boundary-csrf.test.ts); a real second local origin driving a genuine browser-generated `Origin` in [`e2e/csrf.spec.ts`](../../e2e/csrf.spec.ts); replay provenance in [`test/unit/pwa/offline-sync.test.ts`](../../test/unit/pwa/offline-sync.test.ts).
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-05](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-05--no-application-level-csrf-defence--p2). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-04--csrf-defence-in-depth--react-router-bump-p2p3).

### ☑ DEBT-82 — Multi-device preference lost-update; `version` never enforced — P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-08

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-07] `d1-app-preferences-repository.ts` `update` read the whole record, merged the caller's fields into that read, and upserted **every** column from the merged snapshot; `version = version + 1` was bookkeeping only — nothing compared it. Two devices saving different settings from reads of the same version each wrote the other's field back to its stale value, and both calls reported success. Confirmed on `main` before the fix and reproduced as the first test in the regression suite below.
- **Resolved (2026-08-08, AUDIT-FIX-06).** The write is now a **patch**, not a snapshot replacement: the statement's `DO UPDATE SET` names only the columns present in the validated patch, and its change predicate and version guard are evaluated **inside** that one statement, so nothing can be written between a check and the write. Two consequences, chosen deliberately because preferences are two different shapes of value:
  - **Independent fields merge.** A device saving `timezone` cannot touch `appearance`, so the common two-device case has no conflict to resolve and the owner is never asked about a disagreement that does not exist. This is the same per-column shape `updateTask` already used.
  - **A DERIVED value gets a real precondition.** The navigation hidden-set is read-modify-write (read the set, add an id, write the whole set) and therefore cannot merge by column. `update` accepts `options.expectedVersion`; supplying it appends `AND owner_app_preferences.version = ?` to the write, and a stale write matches zero rows and raises the new typed `AppPreferencesConflictError` instead of reporting success. **`version` is therefore no longer incremented-and-ignored** — it is the write precondition on the one path whose value is derived from the old one.
  - The Settings `toggle-navigation` action quotes the version it read and re-derives from the newer set on a conflict (bounded to 3 attempts), so the owner's toggle is never lost, never overwrites another device's toggle, and neither device is asked to resolve anything. A conflict that survives the retries answers **`409`**, never a `500`.
- **Also fixed on the way.** `parseTimezone` rejected `UTC` — `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')` lists canonical zones only and omits links — while the Settings picker offered it, so choosing UTC failed validation on save. The validator now falls through to the runtime probe, which is what every owner-calendar helper actually uses.
- **Evidence.** Source: [`d1-app-preferences-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-app-preferences-repository.ts), [`app-preferences.ts`](../../app/kernel/preferences/app-preferences.ts) (`UpdateAppPreferencesOptions`), [`app-preferences-errors.ts`](../../app/kernel/preferences/app-preferences-errors.ts) (`AppPreferencesConflictError`), [`settings/routes/index.tsx`](../../app/modules/settings/routes/index.tsx) (`withPreferenceRetry`, the `409`). Tests: 10 real-D1 cases in [`test/kernel/app-preferences-concurrency.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/app-preferences-concurrency.test.ts) — two stale devices keep BOTH changes, untouched columns are byte-identical, a same-field race resolves to the later write with the version advancing once per change, a stale compare-and-set is refused with the newer data intact, version 0 means "no row yet", an unchanged write is an idempotent no-op rather than a conflict, and a concurrency token from one workspace cannot authorise a write in another.
- **Remaining limitation.** Two devices changing the SAME scalar setting still resolve to the later write. That is the only honest answer for one field with two intentions, and nothing else is lost with it; the entry does not claim otherwise.
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-07](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-07--multi-device-preference-lost-update--p3). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-06](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-06--concurrency-and-one-owner-day-p3--delivered-2026-08-08).

### ☑ DEBT-83 — Concurrent note-content saves are blind last-write-wins — P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-08

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-08] `d1-note-details-repository.ts` overwrote the whole content guarded only against *identical* content; there was no base-version precondition, so two tabs or devices editing one note silently lost one side's writing. Extends [DEBT-47](#-debt-47--an-open-autosave-editor-does-not-adopt-a-server-side-change-to-its-field--p2--resolved-2026-08-20) from "does not adopt a change" to "destroys one". Offline note *editing* is not shipped (PWA-02 deferred), so the trigger was concurrent online editing.
- **Resolved (2026-08-08, AUDIT-FIX-06).** `NoteDetailsRepository.update` accepts `options.expectedContentUpdatedAt` — the content version the editor loaded — and folds it into the SAME upsert as a compare-and-set, so the check cannot be raced. A stale save matches zero rows and is reported as the typed `NoteDetailsConflictError`; the route answers **`409`** carrying the newer stored text, never a `500` and never a false success. `null` is a distinct quoted value meaning "this note had no saved content when I opened it", guarded as "the stored content is still empty" so a `setTags`/`setArchived` row creation cannot manufacture a false conflict. Callers that quote nothing (the capture panel writing a brand-new note's first body) keep the previous behaviour exactly, but a caller that quotes an UNPARSEABLE value is refused with `400` and writes nothing — degrading that to "no precondition" would have left a stale client an opt-out from the compare-and-set, and refusing costs the owner nothing because their draft never left the editor.
- **The editor's answer reuses the ONE existing reconciliation UI.** No Notes-only dialog and no automatic Markdown merge. A refusal is fed into the shared DS-06 coordinator through a new, honest third save outcome — `{ outcome: "conflict" }`, which is neither success (nothing was written) nor error (nothing malfunctioned) — so the draft stays in the editor, the status returns to `unsaved` rather than `error`, and the newer server text is OFFERED through the same [`RemoteChangeBanner`](../../app/shared/forms/RemoteChangeBanner.tsx) an out-of-band change already uses: *load the newer version*, or *keep mine*. The quoted base version is held until the owner answers, so a stray blur cannot quietly win in the meantime — and while the banner is up, a blur does not attempt a save that is certain to be refused.
- **Evidence.** Source: [`d1-note-details-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-note-details-repository.ts), [`note-details.ts`](../../app/kernel/notes/note-details.ts) (`UpdateNoteContentOptions`), [`notes/routes/mutate.tsx`](../../app/modules/notes/routes/mutate.tsx) (the `409`), [`autosave.ts`](../../app/shared/forms/autosave.ts) (`conflicted`), [`NoteContentForm.tsx`](../../app/modules/notes/NoteContentForm.tsx). Tests: 9 real-D1 repository + route cases in [`test/kernel/note-content-concurrency.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/note-content-concurrency.test.ts); 7 editor cases in [`test/unit/notes/NoteContentForm.test.tsx`](../../test/unit/notes/NoteContentForm.test.tsx); and a genuine two-writer browser journey in [`e2e/notes.spec.ts`](../../e2e/notes.spec.ts) where a second same-origin writer saves the note mid-edit — the banner appears, both versions survive, and reload-then-save recovers.
- **Remaining limitation.** Deliberately no revision history, no CRDT and no automatic prose merge: the owner chooses between two whole versions. Recovering the discarded side after choosing "load the newer version" is not offered — the draft is shown before the choice, never destroyed behind it.
- **Closing condition (met).** Kernel tests assert a stale content save is refused, not silently applied, and that the newer stored content is intact afterwards.
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-08](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-08--concurrent-note-content-saves-are-blind-last-write-wins--p3). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-06](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-06--concurrency-and-one-owner-day-p3--delivered-2026-08-08); [DEBT-47](#-debt-47--an-open-autosave-editor-does-not-adopt-a-server-side-change-to-its-field--p2--resolved-2026-08-20).
- **The Review section path was fixed first (2026-08-05) and is the precedent this copied.** `ReviewRepository.updateSection` was blind last-write-wins in exactly the same way. [REVIEW-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-02--weekly-review--delivered-2026-08-05) added an optional `expectedUpdatedAt` precondition: quoting it turns the write into a compare-and-set, a stale save raises `ReviewConflictError`, the route answers `409` with the newer text, and the owner's own words stay in the editor. A kernel test asserts a stale save is refused and the newer text survives; callers that quote no base version keep the previous behaviour, so nothing existing changed. Notes now follow the same shape, with two additions the Review path did not need: the base version is held until the owner answers, and the refusal is routed into the shared reconciliation banner rather than shown as a save error.

### ◐ DEBT-84 — Documentation drift: production state, theme count, identity status — P3 — DOCUMENTATION CORRECTED 2026-08-11; PRODUCTION STILL UNVERIFIED

- **Current issue, as raised.** [AUDIT-06 / AUDIT-09] `README.md` says "Current release … `2.0.1` … used daily" while `docs/development/DEPLOYMENT.md` in places reads "Next: the V2.0.1 hotfix" and fixes production at the `0001`–`0025` schema; migration-count prose drifts across `0025`/`0027`/`0028`; migrations `0026`–`0028` are not recorded as applied to production; AUDIT-IDENTITY-01 is "RESOLVED 2026-08-04" in this register but "carried forward as outstanding" in `ROADMAP_V2.md`'s closure log; and `help-content.ts` says "choose from the five [themes]" while seven ship.
- **Corrected 2026-08-11 by [AUDIT-FIX-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-05--documentation-truth-pass-p2p3--delivered-2026-08-11), inside HARDEN-01.** Every contradiction above is fixed, and the drift got worse before it was caught: by 2026-08-11 `DEPLOYMENT.md` stated the migration head as `0027` in one place and `0035` in another while the committed sequence had reached `0038`. It now states no number at all, because a repository cannot know what a database has applied — `pnpm run db:production:list` is the only honest answer, and the document says so. Help no longer claims "There is no AI in DalyHub yet" one topic below its own AI section, nor offers a choice of five themes in a product that has had no theme feature since M3-01.
- **One finding was worse than documentation drift.** Every document, the audit's own §19 checklist and `scripts/deploy-production.mjs` all asserted that `/health` is public and that a Cloudflare Access redirect from it is a misconfiguration. Measured on 2026-08-11 from an unauthenticated network, `GET https://hub.daly.id.au/health` returns **302 to the Access login** — because Access protects the whole hostname, which is precisely the origin hardening the deployment documentation exists to enforce. The deploy's post-upload health assertion therefore **could not have passed on any successful deployment**. It now distinguishes "the application answered and reports this release" from "Access answered, so the release is not verified from here" from "this is a real failure".
- **What is NOT closed, and why this entry is ◐ rather than ☑.** The closing condition has two halves: "no two canonical docs disagree" is met; **"production state is stated with evidence" is not.** No production verification was performed, because the implementation environment holds no Cloudflare credentials. The applied migration set, the Worker's secret names and the running release are all unknown. `pnpm run verify:production` performs the whole check in one read-only command and reports `SKIPPED` — never a pass — for anything it cannot reach.
- **Closing condition (unchanged).** No two canonical docs disagree about production state; production state is stated with evidence — i.e. an owner has run `pnpm run verify:production` with credentials and recorded the result.
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-06](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-06--production-state-documentation-drift-production-unverifiable-here--p2), [AUDIT-09](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-09--help-contradicts-the-shipped-theme-count--p3). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-05--documentation-truth-pass-p2p3--delivered-2026-08-11). **Record.** [HARDEN-01](HARDEN_01_RELEASE_RELIABILITY_2026_08.md).

### ☑ DEBT-85 — CSP has no `script-src`/`default-src` — P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-08

> **This entry was SPLIT on 2026-08-08.** It originally carried two unrelated
> audit findings, AUDIT-10 (CSP) and AUDIT-11 (the plaintext production dump).
> AUDIT-11 is resolved and now has its own entry,
> [DEBT-105](#-debt-105--the-scheduled-production-backup-uploaded-a-plaintext-copy-of-the-whole-database--p2p3--resolved-2026-08-08),
> so it can be marked closed without implying the CSP work is done. **The CSP
> concern below remains outstanding.**

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-10] `app/platform/request/security-headers.ts` set only `base-uri`/`frame-ancestors`/`object-src` — no `script-src`/`default-src`, so the Markdown sanitiser was the sole script-injection defence with no second layer.
- **Impact before resolution.** No defence-in-depth if an XSS sink were ever introduced.
- **Resolved (2026-08-08, with SET-03).** A complete, ENFORCING policy is built per response around a fresh 128-bit nonce, in one place — [`content-security-policy.ts`](../../app/platform/request/content-security-policy.ts) — and applied by the request boundary to every exit: served documents, data requests, the public `/health` path, 403 CSRF rejections and 401/403/503 authentication failures alike. Production carries `default-src 'self'`, `script-src 'self' 'nonce-…'` with **no** `'unsafe-inline'` and **no** `'unsafe-eval'`, `object-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `frame-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'self'`, `worker-src 'self'`, `manifest-src 'self'`, `connect-src 'self'`, `font-src 'self'`, `img-src 'self' data: https:` and `style-src 'self' 'nonce-…'`. The nonce reaches the render through the typed request context, so the header and the document can never name different values.
- **The one exception, stated.** `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'`, for React `style={{…}}` attributes. Confined to attributes, so an injected `<style>` ELEMENT is still refused; a style attribute cannot execute script. CodeMirror's runtime `<style>` injection is *nonced* through `EditorView.cspNonce` rather than exempted.
- **Development is separate and gated at BUILD time.** `resolveCspMode` returns the relaxed Vite policy only when `import.meta.env.DEV` (false in every production bundle) and an explicit development/test `ENVIRONMENT` agree, so a deployed bundle cannot be argued into it by an environment variable, header or hostname.
- **Proved, not asserted.** `test/unit/request/content-security-policy.test.ts` asserts each invariant individually (never a snapshot of the whole string). `test/unit/request/request-boundary.test.ts` proves the header's nonce is the one the render received, that it differs per request, and that rejection and failure responses carry the same policy. `e2e/csp.spec.ts` reads the header off the real production build and then, in a browser on the production origin, proves that policy blocks an injected inline `<script>`, an inline `onclick`, an unexpected external script source and a `javascript:` URL — while a nonced script still runs, so the suite cannot pass by blocking everything. The Markdown sanitiser is untouched and still strips all four first; CSP is the second, independent layer.
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-10](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-10--csp-has-no-script-srcdefault-src--p3--resolved-2026-08-08). **Related ADR.** [ADR-082](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-082-a-nonce-based-content-security-policy-one-header-authority-and-a-security-surface-that-refuses-to-overclaim). **Related roadmap item.** [SET-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-set-03--account--security--substantially-delivered-2026-08-08).

### ☑ DEBT-105 — The scheduled production backup uploaded a plaintext copy of the whole database — P2/P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-08

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-11] `.github/workflows/production-backup.yml` exported the entire production database daily to a GitHub Actions artifact as a plain `.sql` file with 30-day retention. Credentials were handled correctly and that was never the finding: the dump is all personal data — People and their contact details, Diary entries, Meeting notes, Reviews, archived and soft-deleted records — and its exposure scaled with who could read the repository's Actions. Raised as half of DEBT-85; split out on resolution so the CSP work stayed independently traceable — it has since been resolved too, by SET-03.
- **Resolved (2026-08-08, with SET-02).** The dump is now encrypted **on the runner, before it becomes an artifact**, with GnuPG symmetric AES-256 and explicitly stated S2K parameters (`--s2k-mode 3`, `--s2k-digest-algo SHA512`, `--s2k-count 65011712`). One pipeline, `scripts/production-backup.mjs`, serves the scheduled and the manually dispatched run identically — there is no weaker manual path.
- **What GitHub now stores.** `dalyhub-v2-production-<stamp>.sql.gpg` and a `metadata.json` of sizes, SHA-256 digests, run identity and the decrypt command. **No plaintext dump. No key.** The plaintext exists only in a scratch directory removed by a trap that runs even on failure, and a guard step refuses the upload if the artifact directory contains anything unencrypted or the metadata names a credential-shaped field.
- **The key.** `BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_PASSPHRASE`, in the protected `production` GitHub environment. It is read from a file written under `umask 077`, never from `argv` and never interpolated into a shell string; shell tracing is never enabled and no log line carries backup contents. It is not committed, not documented, not uploaded and not printed. The owner holds a copy **off GitHub**; rotation, and what happens to older backups, are documented in [`BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md` §6](../development/BACKUP_AND_RESTORE.md#6-the-recovery-key).
- **Recoverability is proved, not asserted.** Every run decrypts its own artifact back, compares SHA-256 with the original and asserts the plaintext does not appear in the ciphertext. `test/unit/deploy/production-backup-encryption.test.ts` does the same end to end with a real dump, a throwaway key and the real `gpg` invocation: encrypt → prove the sensitive strings are absent → decrypt → byte-identical → still passes structural validation; a wrong key fails; a tampered ciphertext fails. `test/unit/deploy/production-backup-workflow.test.ts` holds the workflow contract — upload contents, cleanup, no secret on a command line, the pre-export key guard, minimal permissions, `schedule`/`workflow_dispatch` only (no pull request can run it), and the deliberate retention.
- **Retention.** Unchanged at 30 days, and the reasoning is recorded beside the value: it was not reduced (encryption addressed the exposure) and not extended (encryption is not a reason to keep more data on GitHub for longer).
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-11](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-11--production-d1-dump-stored-as-a-github-artifact--p2p3). **Related roadmap item.** [SET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-set-02--backup--restore-v21).

### ☑ DEBT-86 — `react-router@8.0.0` carries a published advisory — P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-05

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-12] `pnpm audit --prod` flagged `react-router` `8.0.0` (GHSA-qwww-vcr4-c8h2, "RSC Mode CSRF Bypass", `>=7.12.0 <8.3.0`, patched `8.3.0`). DalyHub runs framework mode (not RSC), so the specific exploit likely did not apply, but it sat on a **direct production dependency** and paired with the CSRF gap (DEBT-81).
- **Resolved (2026-08-05, AUDIT-FIX-04).** `react-router` and `@react-router/dev` were bumped **together**, `8.0.0` → `8.3.0`, keeping the repository's exact-version pinning (`pnpm add --save-exact`); `package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` are the only dependency changes, and no unrelated package moved. `pnpm why` resolves ONE version of each — `react-router@8.3.0` (direct dependency, plus via `@react-router/dev@8.3.0` → `@react-router/node@8.3.0`) and `@react-router/dev@8.3.0`. **`pnpm audit --prod` → "No known vulnerabilities found"**, and GHSA-qwww-vcr4-c8h2 no longer appears anywhere in `pnpm audit --json`.
- **Advisory scope, stated accurately.** The official advisory scopes the flaw to the **unstable RSC code paths** ("Framework mode is not affected"): a follow-up to CVE-2026-22030 where action execution could occur before a 400 response. A codebase search found **no unstable RSC entry point or API in DalyHub** — no `react-router/unstable_rsc*`, no `unstable_rsc/*` virtual module, no `@vitejs/plugin-rsc`; the only `renderToReadableStream` is the ordinary framework-mode SSR entry from `react-dom/server` in `app/entry.server.tsx`. This entry therefore does **not** claim DalyHub was exploited, and does not claim the advisory applies to ordinary non-RSC actions. The upgrade was still required: the advisory sat on a direct production dependency, and removing it prevents future exposure if RSC APIs are ever adopted.
- **8.3.0 compatibility reviewed** (changelog, not just the advisory). The behavioural change of note is path-parameter encoding in `href`/`generatePath`, which now follows RFC 3986 path-segment rules so `$ & + , ; = : @` stay literal. DalyHub uses **neither** `href()` nor `generatePath()` from React Router (the one local `href` in `CollectionFilterChips.tsx` builds a query string), and every route parameter is an entity id — `crypto.randomUUID()`, hex and hyphens only. The changed encoding is therefore unreachable from DalyHub's route contract, so no speculative regression test was added; the existing navigation and deep-link coverage was run instead and is green.
- **Remaining advisories, recorded honestly.** Full `pnpm audit` (including dev dependencies) still reports **11 advisories — 6 high, 5 moderate — all dev-only and transitive**: `brace-expansion` (via `eslint` and `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` → `minimatch`), `sharp` and `undici` (via `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` → `miniflare`), and `postcss` (via `vite`). None is a production dependency; none was upgraded here, because unrelated dependency upgrades are out of this item's scope. `pnpm audit` is **not** clean, and this entry does not claim it is — only `pnpm audit --prod` is.
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-12](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-12--react-router-800-dependency-advisory--p3). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-04--csrf-defence-in-depth--react-router-bump-p2p3).

### ☑ DEBT-87 — Non-atomic cross-repository flows can leave inconsistent state — P3 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-08**

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-13] Meeting item→task conversion (`app/platform/meetings/follow-up-operations.ts`) and obligation→task completion (`d1-asset-history-repository.ts`, task completed in a separate transaction *before* the obligation batch) span two writes; a mid-way failure leaves an orphan Task, or a completed Task against a still-open obligation. Documented in the code, untested.
- **Resolved (2026-08-08, AUDIT-FIX-07).** Both are now ONE `D1Database.batch()`.
  - **Meeting item → Task** was five transactions with a compensating `spine.softDelete`. The compensation covered a *thrown* failure and could not cover the process not being there to run it, which is exactly the orphan the finding described. The conversion is now one transaction assembled from the Task, Meeting and EntityLink adapters' OWN prepared statements — no repository's SQL is re-authored — behind ONE domain operation, `MeetingTaskConversionRepository.convert`. A failure now leaves **no row at all**, not a soft-deleted one, so a retry is a first attempt. Idempotency is the `meeting_item_tasks (workspace_id, item_id)` unique index: a losing concurrent conversion rolls its whole transaction back and returns the winner's Task.
  - **Obligation → Task completion** closed the Task first. The Task's completion statements now join the obligation's own batch behind the `completionGuard` the obligation already used, evaluated *inside* the transaction, so the Task closes only if the obligation actually closed and any later failure rolls both back. The `catch { taskOutcome = "already_closed" }` that recorded every Task failure as an established fact in the Activity payload is gone: `taskOutcome` is derived from a real pre-read and names what the same transaction did.
  - **The APIs that permitted the old sequences are deleted**, not deprecated: `MeetingRepository.linkFollowUpTask` and `ObligationTaskGateway.completeTask`. Each was atomic on its own and useless as a guarantee, because the thing it recorded had been created by a different transaction.
  - `createTask` now accepts `status` and `description`, removing two further transactions rather than moving them.
- **Evidence.** 17 real-Workers/real-D1 cases in [`test/kernel/audit-13-atomic-operations.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/audit-13-atomic-operations.test.ts): success commits everything; a fault on EITHER side of each operation commits nothing (counted against **every** Task row, including soft-deleted ones, so "rolled back" is distinguishable from "compensated"); a retry after a failure yields exactly one Task, one mapping, one link and one event; concurrent requests cannot contradict each other; another workspace can neither convert nor complete; and the obligation event's `taskOutcome` matches committed state in all three cases. Plus rewritten fault-injection cases in [`meeting-follow-up.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/meeting-follow-up.test.ts) and a browser journey ([`e2e/audit-13-conversion-atomicity.spec.ts`](../../e2e/audit-13-conversion-atomicity.spec.ts)) proving a double-submitted, replayed and refused conversion each behave.
- **Remaining limitation, and one adjacent class left open.** Two things, both stated rather than hidden.

  **(a) Flows of the same shape that AUDIT-13 did not name are untouched**, because the finding is the finding: quick capture with a context (`createTask` + `applyCaptureRelationship`, compensating) and creating a Task for an obligation (`createTask` + `linkObligationTask` + `entityLinks.create`) are still compensating sagas with the same residual window. The AI acceptance paths' `createTask` + `updateTask({description})` pairs WERE folded in, since `createTask` now takes a description — that makes accepting an unsourced Task atomic outright. Making the remaining two atomic is [ADR-083](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-083-a-compound-domain-mutation-is-one-storage-transaction-composed-from-the-owning-repositories-statements)'s technique applied to two more flows, which is follow-up work and not a rider on an atomicity PR. Traced in [`DOC_EDITOR_ATOMIC_AUDIT_2026_08_08.md` §3.3](DOC_EDITOR_ATOMIC_AUDIT_2026_08_08.md).

  **(b)** ONE write stays outside the conversion's transaction: the AI acceptance path's `addItem`. Recording an action the owner approved is a legitimate change to the Meeting in its own right, `addItem` is atomic with its own ordinal-conflict retry, and an action item without a Task is a state the Meetings module already models and shows. A failed conversion after it leaves the action visible and no Task, and a retry REUSES that item rather than adding a second — no duplicate, nothing lost.
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-13](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-13--non-atomic-cross-repository-flows--p3). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-07--atomic-compound-mutations-one-long-form-editor-and-the-dead-code-p3--delivered-2026-08-08). **ADR.** [ADR-083](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-083-a-compound-domain-mutation-is-one-storage-transaction-composed-from-the-owning-repositories-statements).

### ☑ DEBT-88 — Two "owner today" definitions; latent parentless-task restore; dead/duplicate code — P3 — **FULLY RESOLVED 2026-08-08**

- **Original issue.** [AUDIT-14/15/16] (1) Task paths resolved the stored timezone (`tasks/routes/task-detail.tsx`) while asset history/obligations and the obligation→task gateway hard-coded `Australia/Sydney` (`app/shared/datetime/index.ts`), so a non-Sydney owner got day-shifted recurrence anchors/due-state between modules (adjacent to [DEBT-52](#-debt-52--three-copies-of-calendar-day-arithmetic-in-the-kernel-and-a-fourth-capture-surface--p3)). (2) `spine.restore` required an active structural parent, so a soft-deleted parentless Inbox task would throw `SpineParentUnavailableError` on restore — latent, as no task delete/restore UI exposes it. (3) Dead/duplicate code: `ModulePlaceholder.tsx` (superseded by `ModuleComingSoon.tsx`), `app/modules/notes/use-online-status.ts` (dup of the shared one), and two different `NewTaskForm.tsx` sharing a name (Projects vs Tasks).

**(1) AUDIT-14 — one owner day. RESOLVED 2026-08-08 (AUDIT-FIX-06).**

- The hard-coded default is **gone from the shared helpers**: `formatTodayDate`, `ownerCalendarIso` and `ownerCalendarDateResolver` now REQUIRE an explicit `timeZone`, so a call site can no longer answer "what day is it for the owner?" without consulting the owner, and the compiler finds every one that tries. The shared module's `OWNER_TIME_ZONE` export is removed rather than re-pointed, so nothing can mistake a constant for the answer.
- The authority is `WorkspaceScope.ownerTimeZone()` / `.ownerTodayIso()` — the owner's stored preference, read **once per resolved scope** and memoised, so one request has exactly one "today" no matter how many modules ask. The owner is the trusted actor, never a request value; a missing row or a read failure degrades to the ONE fallback constant, `DEFAULT_OWNER_TIME_ZONE` in `~/kernel/preferences`, which is also what `DEFAULT_APP_PREFERENCES.timezone` is defined as.
- Everything that was resolving Sydney now resolves the owner: the Assets collection, the Asset record and its history/obligation action, `D1AssetRepository.list`, `D1AssetHistoryRepository`'s meter-reading and obligation-completion default dates, and the obligation→task gateway's `ownerTodayIso`. The alignment window (`ownerZonedMidnightUtcIso` / `recentBoundaryStartIso` / `createOwnerAlignmentContext` / `evidenceDateLabel`) and `createOwnerHealthContext` take the zone as a required argument, and the three loaders that were omitting it now pass it. Client-side plan targets (`TaskPlanningSection`, the Today drawer's keyboard commands) take the SERVER-resolved `todayIso` instead of deriving a second answer in the browser.
- Eight identical copies of "read preferences, take `.timezone`, catch, fall back" across Projects, Tasks, Diary and People collapse into the one resolver, and People's own `resolveOwnerTimezone` duplicate is deleted.
- **UTC timestamps are untouched.** Only the CALENDAR reading of an instant is zoned; nothing that stores or compares instants changed.
- **Evidence.** Source: [`composition.ts`](../../app/platform/workspaces/composition.ts) (`ownerTimeZone`/`ownerTodayIso`), [`shared/datetime/index.ts`](../../app/shared/datetime/index.ts), [`d1-asset-history-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-asset-history-repository.ts), [`shared/alignment/window.ts`](../../app/shared/alignment/window.ts). Tests: 14 real-D1 cases in [`test/kernel/owner-today.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/owner-today.test.ts) — a Sydney owner and a Los Angeles owner resolve DIFFERENT dates from the same instant, Project health / Goal alignment / the scope agree for that instant, an obligation completion and a meter reading default to the owner's day (not Sydney's) under a fixed clock, the obligation→task gateway asks the scope, the resolver is memoised per scope and scoped per owner, and both a spring-forward and a fall-back leave calendar-date arithmetic exact.
- **Remaining limitation.** The gateway test proves the WIRING against the real clock (sampling the owner day either side of the call); the deterministic proof that the answer differs by zone is the fixed-clock repository test. `DIARY_DISPLAY_TIME_ZONE` survives as an alias of the one fallback constant, used only where no preference exists.

**(2) AUDIT-15 — parentless Inbox restore. RESOLVED 2026-08-08 (AUDIT-FIX-06).**

- `spine.restore` now admits the ONE spine kind whose parentage is optional. A **Task** satisfies the requirement either by having an active structural parent (unchanged) **or** by holding no live structural link at all — in which case it was an Inbox Task before the delete and returns to the Inbox. The alternative is evaluated in the SAME `UPDATE` as the restore, so a parent linked between the read and the write still gates it.
- No fake Project, no default parent, no Area attachment: the restored Task keeps zero `entity_links` and the workspace gains no new entity. Every other kind — and a Task that genuinely retains a parent link — keeps the original rule, still raising `SpineParentUnavailableError`.
- **Evidence.** Source: [`d1-spine-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-spine-repository.ts), [`spine-repository.ts`](../../app/kernel/spine/spine-repository.ts) (the contract). Tests: 8 real-D1 cases in [`test/kernel/inbox-task-lifecycle.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/inbox-task-lifecycle.test.ts) — the full create → soft-delete → restore lifecycle keeps the Task project-less with its title and planning fields intact, exactly one `entity.restored` event (none for the idempotent repeat), another workspace can neither see nor restore it, an unassigned-then-deleted Task restores to the Inbox, and a Task/Project/Goal whose retained parent is deleted is still refused.
- **Remaining limitation.** Still latent in the product: no Task delete/restore UI exposes this path, so the fix is proven at the kernel rather than in a browser journey.

**(3) AUDIT-16 — dead/duplicate code. RESOLVED 2026-08-08 (AUDIT-FIX-07).**

- Each candidate was classified before anything was deleted, because "looks unused" and "is dead" are different claims.
- **`app/shared/shell/ModulePlaceholder.tsx` — superseded, deleted.** `ModuleComingSoon.tsx` replaced it; no production route imported it, and the only reader was `test/unit/shell/AppShell.test.tsx`, which used it as "something with a Pane Header". `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` claimed `/tasks` still used it; it does not, and the sentence is corrected.
- **`app/modules/notes/use-online-status.ts` — duplicate, deleted.** Byte-identical to `app/shared/linked-items/use-online-status.ts`, whose own header says it was promoted FROM the Notes copy. `NoteContentForm` imports the shared one.
- **The two `NewTaskForm.tsx` — NOT duplicates, deliberately retained.** Both have real callers and different bodies for a reason: the Projects one posts a title alone to `/projects/:projectId/mutate` and the SERVER binds the parent, so a client cannot retarget the Task; the Tasks one posts a client-chosen parent to `/tasks`, re-verified there, plus six planning fields and quick-capture parsing. Same words, different trust boundaries. Deleting a live component to satisfy a naming finding would be the wrong fix, so the NAME was fixed: the Projects one is `NewProjectTaskForm`.
- **Also deleted, as obsolete by the same PR:** `MarkdownField` and `InlineMarkdownField` (DEBT-101 / DEBT-97), the test that only tested the first of them, the CSS both left behind, and the two mutation APIs that permitted the pre-AUDIT-13 non-atomic sequences.
- **Deliberately NOT swept.** No global CSS purge and no unrelated module cleanup. The kernel's open `EntityType` set stays open ([ADR-013](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) §4.6 — dormant by design, not dead).
- **Related finding.** [AUDIT-14](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-14--two-owner-today-definitions--p3), [AUDIT-15](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-15--soft-deleted-inbox-task-cannot-be-restored-latent--p3), [AUDIT-16](END_TO_END_AUDIT_2026_08_05.md#audit-16--deadduplicate-code--p3). **Related roadmap item.** [AUDIT-FIX-06](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-06--concurrency-and-one-owner-day-p3--delivered-2026-08-08) (§1, §2) and [AUDIT-FIX-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-audit-fix-07--atomic-compound-mutations-one-long-form-editor-and-the-dead-code-p3--delivered-2026-08-08) (§3); [DEBT-01](#-debt-01--duplicate-card-implementations-per-module--p2) family for the duplicates.

### ☐ DEBT-89 — Review Inbox announces "Task completed" for a REFUSED completion — P2

- **Current issue.** [`TasksReviewWorkspace.tsx:92-98`](../../app/modules/tasks/TasksReviewWorkspace.tsx) announces `"Task completed."` and revalidates as soon as the completion fetcher settles with **any** data, without reading the result. `/tasks/:taskId` genuinely refuses a completion — an archived Project (`TaskProjectArchivedError` / `SpineParentUnavailableError`) or any storage failure returns `{ kind: "completion", ok: false, message }`, and a Task deleted in another tab returns a 404 body. In those cases TASKS-04's Review Inbox tells the owner, and a screen reader through its `role="status"` region, that the Task completed when it did not.
- **Impact.** A false success announcement on the one surface whose whole purpose is deciding what has been dealt with — and it is announced to assistive tech, where there is no visible card state to contradict it. Directly against [AGENTS.md §15](../../AGENTS.md#15-accessibility-requirements) ("Announce change" must announce the real change) and the product's "report the server's answer, never the optimistic guess" rule that `TaskQuickEditPanel` already follows in the same file's own panel.
- **How it was found.** An automated review of [#117](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/117) caught the identical defect in the guided weekly Review's Inbox step, which had copied this pattern. **REVIEW-02 fixed its own copy** — the decision is now the pure, unit-tested `inboxCompletionOutcome` in [`review-guide-view.ts`](../../app/modules/reviews/guided/review-guide-view.ts), a refusal is shown as well as announced, and the Task stays in the queue for a retry. The TASKS-04 original was deliberately **not** changed there: it is a different module and a feature PR is the wrong place for a drive-by fix ([AGENTS.md §13](../../AGENTS.md#13-pull-request-standards)).
- **Desired future state.** Review Inbox reads the route's answer and announces it, reusing the same pure helper rather than a second copy of the rule.
- **Closing condition.** A test proves a refused completion is announced as the route's refusal message (not as a success) and does not revalidate the queue.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md); the worked precedent is [REVIEW-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-02--weekly-review--delivered-2026-08-05).

### ☑ DEBT-90 — AI-accepted Tasks are not recorded as meeting-item follow-ups — P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-05

- **What it was.** [`app/modules/ai/routes/apply.tsx`](../../app/modules/ai/routes/apply.tsx) created an accepted Task through the canonical `scope.tasks.createTask`, not through **MEET-02's** `meeting_items` → Task conversion authority. The Task was correct in every other respect — owner as actor, ordinary Activity, ordinary lifecycle — but no `meeting_item_tasks` row was written, so the Meeting's Follow-up tab did not show it as a converted item and quietly under-reported AI-originated follow-up work.
- **How it was fixed (AI-02).** The acceptance path is now [`app/modules/ai/apply-proposal.ts`](../../app/modules/ai/apply-proposal.ts). It resolves the proposal's source record server-side from its id, and for a Meeting calls `convertMeetingProposalToTask` in [`app/platform/meetings/follow-up-operations.ts`](../../app/platform/meetings/follow-up-operations.ts) — which creates or **reuses** the Meeting's `action` item and then converts it through the unchanged `convertMeetingItemToTask`. The mapping row and its structural Activity are written at MEET-02's own commit point.

  The orchestration moved from `app/modules/meetings/` to `app/platform/meetings/` in the same change: the module-boundary rule correctly forbids `app/modules/ai` importing another module's internals, and duplicating the orchestration would have created the second conversion path this entry is about. See [ADR-073 §73.1](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#731-addendum--the-acceptance-path-canonical-conversion-authority-proposed-notes-and-openai-application-state-storage-ai-02-2026-08-05).
- **Evidence.** [`test/kernel/ai-apply-proposal.test.ts`](../../test/kernel/ai-apply-proposal.test.ts), against real repositories and real D1 constraints, proves: the `meeting_item_tasks` mapping is written (asserting the zero-row before-state first); `meetings.listFollowUps` — the projection the Follow-up tab renders — reports it as converted with the correct `itemId`; a replayed acceptance returns the SAME Task with `created: false` and leaves exactly one mapping row, one Task and one action item; an item the owner had already written is reused rather than duplicated; and an already-converted item stays converted. `e2e/ai-assistance.spec.ts` proves the same through the real UI: the Follow-up tab moves from its empty state to `Open (1)` and the meeting item offers *Open task*, not *Create task*. Duplicate conversion is still refused by the partial unique index — **no uniqueness error is caught and ignored** anywhere on this path.
- **Related roadmap item.** [AI-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md) ☑ (2026-08-05). **Related:** [MEET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md).

### ☐ DEBT-91 — The Weekly Review assistant's fact block is narrower than the guided Review's own evaluators — P3

- **Current issue.** The assistant is fed facts DalyHub calculates rather than asking a model to count — which is the right design — but the bounded computation in [`app/modules/ai/review-facts.ts`](../../app/modules/ai/review-facts.ts) reports `0` for stalled Projects, Projects without a visible next action, Goal alignment and Diary counts instead of re-deriving PROJ-02 / AREA-03. The guided Review already computes several of these for the step the owner is on.
- **Impact.** Two computations of the same week can disagree, and the assistant's summary is the one that will be wrong — it will describe a quiet week because its inputs were zeroed, not because the week was quiet.
- **Desired future state.** The assistant reads the guided flow's existing bounded context projection instead of its own reduced one, so the two cannot disagree about the same period.
- **Closing condition.** A test proves the assistant's fact block matches the guided Review's own evaluators for the same Review period, including at least one non-zero stalled-Project case.
- **Related roadmap item.** Part of what [AI-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ai-03--planning--review-assistance) still owes; the evaluators are [REVIEW-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-02--weekly-review--delivered-2026-08-05)'s.

### ☐ DEBT-92 — Generated AI results are not persisted, so reuse is bounded to one isolate — P3

- **Current issue.** Validated results live in a small bounded in-memory store in [`app/platform/ai/ai-runtime.ts`](../../app/platform/ai/ai-runtime.ts). This is deliberate — `ai_usage_requests` has no column for a response body — but it means the request fingerprint's "you already ran this" reuse only holds within one isolate's lifetime.
- **Impact.** The same question asked again from another device, or after the isolate recycles, is paid for twice even though the fingerprint matches and the source records have not changed. The owner is charged for DalyHub's forgetfulness.
- **Desired future state.** A deliberate decision either way, with the cost written down: a saved-result table would become the largest store of AI-authored content in the product, and "no" is the conservative answer while there is no evidence that repeated identical requests are a real cost.
- **Closing condition.** Either usage data showing duplicate identical requests are material and a bounded saved-result store with its own retention, or a recorded decision not to build one.
- **Related roadmap item.** [AI-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ai-01--proposal-architecture--review-ui--delivered-2026-08-05).

### ☐ DEBT-93 — AI evidence retrieval is keyword and relationship only — P3

- **Current issue.** [`app/platform/ai/evidence-retrieval.ts`](../../app/platform/ai/evidence-retrieval.ts) selects evidence by term match over the existing search projections plus what is linked. There are no embeddings and no semantic search, so a question phrased entirely differently from the records it is about retrieves nothing.
- **Impact.** Ask DalyHub answers "the evidence doesn't support an answer" in cases where the workspace does in fact hold the answer under different words. That refusal is honest, and it is still a miss.
- **Desired future state.** Embeddings evaluated separately on their own merits — cost, storage, staleness, and whether Workers AI is justified — rather than adopted because a feature happens to involve AI.
- **Closing condition.** A recorded evaluation with a decision, or a bounded semantic-retrieval path measured against the checked-in evaluation corpus.
- **Related roadmap item.** [AI-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-ai-01--proposal-architecture--review-ui--delivered-2026-08-05); [SHARED_SEARCH.md](../development/SHARED_SEARCH.md) is the retrieval this composes.

### ☐ DEBT-94 — AI preferences are the one kind of owner configuration the export snapshot omits — P3

- **Current issue.** The X-04 snapshot carries the owner's configuration under `owner` — [`build-snapshot.ts:117`](../../app/platform/export/build-snapshot.ts) reads `owner_app_preferences` and the TASKS-03 saved views — but not `workspace_ai_preferences`, which is owner configuration of exactly the same kind (budgets, allowed features, privacy consent, logging choice). The usage ledger's exclusion is principled; this one is a judgement call that reads as an omission unless someone goes looking.
- **Impact.** An owner who exports, restores and finds their AI budgets, feature switches and privacy consent all back at defaults has lost settings the archive claimed to carry. The counter-argument is real — silently re-enabling spending and consent by restoring into a different environment is its own failure — which is precisely why it needs deciding rather than defaulting.
- **Desired future state.** A decision recorded either way: AI preferences included in the snapshot (with restore made explicit about spending and consent), or excluded with the reason stated in the export's own `limitations` block so the archive is honest about what it does not carry.
- **Closing condition.** Either the snapshot schema carries AI preferences with a restore test, or the export's `limitations` names the omission and a test asserts it is named.
- **Related roadmap item.** [X-04](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-x-04--export--data-portability) ☑ · [SET-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-set-02--backup--restore-v21) ☐, which is where it has to be answered.

### ☐ DEBT-95 — `AGENTS.md` §6 and §15 still describe the colour system the product no longer has — P2
- **Current issue.** Two changes have now overtaken the same two sentences. [ADR-075](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-075-the-appearance-preference-and-one-authority-for-routine-creation) gave the owner a three-value **Appearance** preference over the one generated light/dark pair, and [ADR-088](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-089-five-generated-colour-schemes-over-one-design-system--a-second-root-attribute-orthogonal-to-appearance) (THEME-01) grew that one pair into **five generated colour schemes**. §6 is now wrong twice over — it says the pair is "selected by the operating system", that colour is "generated from one seed", and that there is "no theme feature" — and §15 says "`prefers-color-scheme` is not merely honoured — it is the *only* thing that decides light or dark, because there is no theme preference to override it". §15 is the worse of the two, because it sits inside the accessibility requirements, which is the last place a reader should have to discount what they read.
- **Impact.** An agent reading the constitution — which §0 makes the highest authority — is told the product cannot do things it demonstrably does. The correct reading requires also knowing that ADR-075 and ADR-088 supersede it, which is exactly the indirection §0's supersession clause is meant to be a stopgap for, not a resting state. The risk compounds now that there are five schemes: an agent that believes there is one pair may write a module rule that is only correct in Daly Violet.
- **Desired future state.** §6 states that colour is generated per **colour scheme** (five of them, Daly Violet the default), that the owner chooses **System / Light / Dark** independently over whichever scheme is chosen, and that a scheme is a token map rather than a theme feature — no module rule branches on one. §15 keeps its requirement — honour the device — and says plainly that `System` is the default and is that honouring, while an explicit Light or Dark is a deliberate user override, which is user control rather than a failure to respect a device signal.
- **Closing condition.** A dedicated PR amending `AGENTS.md`, per the file's own closing rule ("change this file in a dedicated PR that explains the reasoning"). ADR-075's and ADR-088's supersession tables are then redundant and can say so.
- **Related roadmap item.** None — this is documentation owed by the shell-polish change and by THEME-01, deliberately deferred so a constitutional amendment is not folded into a feature PR.

### ☑ DEBT-96 — Two identical capture controls sit in the same corner on a phone — P3 — RESOLVED 2026-08-07
- **Current issue.** The phone bottom bar carries a **Capture** slot ([`BottomNav.tsx`](../../app/shared/shell/BottomNav.tsx)) and M3-01's floating action button ([`CaptureFab.tsx`](../../app/shared/shell/CaptureFab.tsx)) is shown at every width. Both are named "Capture", both open the same shared sheet with no context, and both sit at the bottom-right within a thumb's reach of each other. They do not overlap — the button is offset above the bar by `--app-bottomnav-height` plus the home inset, asserted at 320px in `e2e/creation-controls.spec.ts` — but the duplication is the same kind the shell-polish change removed from four pane headers, and the repository has already stated the principle: `e2e/mobile-capture-journeys.spec.ts` asserts Diary shows "exactly one in-page primary create action — no competing FAB", because "with a bottom bar carrying Capture, a FAB would be a second accent control in the same corner".
- **Impact.** Two controls with the same accessible name in one viewport is ambiguous to a screen-reader user browsing by control, and it spends the phone's scarcest space twice on one action.
- **Desired future state.** One global capture control per viewport: the floating action button above `md`, the bottom bar's Capture slot below it.
- **Closing condition.** The FAB is `display: none` below `md`; `today-mobile.spec.ts`'s FAB/bulk-bar regression assertion is re-sited to a width where the FAB exists; the mobile screenshot baselines are refreshed.
- **Resolution (CAPTURE-02, 2026-08-07).** All three, exactly as written. `.dh-fab` is `display: none` inside the same `max-width: 48rem` query that raises the bottom bar ([`shell.css`](../../app/styles/shell.css)) — `display: none` rather than a visibility hide, so the duplicate leaves the Tab order and the accessibility tree as well as the screen. The bar's slot opens the SAME shared sheet through the same callback and opener, so every capture type, the route behaviour and the focus restoration on close are untouched. `today-mobile.spec.ts`'s bulk-bar assertion is re-sited: the phone case now asserts the corner is unconditionally clear and Capture is still on the bar, while the suppression rule itself is asserted at 900px in [`e2e/global-capture.spec.ts`](../../e2e/global-capture.spec.ts) — the width where there is a floating button to suppress. `creation-controls.spec.ts`'s 320px assertion changed from "the button clears the bar" to "there is no button, and the bar has Capture".
- **Evidence.** [`e2e/global-capture.spec.ts`](../../e2e/global-capture.spec.ts) — at 320px, 400px and 700px: the floating button computes to `display: none`, the bar's Capture slot is visible and a ≥44px target, the sheet it opens offers all four record types, and Escape returns focus to the bar's control. Contextual creation (Diary's day-scoped header create) and empty-state creation are asserted present in the same file, because the rule removed a duplicate and nothing else.
- **Related roadmap item.** None — recorded in [ADR-075](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-075-the-appearance-preference-and-one-authority-for-routine-creation)'s alternatives as considered and deliberately deferred, because it rewrites mobile shell and screenshot specs for a judgement call that deserves its own change. Taken up by the August 2026 interaction audit's finding 1, which found the more serious half (the button covering content) in the same control.

### ☐ DEBT-99 — Thirty-eight module-level controls still hand-roll their own state layer — P3
- **Current issue.** M3-INT (PR #127) made `base.css` the one implementation of the M3 state layer and converted the reusable primitives to it: buttons, record actions, card actions, the overflow menu's trigger and items, the segmented control, the editor toolbar and its mode toggle, the inline-edit trigger and its block Edit control, the inline select's options, the account-menu links, the phone bar's controls and the command palette's close. What remains is module-level chrome that still writes `background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--md-sys-color-on-surface) 8%, transparent)` under its own `:hover` — thirty-eight rules across `activity-feed`, `assets`, `capture`, `card`, `diary`, `drawer`, `feedback`, `filters`, `forms`, `help`, `inspector`, `linked-items`, `meetings`, `people`, `search`, `settings`, `sheet`, `summary-cards` and `today`. They are enumerated exactly, as `file: selector`, in [`test/unit/tokens/state-layer.test.ts`](../../test/unit/tokens/state-layer.test.ts).
- **Why it was not done here.** The August 2026 audit's own instruction for finding 3 was "not convert all 107" but the reusable primitives, and that is what happened. Each survivor is a small local conversion with a small local payoff; doing thirty-eight of them inside a PR that also fixed the shared record header and the shared writing surface would have made all three unreviewable.
- **Impact.** Low and bounded, but real: each is a hover fill with no `:active` or `:focus-visible` sibling, so those controls have no pressed state and give keyboard users no state layer. That is the same shortfall the audit predicted, on a smaller set.
- **Desired future state.** Every interactive control in the product reaches its states through the one implementation, and the baseline list is empty.
- **Closing condition.** `KNOWN_HAND_ROLLED` in `state-layer.test.ts` is `[]`, with no rule relaxed to get there.
- **Guardrail already in place.** The list is a RATCHET, not a note: the test fails if it grows (a new bespoke layer) and fails if an entry goes stale (a converted one left behind). It may only shrink.
- **Related roadmap item.** M3-INT; [ADR-077](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) decision 1.

### ☐ DEBT-107 — Eleven recorded-not-fixed findings from the August 2026 UI quality audit — P3
- **Current issue.** The August 2026 UI quality audit ([`DALYHUB_UI_QUALITY_AUDIT_2026_08.md`](../design/DALYHUB_UI_QUALITY_AUDIT_2026_08.md)) fixed its Critical/High findings and the clear shared Mediums, and deliberately deferred eleven: the Person record's eight equal-weight quick actions (UIQ-011), the Goal "Open" status wording that reads as a button (UIQ-012), four view-switcher presentations across collections (UIQ-013), Reviews' primary-action placement (UIQ-014), phone-weight full-width actions on the desktop Task drawer (UIQ-015), the desktop capture dialog's drag handle (UIQ-016), the editor toolbar's missing scroll affordance (UIQ-017), Review Inbox's duplicated "Back to Tasks" (UIQ-018), Notes filter-row alignment (UIQ-019), Today's bespoke Waiting preview rows (UIQ-020), and the shared overflow menu's missing viewport clamp/flip (UIQ-021).
- **Why it was not done here.** Each is either a product decision the owner should make (which two Person actions lead; whether a Goal is "Open" or "Active"), a convergence that touches every module at once (view switchers), or a behavioural change to a tested shared contract (DS-12 menu placement) that deserves its own acceptance pass. The audit's brief was explicit: record rather than let the PR become endless.
- **Impact.** Medium-to-low per item; none blocks an interaction. The full severity, root-cause and recommended-fix detail for every item lives in the register — this entry exists so the debt inventory stays complete, not to restate it.
- **Desired future state.** Each register row either fixed or explicitly accepted by the owner.
- **Closing condition.** The register's "Recorded, deliberately not fixed here" table is empty or every remaining row carries an owner decision.
- **Related roadmap item.** The audit register; DS-12/DS-16.

### ☐ DEBT-102 — DalyHub has no capture-processing state, so "unprocessed captures" cannot be shown — P3
- **Current issue.** The Today redesign's rail contract asks for a row reading "{n} unprocessed captures", highest position in the rail. The data model has no such state: the shared capture sheet writes a real Task, Note, Meeting or Diary entry straight into its module, and none of them carries a `processed` flag or an inbox membership. The nearest true concept is DalyHub's **Inbox** — the `inbox` system view, "an open task with no Area or Project above it" ([`d1-task-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-task-repository.ts)) — which is a FILING state, not a processing one. The rail therefore renders "Inbox · {n} unfiled tasks" and links to `/tasks?system=inbox`.
- **Impact.** Small and, for now, in the owner's favour: the row is true. What is missing is the wider concept — a captured Note or Diary entry is never surfaced for triage at all, because only Tasks have a filing state to be absent from.
- **Why it was not built here.** Adding a processing flag is a kernel change with a migration, an Activity verb, an export field and a search decision behind it, and the redesign brief explicitly rules out new backend aggregation. Inventing the label without the state would have been worse than the gap: the screen would claim a workflow the product does not have.
- **Desired future state.** Either a deliberate decision that filing IS processing in DalyHub (and the row keeps its current, honest wording), or a real capture-triage concept spanning every captured entity.
- **Closing condition.** A dated decision either way, referenced from `PRODUCT_PRINCIPLES.md` → the entities.
- **Related roadmap item.** [UX-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-ux-01--daily-driver-ux-ui-and-product-polish).

### ☐ DEBT-103 — The workspace-wide Activity feed endpoint has no UI consumer — P3
- **Current issue.** [`/today/activity`](../../app/modules/today/routes/activity.tsx) is the product's only reader of the workspace-wide FND-05 Activity stream, and its only consumer was Today's "Recent activity" widget. The Today redesign removed that widget: the layout contract is the day and the attention rail, and a scrolling history of everything that changed is neither. The endpoint, its descriptor map ([`landing/activity.ts`](../../app/modules/today/landing/activity.ts)) and its kernel tests are deliberately KEPT — deleting a tested platform capability to tidy up after a UI change would be the wrong trade — but nothing renders them today.
- **Impact.** None on screen. The cost is a real capability with no way in: workspace-wide history is currently only reachable per record, through each record's own Activity tab.
- **Desired future state.** A surface that owns cross-module history — an Activity screen, or a Review step — consuming this endpoint through the shared DS-05 feed. Not a widget wedged back onto Today.
- **Closing condition.** A product route renders `/today/activity`, or the endpoint and its descriptors are removed together with a recorded decision that workspace-wide history is per-record only.
- **Related roadmap item.** [REVIEW-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md).

### ☐ DEBT-104 — `/today/plan` survives with no caller — P3
- **Current issue.** The bulk planning action route ([`routes/plan.tsx`](../../app/modules/today/routes/plan.tsx)) was posted to by Today's multi-select bulk bar. The redesign replaced the roving multi-select collection with plain rows — a checkbox completes, a title opens the record — so nothing calls it. Per-task planning is unaffected: it goes through the re-homed `/tasks/:taskId` action, which the Task Drawer and its keyboard commands still use, and bulk planning still exists in the Tasks module.
- **Impact.** None on screen. The cost is the same trap as any unreferenced shared surface: it is discoverable, tested and stale, so the next agent may build against it rather than against the Tasks module's own bulk path.
- **Desired future state.** One bulk planning path, owned by Tasks, with `/today/plan` either retired or re-pointed.
- **Closing condition.** `grep -rn "today/plan" app/` finds only the route file itself and its manifest entry, and a dated decision names the Tasks bulk route as the single bulk planning path.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-03](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md#-tasks-03--the-tasks-collection).

### ☐ DEBT-100 — A combobox closed with Escape does not reopen on click — P3
- **Current issue.** `SelectField` opens its listbox on FOCUS. After Escape closes it the input is still focused, so clicking the control fires no focus event and nothing happens; the only way to reopen it is ArrowDown or Tabbing away and back. Discovered while asserting the PR #124 selection behaviour survived the Settings migration ([`e2e/interaction-consistency.spec.ts`](../../e2e/interaction-consistency.spec.ts)), which now documents the behaviour where it works around it.
- **Why it was not done here.** It is PR #124's interaction model, not something the M3-INT migration introduced, and changing when a shared combobox opens touches every consumer's keyboard and pointer behaviour — including the anchored inline select and the entity-link picker. That deserves its own change and its own acceptance pass rather than a same-day patch inside a styling PR.
- **Impact.** Low. A user who presses Escape and then clicks the box expects it to open; today they must click twice (blur, then focus) or press ArrowDown.
- **Desired future state.** A pointer press on a closed combobox opens it, whether or not it already had focus, without re-opening it on the press that CLOSES it.
- **Closing condition.** An E2E assertion that Escape-then-click reopens the list, on `SelectField` and on the inline select.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-16 / [ADR-076](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) decision 10.

---

### ☐ DEBT-109 — Ordinal monthly recurrence ("first Monday of every month") is not expressible — P3

- **Current issue.** The typed recurrence model represents a monthly rule as an ANCHOR DAY (`anchor_day`, 1–31, with documented clamping into short months), which cannot express "the first Monday" or "the last Friday". TASKS-07 investigated adding it and deferred it deliberately ([ADR-085](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-085-the-tasks-daily-driver--the-matrix-removed-editing-moved-onto-the-row-bulk-made-structural-and-recurrence-given-a-second-scheduling-mode) decision 11).
- **Impact.** An owner whose routine genuinely is "first Monday of the month" must approximate it with a date and correct it by hand, or use a 4-week interval that drifts against the calendar. It is a real gap, and a narrow one.
- **Why it was not built.** It needs a SECOND monthly representation (an ordinal 1–5/last plus a weekday) beside the existing one, and every consumer would have to carry both: `validateTaskRecurrenceRule`, `nextTaskOccurrenceDate`, `taskRecurrenceLabel`, `recurrence-authoring`, the editor's controls and the quick-capture grammar. Bolting it onto `anchor_day` — overloading the column, or inferring an ordinal from a stored date — is how a sound typed model turns into a generic expression engine, which is exactly what the programme's brief refused.
- **Desired future state.** An additive `anchor_ordinal` (1–5 or `last`) + `anchor_weekday` pair on `task_recurrence_rules`, valid ONLY for `frequency = 'month'`, mutually exclusive with `anchor_day`, with the label reading "First Monday of every month" and the editor offering "on the … " beside the existing "on the 15th".
- **Closing condition.** A rule authored as "last Friday of every month" survives a complete/reopen/complete cycle, clamps correctly in a month with four Fridays and one with five, and reads identically in the editor, on the row, in the Drawer and on Today.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-07](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md#-tasks-07--recurrence-20--delivered-2026-08-08) (follow-on).

### ☑ DEBT-110 — Bulk operations are bounded at 100 tasks with no surface that says so — P3 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-09**

- **Original issue.** Every bulk mutation validates its id list through `validateTaskIdList`, which caps at `MAX_PLAN_BATCH_SIZE` (100) — deliberately, so one transaction stays small and predictable. The bulk bar's "Select all" only ever selects what is VISIBLE (one loaded page), so the cap is not reachable in ordinary use, but nothing on the surface stated either fact. An owner who loads several pages and then selects everything would meet a typed validation error rather than a bound they had been told about.
- **Impact.** Small and latent: a refusal that is correct but unexplained, on the one operation where the owner is acting on many records at once.
- **What was fixed.** The bound is now stated BEFORE the action, and the rule is pure and tested rather than inline in the bar's JSX ([`task-selection.ts`](../../app/modules/tasks/task-selection.ts) — `boundBulkSelection` and `bulkSelectionOverBy`, covered by [`test/unit/tasks/task-selection.test.ts`](../../test/unit/tasks/task-selection.test.ts)):
  - **"Select all" is capped at the bound** and says what it will take ("Select all 100"), with a line beside it stating how many rows are loaded and why the offer stops there. It can no longer build a selection whose every action is guaranteed to be refused.
  - **A selection past the bound** — now reachable only by Shift-ranging across more than one loaded page — replaces the toolbar with the bound and the remedy ("Deselect 37 to continue"), instead of offering eleven controls that each end in the same typed validation error.
- **Resolved by TASKS-10.** The owed E2E case now generates an isolated block of
  105 tasks through the canonical `/tasks/new` route, loads more than one page in
  the real Tasks UI, and proves both bound surfaces: "Select all" is capped at 100,
  and a Shift-range selection of 101 rows replaces the toolbar with "Deselect 1 to
  continue." See [`e2e/tasks-v22-daily-driver.spec.ts`](../../e2e/tasks-v22-daily-driver.spec.ts).
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-10](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md#-tasks-10---daily-driver-verification-and-capture-polish--delivered-2026-08-09).

### ☑ DEBT-111 — Today lost its Assets section, so an obligation with no linked Task reaches the owner nowhere outside the Assets module — P2 — RESOLVED 2026-08-09

- **Status: raised 2026-08-09 by the [E2E regression audit](E2E_REGRESSION_AUDIT_2026_08_09.md).** Found by five `assets-ownership.spec.ts` journeys failing on `getByRole("list", { name: "Assets needing attention" })`, which had been reproducing on `main` since the Today redesign and were part of the [DEBT-106](#-debt-106--mains-e2e-suite-is-broadly-red-36-journeys-across-19-spec-files-fail-for-reasons-unrelated-to-the-change-that-found-them--p1) census with no cause attached.
- **Current issue.** [ADR-063 decision 10](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) and [`ASSETS_MODULE.md` §2e](../development/ASSETS_MODULE.md) both describe a Today **Assets** widget with a stated deduplication rule. The Today redesign ([#131](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/pull/131)) rebuilt the screen and its attention rail carries four kinds — `inbox`, `waiting`, `project`, `goal` — and no asset. `grep -rn "asset-today" app/` now finds only the kernel module and its own barrel re-export: **`app/kernel/assets/asset-today.ts` has no consumer.**
- **Impact.** The kernel rule is intact and the Assets module's own surfaces are unaffected, so nothing is wrong with the DATA. What is lost is reach: ADR-063 decision 8 says the linked **Task** is the thing that appears in Tasks and Today, and decision 10 says the **obligation** appears on Today when it has no open Task. The second half no longer happens. An owner who schedules "renew the registration" and never makes a Task for it will not be reminded anywhere they habitually look. That is precisely the gap [DEBT-57](#-debt-57--asset-obligations-are-tracked-but-nothing-reaches-the-owner-outside-the-app--p2--resolved-2026-08-16-notify-01) says the product has *outside* the app, now reproduced inside it.
- **Why the E2E audit did not simply restore it.** Rebuilding a section of a screen that was deliberately redesigned is product work, not test repair, and the redesign's whole thesis was "a place to work, not a dashboard about work" — the rail's four kinds and its five-row cap are a stated design, and adding a fifth kind to it is a decision for whoever owns Today, not a side effect of getting CI green ([AGENTS.md §13](../../AGENTS.md#13-pull-request-standards)). The audit updated the affected journeys to assert the obligation where the product genuinely surfaces it today (the collection's obligation signal) and raised this entry instead. Nothing was deleted: the kernel module, its tests and the documented rule all stand, so restoring it is a rail row and a loader call rather than a rebuild.
- **Desired future state.** Either an `asset` kind on the Today attention rail, honouring the existing kernel deduplication rule and the rail's cap — or a dated decision that Today does not carry Assets, with ADR-063 decision 10, `ASSETS_MODULE.md` §2e and `asset-today.ts` retired together.
- **Closing condition.** `grep -rn "asset-today" app/` finds a product consumer and an E2E journey asserts an obligation with no linked Task reaching Today — or all three documents above say Today does not carry Assets and the dead kernel module is gone.
- **Resolved by TODAY-09.** Today now consumes the Assets Today kernel rule in
  [`app/modules/today/day/load.ts`](../../app/modules/today/day/load.ts) and renders
