Today's focus bands, a Project record's task list and global search results still render the generic `Card` under the pre-DS-04 `.dh-collection--tasks .dh-card__*` override layer in [`tasks.css`](../../app/styles/tasks.css). A task therefore has two visual anatomies depending on which screen you are looking at, and a person who plans in Today and executes in Tasks sees both within one minute.
- **Why it was not "fixed".** Deliberate, and it is the DS-04 brief's own instruction for a shared surface: wiring the row into one module is what makes a cross-module regression impossible by construction rather than by inspection. Converting Today and Projects in the same change would have made a bounded Tasks redesign into an eleven-module one, in a PR nobody could review for either.
- **Impact.** The product reads as two design generations at once on the two screens the owner uses most.
- **Desired future state.** DS-05 (Projects/Areas/Goals) and DS-06 (Today) adopt `TaskRow`, and the `.dh-collection--tasks .dh-card__*` layer is deleted with the last consumer.
- **Closing condition.** No product surface renders a task through `~/shared/card`, and the UIX-01 task-card override block in `tasks.css` is gone.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DS-05](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md), [DS-06](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md). **ADR.** [ADR-095](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-095-the-task-row--a-product-component-over-the-generic-card-one-column-grid-and-container-queries-as-the-responsive-authority-for-a-list).

### ☐ DEBT-129 — The row's date editor is still a Save/Cancel form in a popover — P3
- **Current issue.** `InlineDateField` ([`app/shared/inline-edit/InlineDateField.tsx`](../../app/shared/inline-edit/InlineDateField.tsx)) opens a ~200px popover containing three shortcut buttons, a native `<input type="date">` and a Save/Clear/Cancel row. Every other inline editor on a task row commits on selection; the date editor is the one that still asks the owner to confirm, and the native input is the tallest control in the list's whole interaction surface.
- **Why it was not "fixed".** DS-04's date work was scoped to the interaction being reachable, unclipped and not a modal, which it is. Replacing the native input with a calendar grid is a new shared component with its own keyboard model, its own localisation questions and its own accessibility surface — not a styling change, and not something to land inside a visual convergence pass.
- **Impact.** Setting a due date is three interactions where setting a priority is two, on the field a planning session touches most.
- **Desired future state.** The one-press shortcuts commit immediately (as the menu options do), and an explicit date needs Save only when a date is typed rather than chosen.
- **Closing condition.** Choosing "Today", "Tomorrow" or "Next week" from a row writes without a second press, proven by `e2e/tasks-collection.spec.ts`.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-05 or a later editing pass. **ADR.** [ADR-095](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-095-the-task-row--a-product-component-over-the-generic-card-one-column-grid-and-container-queries-as-the-responsive-authority-for-a-list).

### ☐ DEBT-130 — The Task Drawer's header states the word "Task" three times — P3
- **Current issue.** The drawer panel's own header renders "Task" and "Task record" above a `RecordLayout` whose title is preceded by the shared task `EntityIcon`. DS-04 de-carded and neutralised the panel but did not touch its header composition, so the first 100px of the panel says the same word three times before the task's own title.
- **Why it was not "fixed."** `drawer__header` is the shared drawer's, used by every record type; changing what it publishes is a shared-shell decision rather than a Tasks one.
- **Impact.** Cosmetic, on the surface DS-04 otherwise brought closest to the concept.
- **Desired future state.** A record hosted in the drawer publishes its type once.
- **Closing condition.** Opening a task shows the word "Task" at most once above the title.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-05.

### ☑ DEBT-131 — A task row is 46px because the completion control is 45px, and nothing else decides it — **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (FINAL-UI)**
- **What it was.** MEASURED across the DS-04 baseline and final captures at 1440: the row pitch was 46px in both. `--dh-row-height` at `compact` resolved to `max(2.75rem, 45px)` and the shared `.dh-check-circle-target` inside the row was sized to the same floor, so the row could not be shorter than the control at its leading edge. DS-04 removed the block padding that sat on top of that floor; it did not, and could not, move the floor.
- **The decision, which is the one this entry asked for.** [ADR-096](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) decision 3 amends D18. The 45px figure is WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.5 (AAA), which is a *touch* guideline, and it was being spent on a cursor. Under `(pointer: fine)` the completion target is now 45×28 and `--dh-row-height` at `compact` is 36px; under `(pointer: coarse)` every pixel is restored unconditionally, in the block that has always done so. SC 2.5.8 (AA, 24×24) is cleared on both axes with a third to spare.
- **The objection this entry raised, answered.** "A task list IS touched by a finger where the rail never is" — and on a finger it still gets the full AAA target. What changed is that a mouse no longer pays for it. The specs that assert 44px at phone WIDTHS run under a fine pointer and were the reason this could not be a quiet change; they pass, because the row's *hit area* is not what they measure.
- **Measured after.** 56px → 37px at 1440 (`docs/design/assets/final-product-ui/baseline` vs `final`). About nine more rows on a 1440×950 fold.
- **Related roadmap item.** FINAL-UI (the concept convergence).

### ☐ DEBT-132 — 1,471 feature-CSS references still speak MD3's typescale, shape, motion and elevation vocabulary — P3
- **Current issue.** DS-08 migrated every MD3 colour role that has an exact 1:1 `--dh-*` alias — 1,269 references across 68 stylesheets, verified pixel-identical (0 differing pixels of 2,301,120 on a full-page Today capture before and after). What remains in `app/styles/*.css` is 983 `--md-sys-typescale-*`, 310 `--md-sys-shape-*`, 157 `--md-sys-motion-*` and 21 `--md-sys-elevation-*`.
- **Impact.** A feature stylesheet still asks for a SIZE where the product publishes a ROLE, so "what size is a card title?" is answered in sixty places rather than one, and the DS-01 promise that `--dh-*` is the public design API is only true of colour.
- **Why it was not done in DS-08.** The mapping is many-to-one and semantic, not mechanical. DalyHub publishes seven type roles (`page-title`, `record-title`, `section-title`, `body`, `row`, `meta`, `label`) over a scale where three different things are `body-medium` for three different reasons — so choosing the role is a judgement per call site. Substituting mechanically would either pick wrong or erase the distinction the semantic layer exists to draw. [§58](../design/DS_05_08_WHOLE_APP_VISUAL_COMPLETION_2026_08.md) of the DS brief rules out exactly that kind of speculative change.
- **Desired future state.** Feature CSS names the ROLE. `--md-sys-typescale-*` survives only inside `tokens.css`, under the `--dh-text-*` layer.
- **Closing condition.** `grep -c "var(--md-sys-typescale" app/styles/*.css` excluding `tokens.css` reads 0, and `dalyhub-tokens.test.ts` asserts it.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-08 (token finalisation).

### ☐ DEBT-133 — `--md-sys-color-primary` is used 190 times where the product means either "accent" or "focus" — P3
- **Current issue.** The DalyHub layer deliberately publishes TWO names over one M3 role: `--dh-color-accent` (a brand decision) and `--dh-color-focus` (an accessibility contract that "must survive a rebrand", per `tokens.css`). 190 feature-CSS references still use the raw role, so which of the two a rule means can only be read from its property.
- **Impact.** A future rebrand that moves the accent would silently move every focus indicator with it — which is precisely the coupling the two names were introduced to prevent.
- **Why it was not done in DS-08.** The colour migration was restricted to tokens with exactly one `--dh-*` alias, because that subset is provably value-preserving and needs no judgement. This one needs judgement at every site, and getting it wrong is invisible until a rebrand.
- **Desired future state.** Every one of the 190 says `--dh-color-accent` or `--dh-color-focus`.
- **Closing condition.** `grep -c "var(--md-sys-color-primary)" app/styles/*.css` excluding `tokens.css` reads 0.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-08 (token finalisation).

### ☐ DEBT-134 — `--md-sys-color-outline-variant` is a within-card separator the DalyHub layer does not name — P3
- **Current issue.** 173 references, and no `--dh-*` equivalent. The product publishes `--dh-color-border` (separates) and `--dh-color-border-strong` (identifies a control, 3:1). `outline-variant` is used as a third thing: the rule BETWEEN rows inside a card, a step stronger than the card's own edge.
- **Impact.** A real gap in the semantic layer rather than an unmigrated consumer — the DS-08 pass could not migrate it because there is nothing to migrate it to.
- **Desired future state.** Either a third published name (`--dh-color-border-inset`, or similar) with the split argued, or a decision that these sites should have been `--dh-color-border` all along.
- **Closing condition.** The token exists in `tokens.css` and `dalyhub.ts`, or the 173 sites have moved to `--dh-color-border`.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-08 (token finalisation).

### ☑ DEBT-135 — Nine E2E journeys are red at `de4f5d1` (DS-04), independently of DS-05…DS-08 — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 (HARDEN-05)**
- **Current issue.** Each was verified by checking out **`de4f5d1`** — the DS-04 merge, and the actual base of the DS-05…DS-08 branch — and re-running it there. Nine fail on that tree:
  - `collection-header.spec.ts:404/412/435/457` — the shared menu's viewport-fit journeys (UIQ-021).
  - `areas.spec.ts:180` — the Areas collection's icon/count/work-state assertions.
  - `diary.spec.ts:67` — Diary's day-mode axe pass, reporting `target-size` on `.dh-btn--secondary` and `.dh-feedback__dismiss-all` inside the FEEDBACK surface.
  - `today-mobile.spec.ts:186/208/225` — the Tasks swipe tray. These are **stale from DS-04 specifically**: they locate `.dh-card[data-card-id="t-drawer"]`, and [DS-04 §5](../design/DS_04_TASKS_REDESIGN_2026_08.md) records that it "did not keep the swipe tray — it went with the Card". The three assert an object DS-04 deliberately deleted, so they are testing a removed feature rather than reporting a regression.
- **A methodology note, because it nearly produced a wrong answer.** The first attempt verified by `git stash`-ing the change — on a tree where everything was already committed, so nothing was stashed and the "baseline" run was the branch itself. The second attempt used local `main`, which was stale at `d4592d5` (DS-03) and where the swipe-tray tests still PASS because the Card had not yet been removed. Only the third — an explicit checkout of the branch's own parent — answers the question. **Compare against the branch's base commit, not against a branch name.**
- **Impact.** A red baseline makes "did my change break this?" cost a checkout-and-rerun every time, which is what it cost this pass three times over.
- **Desired future state.** The three swipe-tray journeys are deleted or rewritten against `TaskRow` (DS-04 owns that decision); the other six are fixed or each recorded with a dated reason it is expected to fail.
- **Closing condition.** The nine pass at `main`'s tip, or the register names them individually with a reason.
- **Met 2026-08-17 by [HARDEN-05](HARDEN_05_GREEN_MAIN_2026_08.md): all nine pass at `main`'s tip.** The four `collection-header` UIQ-021 journeys were failing on their own PRECONDITION rather than on placement — `openRowMenuNear` asked for `article.dh-card`, which DS-04 retired — so they had been measuring nothing. `areas.spec.ts:180` and `diary.spec.ts:67` pass. And the decision this entry asked DS-04 to make about the three `today-mobile` swipe-tray journeys is made: they are **rewritten against `TaskRow`**, not deleted. DS-04 §5 replaced the tray with the row's two committing gestures, so the journeys assert those on the same surface, including an axe scan taken with the finger still down — which is the only state in which the row draws the affordance at all.
- **Related roadmap item.** The E2E reliability programme (see DEBT-41's lineage).

### ☐ DEBT-136 — Four controls sit below DalyHub's 44px touch floor, each for a documented reason — P3
- **Current issue.** With `scripts/ds-final-audit.mjs` measuring the EFFECTIVE target against the declared 44px floor, seven findings remain at 390px and they are three distinct controls: `.dh-inline-edit__trigger` (24×24, four instances per task row), `.dh-taskrow__title` and `.dh-today__goal-title` (20px tall inline links whose target is the grid cell around them).
- **Impact.** None that is a WCAG 2.2 AA failure — all clear the 24px minimum, and [D43](../design/DALYHUB_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md#5-documented-departures-from-stock-material) settled the inline triggers deliberately. The gap is between the standard and DalyHub's own stricter promise.
- **Desired future state.** Either the row's inline editors take the 44px floor on a coarse pointer (which is [DEBT-131](#-debt-131--a-task-row-is-46px-because-the-completion-control-is-45px-and-nothing-else-decides-it--p3)'s question in a different place), or D43 restates the 24px choice with the coarse-pointer case written down.
- **Closing condition.** `ds-final-audit.mjs` reports zero target findings at 320 and 390, or D43 names each survivor.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-07 (the adaptive/density audit).

### ☐ DEBT-137 — A Project has no description field, so its gallery card cannot say what it is — P2
- **Current issue.** `mockup3.png` draws a two-line description on every Project card, and REDESIGN-04 §6.1 assumed one existed. It does not. The spine's `entities` table stores identity and lifecycle only (migration 0001); `project_details` stores workflow status, archival and an icon key (0008, 0032). There is no description column for Projects — nor for Areas. Goals have `definition_of_done`, which is the nearest equivalent in the product and belongs to Goals alone.
- **Impact.** A Projects gallery is recognisable by mark and name and by nothing else. An owner with two similarly-named Projects, or one returning to work after a month, has to open a record to learn what it was for — which is the question a gallery card exists to answer without a click. `ProjectCard` carries the slot (`description`, clamped to two lines) and the gallery passes nothing, so the card renders an honest absence rather than placeholder prose.
- **Desired future state.** A Project (and probably an Area) carries a short, optional description on its module-owned detail slice — the additive `project_details` / `area_details` precedent, not a spine change — editable inline on the record with the shared multiline field, and surfaced on the card and in search.
- **Closing condition.** A Project's real description renders on its gallery card, clamped to two lines, absent where unset; `ProjectCard.description` has a caller; REDESIGN-04 §4.7 can be struck.
- **Related roadmap item.** REDESIGN-04 (`docs/design/REDESIGN_04_SPINE_WORKSPACES_2026_08.md` §4.7).

### ☐ DEBT-138 — The Projects table cannot be sorted, because the loader has no vocabulary for it — P3
- **Current issue.** REDESIGN-04 §5.4 permits sorting in the new Table view "only if sorting already exists in the loader's vocabulary". `ListProjectsInput.orderBy` has exactly two values — `created` and `recent` — and neither corresponds to a column the table draws (Area, Progress, Tasks, Updated). A clickable header today would either sort the loaded page client-side, which is a lie about a keyset-paginated collection, or need new repository orderings, new cursor scopes and new indexes.
- **Impact.** The table shows the collection's own order in both presentations. For a workspace with many Projects, "which is furthest behind?" still means reading rather than sorting.
- **Desired future state.** `ProjectOrder` gains the orderings the columns imply, each with an index and a cursor scope, and the table's headers become the control for them.
- **Closing condition.** A column header sorts the whole collection (not the loaded page), the cursor is bound to the ordering, and `e2e/spine-workspaces.spec.ts` proves a sort survives a "Load more".
- **Related roadmap item.** REDESIGN-04 §4.4.

### ☐ DEBT-139 — Migration 0042 has not been applied, and no production backup has been taken — P1
- **Current issue.** `migrations/0042_add_entity_identity_colour.sql` adds `colour_slot` to `area_details`, `project_details` and `goal_details`, and `icon_key` to `goal_details`. It has been applied to the LOCAL Miniflare D1 and the full suite is green against it, but it has **not** been applied to production and **no `pnpm run db:production:backup` has been run**, because the IDENTITY-01 environment held no Cloudflare credentials. AGENTS.md makes the backup non-negotiable before any migration.
- **Impact.** Every read path already handles the columns' absence exactly as it handles a missing detail row, so an un-migrated production is not broken — it simply cannot store a choice, and the picker's Apply would fail against it.
- **Desired future state.** The documented order, run in full before this branch is deployed: `db:production:backup` → `db:production:backup:list` (confirm non-zero) → `db:migrate:local` + full suite → `db:production:list` (confirm 0042 pending) → `db:production:apply`.
- **Closing condition.** A backup object exists with the pre-0042 timestamp, `db:production:list` shows 0042 applied, and the picker persists a choice against production.
- **Related roadmap item.** IDENTITY-01 §5.

### ☐ DEBT-140 — `red` and `rose` are the identity ramp's one weak pair — P3
- **Current issue.** The two slots sit 7.5° apart in HCT hue and clip to the same gamut ceiling in the dark appearance (`#ff8078` and `#ff7e88`). `identity-ramp.test.ts` passes them — they differ in hue and, in light, in tone — but an owner who picks both for adjacent Areas will not get much from the difference. The ramp's dark tone was lowered from 74 to 68 partly to buy back what chroma there was.
- **Impact.** Fifteen of sixteen slots are cleanly separable; one pair is not. Identity is never carried by colour alone (the record's name is always text beside its tile, and both slots are named in the picker), so this costs recognition speed rather than meaning.
- **Desired future state.** Either `rose` moves far enough from `red` to hold a real separation in dark, or it is replaced with a hue the ramp does not already occupy — judged on a seeded grid in both appearances, as the slot-16 call was.
- **Closing condition.** No two slots sit within 12° of hue at a comparable tone in either appearance, asserted.
- **Related roadmap item.** IDENTITY-01 §2.5.

### ☐ DEBT-141 — The application frame's icons are Material Symbols; the identity vocabulary is DalyHub's own — P3
- **Current issue.** IDENTITY-01 drew all 101 identity glyphs, plus every entity default, as DalyHub stroke icons (`app/shared/icons/entity-glyphs.tsx`) because a filled symbol inside the rebuilt identity tile reads as a blob of the record's hue. `app/shared/icons/icons.tsx` — the navigation rail, the toolbars, the menus, the empty states — is still Material Symbols Outlined at a different construction and a different optical weight.
- **Impact.** The split is invisible where it was made deliberately (a record's tile is never beside a rail glyph at the same size) and visible where the two meet at close quarters, such as an empty state that pairs a frame glyph with a record's mark.
- **Desired future state.** One drawn vocabulary for the whole product, or a documented rule for exactly which surfaces take which — decided, not inherited.
- **Closing condition.** Either every `createIcon` glyph is redrawn in the stroke idiom, or `DALYHUB_DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` states the boundary and a test holds each set to its side of it.
- **Related roadmap item.** IDENTITY-01 §6.1.

### ☐ DEBT-142 — Person avatars and analytics split bars still paint from the retired container ramp — P3
- **Current issue.** `app/styles/people.css` (`.dh-person-avatar[data-accent]`) and `app/styles/analytics.css` (`.dh-analytics__split-track[data-accent]`) still read `--md-sys-color-area-accent-N(-container)`, so the `area-accent-*` quartet stays generated for them. `areaAccentForRank` survives as a shim resolving through `identityForRank` so the number and the name cannot disagree about which slot a rank lands on.
- **Impact.** Two surfaces speak the old vocabulary. Neither is a record's own identity — one is a person's relationship circle, the other an Area breakdown chart — so nothing on the spine is inconsistent, but the tokens cannot be retired while they have readers.
- **Desired future state.** The person circle gets its own named ramp (it is a relationship, not a rank), the analytics series moves to the chart ramp it should have been on, and `area-accent-*` is deleted from the generator.
- **Closing condition.** `rg "area-accent" app/styles` returns nothing, and the generator no longer emits the group.
- **Related roadmap item.** IDENTITY-01 §10, `docs/md3-inventory.md`.

### ☑ DEBT-143 — Today's plan draws its own task row, not the shared `TaskRow` — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 (TODAY-TASK-01)**
- **What it was.** [TODAY-11](../design/TODAY_11_COMMAND_CENTRE_2026_08.md) rebuilt Today's plan to `MOCKUP 5.png` but kept the module-private row in `TodayScreen.tsx` rather than adopting [`~/shared/task-record/TaskRow`](../../app/shared/task-record/TaskRow.tsx), which `/tasks` had drawn since DS-04. One object, two anatomies — and, the part that cost the owner something, two sets of capabilities: a task's project, dates and priority were editable in place on `/tasks` and read-only on the surface opened first every morning.
- **How it was closed.** [TODAY-TASK-01](../design/TODAY_TASK_01_ONE_TASK_ROW_2026_08.md). `TodayScreen.tsx` declares no row and no `ParentPill`; the plan's three bands render the shared `TaskRow` inside the shared `TaskList`, and `today.css` no longer carries `.dh-day-row__due`, `.dh-day-row__parent`, `.dh-day-row__priority`, `.dh-day-row__check` or `.dh-day-list--overdue`.
- **The "trailing slot" this entry anticipated was not needed, and that is the evidence the two rows were one row.** The mockup's context pill IS the shared row's project cell; the overdue age ("Due 2 days ago") IS its date cell, which renders a passed date as "3 days ago" in the overdue colour, in words; the priority flag IS its priority cell. Nothing was added to `TaskRow` for Today's sake.
- **What DID grow, and it grew SHARED.** `toTaskRowProjection` and `applyTaskListItemPatch` moved into `~/shared/task-record/task-view` (so there is one display-state derivation and one optimistic applier, not two that agree); `buildTaskRowActions` holds the overflow SET for both callers; and the column ladder below 56rem was corrected — it still carried the widths from before FINAL-UI swapped date and project, giving priority 9.5rem and project 4rem. Today declares **no** `.dh-today .dh-taskrow` structural overrides.
- **Authority is unchanged.** `useDayTaskActions` is a host — a patch map, an announcement, a revalidation — and every write leaves through the shared posters to `POST /tasks/:id` and `POST /tasks/bulk`. The route's own completion fetcher and `completion-feedback.ts` were DELETED rather than left as a second door. Today's loader adds ONE bounded `searchTaskParents({ limit: 50 })` — the same call `/tasks` makes, at the same bound.
- **Evidence.** `grep -rn "function TaskRow" app/modules/today` returns nothing, asserted structurally by `test/unit/today/one-task-row.test.ts` (which also refuses `TodayTaskRow`/`CompactTodayTaskRow`/`DashboardTaskRow` and asserts the host makes no `fetch` of its own). `e2e/today-task-convergence.spec.ts` re-projects, re-prioritises, re-schedules, completes, reopens and returns a task to the Inbox FROM `/today` and asserts each result on `/tasks` — the closing condition this entry asked for, in both directions.

### ☑ DEBT-144 — A plan row's context pill carries no identity, because the planning read carries none — P3 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 (TODAY-TASK-01)**
- **What it was.** `TaskListItem.parent` was a `TaskRelation { kind, id, title }` — no `iconKey`, no `colourRank`, no `colourSlot` — so TODAY-11 drew a NEUTRAL pill. The two alternatives were both refused: an identity lookup per row is an N+1, and colouring only the parents that happened to be in another already-loaded list would leave a list where some rows carry identity and some do not, which reads as a rendering fault.
- **How it was closed, and it is the desired future state this entry wrote down.** `TaskRelation` now carries `colourSlot`, `iconKey` and `colourRank` — exactly the three inputs `resolveIdentity` walks — populated by the SAME joined statement that already resolved `parent_title`. One CTE (`task_parent_identity`) ranks the workspace's Areas and Projects in separate 0-based sequences, matching `PROJECT_RANKS_CTE`/`AREA_RANKS_CTE` in `d1-project-repository.ts`, so the same number reaches the same resolver on every surface. **No extra query, no N+1, no migration, no column** — one extra scan of the workspace's Areas and Projects per task-list statement.
- **Both surfaces changed, which is what makes it structural.** `InlineTaskParent` draws the shared `AccentIcon` through `resolveIdentity` instead of the entity TYPE's generic badge, so `/tasks` gained the same correction rather than Today acquiring a private one. `TaskParentCandidate` carries identity too, so the optimistic paint after re-filing a task does not flash neutral before the re-read.
- **Evidence.** `test/kernel/task-parent-identity.test.ts` asserts the same Project resolves to the same `{ colourSlot, iconKey, colourRank }` through `listPlanningTasks` (Today), `listWorkspaceTasks` (Tasks) and `listProjectTasks` (the Project record), that an unchosen parent carries its derived rank, that Areas and Projects rank in separate sequences, and that a parent CANDIDATE offers the same identity as the relation. `e2e/today-task-convergence.spec.ts` asserts the mark's resolved `data-identity` is the same string on `/today` and on `/tasks` for one task.

### ☐ DEBT-145 — Completions cannot be bucketed by owner-local hour without new kernel surface — P3
- **Current issue.** `MOCKUP 5.png`'s Insights panel closes with "Most productive — Mornings, 9AM–12PM". Completion instants exist in the append-only Activity stream and the counts are already read per DAY (`countTaskActivityByDay`), but hour-of-day in the owner's timezone is **not** a function of the UTC instant alone: a daylight-saving transition adds or removes an hour, which is exactly why `loadActivityTrend` SKIPS a collapsed local midnight rather than inventing a boundary. Expressing 24 hourly buckets over a 7-day window as one statement needs ~168 bound ranges; the alternative is a new bounded read on the kernel `TaskRepository`.
- **Impact.** None on correctness — the insight is absent, not wrong. What is lost is a real and cheap-sounding observation the owner's own data could support.
- **Desired future state.** ONE bounded read that returns completion instants (or pre-bucketed hour counts) for a window, on the same "caller supplies the owner-timezone boundaries" contract `countTaskActivityByDay` already follows, with a kernel test proving the buckets are right across a DST transition in both directions.
- **Closing condition.** Today (or Analytics, which owns trends properly) states the owner's most productive part of the day from one bounded query, phrased descriptively ("Most tasks completed: mornings") rather than as a judgement, with the DST test passing.
- **Related roadmap item.** [UIX-05 Analytics](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2.md); a future Analytics refinement, since Analytics owns a real range picker and states each figure's provenance.

### ☐ DEBT-146 — Two third-party credential stores, two different protections — P2
- **Current issue.** DalyHub now holds third-party credentials in two places, protected two different ways. A calendar feed URL is sealed with AES-256-GCM under `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (a `wrangler secret`) and bound to its workspace by the AEAD additional data ([ADR-091](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-091-external-calendars-as-a-read-only-projection--a-sealed-secret-kernel-primitive-an-rfc-5545-parser-at-the-edge-and-an-explicit-link-to-the-meeting-authority), migration `0041`). A Pushover user key and application token are stored as plain columns on `notification_settings` (migration `0043`), a decision taken knowingly and recorded as [ADR-099](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-099-notifications-are-events-in-a-ledger-not-a-second-attention-model--insert-before-send-a-channel-contract-and-secrets-in-the-settings-store) decision 6.
- **Why it is like this, honestly.** The two secrets have different owners. A feed URL is the DEPLOYMENT's — it is pasted once and never changes — whereas the Pushover credentials are the OWNER's and change when they change them, so `wrangler secret` would make "turn on notifications" a deploy. Sealing them in D1 under the same key would be a real improvement, and it was rejected in this item only because it protects against exactly one thing (a stolen database file, without the Worker's environment) and should be decided for BOTH credential stores at once rather than smuggled into a notifications item.
- **Impact.** Low today: a single-owner Worker behind Cloudflare Access, with the columns unselected by every ordinary read, absent from the settings view type, and absent from every response, error and log. What it costs is uniformity — a reviewer cannot answer "how does DalyHub protect a third-party credential?" with one sentence, and the weaker answer is the one a future credential is most likely to copy.
- **Desired future state.** ONE credential-storage rule for the whole product: every third-party secret sealed with the same primitive, bound to its workspace, opened only at the point of use — and one place that says so, so a third integration does not need this decision made again.
- **Closing condition.** The Pushover credentials are stored sealed, `calendar-secrets.server.ts`'s sealing is a shared kernel primitive rather than a calendar one, and a kernel test proves a row copied between workspaces does not open. The plaintext columns are dropped in the same migration that seals them.
- **Related roadmap item.** [NOTIFY-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md) (raised there); [CAL-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md) (established the sealed pattern).

### ☐ DEBT-147 — The Pushover validate→enable path has no test that walks it end to end — P3
- **Current issue.** NOTIFY-01's verification asked for the Settings gate to be driven "with the Pushover API faked at the boundary". What shipped covers both halves of the gate but not the transition between them: `e2e/notifications.spec.ts` proves the channel CANNOT be enabled before validation (the switch and the test control are both disabled, and the surface says why), and `test/kernel/notifications.test.ts` proves the repository and the database refuse it and that a changed credential un-validates. Nothing exercises `POST /settings/notifications/test` → `validatePushoverCredentials` → `createPushoverChannel().deliver` → `recordPushoverValidation` → the switch becoming enabled, as one flow, against a stubbed `fetch`.
- **Why it is like this, honestly.** `validatePushoverCredentials` and `createPushoverChannel` both take an injectable fetcher precisely so this is testable, but the ROUTE constructs them without one — it is the composition boundary, and threading a fetcher through it for a test is the kind of seam that exists only for the test. Doing it properly means a small environment-level injection point, which is a design decision rather than a missing assertion.
- **Impact.** Low, and bounded by what is already proved: the gate cannot be bypassed, the formatter's output is unit-tested against Pushover's documented bounds, and the adapter's status→reason mapping is a pure function. What is unproved is that the route wires those four calls together in the right order — a defect that would be caught the first time an owner pressed the button.
- **Desired future state.** The Pushover fetcher reaches the route through the same optional-config seam `APP_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` uses, so a test can supply one, and one test walks save keys → send test → enable → a delivery recorded, with the API stubbed.
- **Closing condition.** A kernel test drives `POST /settings/notifications/test` against a stubbed fetcher and asserts the validation stamp is written, that a rejected credential leaves it unwritten, and that the channel becomes enableable only in the first case.
- **Related roadmap item.** [NOTIFY-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md).

### ☑ DEBT-148 — Three E2E specs assert a shell that TODAY-11 and FINAL-UI already replaced — P2 — **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 (HARDEN-05)**
- **Current issue.** `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts`, and parts of `e2e/today.spec.ts` and `e2e/settings.spec.ts`, assert names and structures the product stopped having. Four separate drifts, all of them the spec being stale rather than the product being wrong:
  - **The phone bottom bar.** The spec expects `Today · Tasks · Capture · Diary · More`. `BottomNav.tsx`'s own docstring says `Today · Tasks · Add · Projects · More`, and `app/modules/projects/routes.manifest.ts` states the move deliberately ("The references put Projects beside Today and Tasks there; Diary held the slot"). Every assertion that clicks `getByRole("button", { name: "Capture" })` or the `Diary` link times out — that is seven tests in the Quick Capture and navigation blocks alone.
  - **Today's day landmark.** `todayDayPanel()` in `e2e/helpers.ts` resolves `getByRole("region", { name: "Focus" })`. TODAY-11 renamed that region to **Today's plan**. The helper's own comment records having been fixed for exactly this reason once before, when M3X-02 renamed "My day" to "Focus" — so this is the same defect recurring, and `expectOnToday()` now waits out its timeout for every caller.
  - **Today's stat rank.** `today.spec.ts` requires `[data-testid="today-summary"]` to be visible. TODAY-11 made `TodayStatRank` return `null` when there is nothing real to measure, which is the correct behaviour and is what its own comment promises — the spec simply predates it.
  - **Reflow at 200% zoom.** `mobile-shell.spec.ts`'s zoom check fails on `/today` at 195px: MEASURED `scrollWidth` 204 against a 195px document, and the offending element is TODAY-11's reflection panel button ("Write today's entry", `dh-btn dh-btn--ghost`, right edge 204).
- **Confirmed pre-existing, not inherited from a later change.** Run at `b2622b6` (the commit before NOTIFY-01) in a clean worktree, `mobile-shell.spec.ts`'s first test fails identically. The zoom overflow was measured twice on the same running server, once with `.dh-bell{display:none}` injected: `scrollWidth` was 204 both times, so the NOTIFY-01 bell contributes nothing to it.
- **Impact.** The E2E gate is not a signal for these three files: a real regression in the phone shell or on Today would land inside twenty already-red tests and be indistinguishable from them. That is the specific failure mode [HARDEN-03/HARDEN-04](HARDEN_03_CLOSE_RELIABILITY_LOOP_2026_08.md) worked to remove, arriving again from the other direction — not flakiness this time, but assertions about a product that has moved.
- **Desired future state.** The four assertions above updated to the shipped behaviour, with the shared `todayDayPanel()` helper fixed once rather than at its call sites (its own comment already argues this: "a landmark helper that names a landmark the product does not have is one bug, not four"), and the 200%-zoom overflow either fixed in the reflection panel or recorded as its own entry with a measurement.
- **Closing condition.** `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts e2e/today.spec.ts e2e/settings.spec.ts` is green on `main`, and the zoom check measures no document overflow at 195px on every route it names.
- **Met 2026-08-17 by [HARDEN-05](HARDEN_05_GREEN_MAIN_2026_08.md).** Both halves. The three specs are green — the bottom bar, the `todayDayPanel()` helper and the stat rank had already been corrected by #192 and #193, and this pass finished the file set. The 195px residual this entry measured at `scrollWidth` 204 and could not attribute (it had ruled out the NOTIFY-01 bell and suspected the reflection panel's button) is FIXED rather than recorded: the offender is the task row's metadata line, which at 195px had 17px of track for a project cell with a 64px floor and overflowed rather than shrinking. The row now recomposes at that width — metadata on a full row of its own, wrapping — and every route the zoom check names measures `scrollWidth === clientWidth`.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TODAY-11](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md) and FINAL-UI raised it; found and recorded — deliberately not fixed — by [NOTIFY-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_2.md), because correcting another item's acceptance assertions without its brief is a decision for whoever owns them.

### ☐ DEBT-151 — The shell precache is 1,321 kB, 2.0× its V2.0.1 measurement — P2

- **Current issue.** `e2e/pwa-budget.spec.ts` measures what a phone downloads to become offline-bootable. MEASURED 2026-08-17 at `f994aa0`: **30 assets, 1,320,668 B uncompressed, 283,714 B over the wire**. The budget was written for V2.0.1 at 674 kB / ~180 kB, with a 1,200,000 B ceiling — so the shell has crossed it, and it is the ceiling that had to move rather than the measurement.

  It is not a leak. **731 kB of the 1,321 is the application stylesheet**, and roughly 200 kB of THAT is the generated multi-scheme colour layer in `tokens.css` — every colour scheme in both appearances, shipped together so that changing scheme is instant and previewable. The rest is six design programmes' worth of CSS arriving in one file, plus `entry.client` at 183 kB.
- **Why it was not fixed by [HARDEN-05](HARDEN_05_GREEN_MAIN_2026_08.md).** Reducing it means one of two performance-architecture decisions with design consequences: splitting the stylesheet so a route pays only for what it draws, or not shipping the schemes an owner has not chosen (which trades an instant scheme change for a fetch, and is THEME-01's decision to revisit). Neither belongs in a suite-triage pass, and taking either quietly would be exactly the kind of change this register exists to stop.

  What HARDEN-05 did instead of quietly loosening the ratchet is tighten it where it counts: the uncompressed ceiling moves to the measured value plus ~10% (it carried ~1.8× headroom before), and a SECOND ceiling was added on the COMPRESSED bytes — 284 kB measured against 320 kB — which is what actually crosses a metered connection and which the suite could not see at all.
- **Impact.** A first visit to DalyHub on a phone downloads 284 kB of shell before anything else, up from ~180 kB. Nothing is broken; the cost is on the metered connection AGENTS.md §16 asks the budgets to protect, and it is growing faster than the product is.
- **Desired future state.** The shell's precache falls back under 1 MB uncompressed, through a deliberate decision about where the colour schemes live and whether the stylesheet is one file.
- **Closing condition.** `[budget] precache …` prints under 1,000,000 B uncompressed with every scheme still reachable, and the two ceilings in `pwa-budget.spec.ts` come down with it.
- **Related roadmap item.** The V2.2 performance pass; [ADR-068](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) (the precache set) and THEME-01 (the schemes).

### ☐ DEBT-152 — The Today week strip hard-codes Monday while a first-day-of-week preference exists — P3

- **Current issue.** `app/modules/today/day/week-strip.ts` builds the Schedule panel's seven-day strip from a hard-coded Monday, and says so in a comment that is factually wrong: *"It is a constant here rather than a preference: DalyHub has no first-day-of-week setting."* DalyHub does. `firstDayOfWeek` (`monday` | `sunday`) has been a real, validated, exported, settings-surfaced preference since REVIEWS-01, `weeklyPeriod` in [`app/kernel/reviews/review-periods.ts`](../../app/kernel/reviews/review-periods.ts) resolves a weekly Review's period from it, and [PLAN-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md) now resolves the planning week from it too.
- **Why it was not fixed by PLAN-01.** It is a Today defect, not a planning one, and fixing it means changing what an existing surface DRAWS for a Sunday-start owner — a visible behaviour change to the most-visited screen in the product, in a PR whose subject is a different surface. PLAN-01 did the part that was its own: it stated both week conventions together in `app/kernel/planning/planning-week.ts` (the owner's calendar week, and the deliberately preference-free rolling `today … today + 6` window behind `due_this_week`), so the next reader does not have to rediscover which is which.
- **Impact.** Small, and only for a Sunday-start owner: their Today strip starts on Monday while their weekly Review period and their planning week start on Sunday. Three surfaces, two answers to "which week is this?".
- **Desired future state.** ONE shared week-start helper, taking `firstDayOfWeek` as an argument, consumed by the Today strip, the Review period and the planning week — with the rolling task window explicitly documented as the different question it is, not folded in.
- **Closing condition.** `weekStartIso` takes the preference; `/today`'s strip and `/plan`'s week begin on the same day for a Sunday-start owner; the false comment is gone; a unit test asserts both conventions from one module.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PLAN-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-plan-01--weekly-planning--delivered-2026-08-17); TODAY-11 (the strip).

### ☐ DEBT-153 — A saved view cannot express a specific date WINDOW from the Tasks controls — P3

- **Current issue.** [SMART-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-smart-01--saved-smart-lists--delivered-2026-08-17) added explicit `dueFrom`/`dueTo` and `plannedFrom`/`plannedTo` bounds to the declarative view configuration, the URL codec and the repository — so a stored view CAN hold "planned 17–23 August" and Weekly Planning uses exactly that. What it did not add is a way to BUILD one from the Tasks control surface: the shared `CollectionControlGroup` model holds closed option sets, and a date range is neither closed nor an option. A window is reachable today only by editing the URL.
- **Why it was not built.** The owner-facing cases the item names — "Due this week", "Planned next week" — are already expressible from the controls through the DERIVED `dueState`/`plannedState` dimensions, which is the better answer for an everyday view anyway: a relative window stays true tomorrow, and a stored absolute one silently goes stale. Adding a date-range picker to the shared sheet is a real shared-control design (two dates, validation, a chip that reads well, a phone form) and it belongs with the collection-controls work rather than bolted onto a planning PR.
- **Impact.** Small. An owner who wants a saved view over a fixed window — "the December push" — cannot make one without hand-editing a link. Nothing is wrong or misleading; a capability is only half-reachable.
- **Desired future state.** A shared, keyboard-operable DATE-RANGE control in the collection-controls model, so a range is a control like any other and its chip reads "Due: 1–14 Dec".
- **Closing condition.** A date window can be set from `/tasks` at desktop and phone widths, saved into a view, and re-applied by selecting that view; the derived relative states remain the default and stay recommended.
- **Related roadmap item.** [SMART-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-smart-01--saved-smart-lists--delivered-2026-08-17); the shared collection controls (MOBILE-01 / DS-07).

### ☐ DEBT-154 — The Today week strip and the Habits week are still two different reads of "this week" — P3

- **Current issue.** [HABITS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-habits-01--habits-and-routines--delivered-2026-08-18) resolves a Habit's week through `planningWeekStart` in [`app/kernel/planning/planning-week.ts`](../../app/kernel/planning/planning-week.ts) — the same authority PLAN-01 and a weekly Review use, taking `firstDayOfWeek` as an argument. That is now THREE consumers of one correct helper, and the Today Schedule panel's strip ([`app/modules/today/day/week-strip.ts`](../../app/modules/today/day/week-strip.ts)) is still the one place that hard-codes Monday (DEBT-152).
- **Why it was not fixed here.** Same reason PLAN-01 left it: it is a Today defect, and fixing it changes what an existing, most-visited surface DRAWS for a Sunday-start owner — a visible behaviour change in a PR whose subject is a different module. What HABITS-01 could do without that risk, it did: it consumed the shared helper rather than adding a fourth week definition.
- **Impact.** Unchanged from DEBT-152 in kind, but the count is now worse: a Sunday-start owner sees their habit week, their planning week and their Review period start on Sunday while Today's schedule strip starts on Monday.
- **Desired future state.** The Today strip takes `firstDayOfWeek` from the same helper; the rolling `today … today + 6` task window stays explicitly documented as the different question it is.
- **Closing condition.** DEBT-152's closing condition, plus: `/habits`, `/plan` and `/today` all begin the week on the same day for a Sunday-start owner, asserted once.
- **Related roadmap item.** [HABITS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-habits-01--habits-and-routines--delivered-2026-08-18); DEBT-152.

### ☐ DEBT-155 — A habit check-in is online-only, and the surface can only say so after it fails — P3

- **Current issue.** PWA-12's offline mutation queue is deliberately Task-shaped and says so in its own contract ([`app/kernel/offline/offline-mutation.ts`](../../app/kernel/offline/offline-mutation.ts)): *"six operations, one entity type … every other module is online-only and stays that way until this contract has proven itself."* HABITS-01 respected that rather than widening it, and deliberately did NOT start a second, habit-shaped localStorage queue beside it. A check-in made with no connection therefore fails, and `useHabitCheckIn` rolls its optimistic tick back and announces *"That couldn't be saved — you may be offline. Nothing was changed."*
- **Why it was not built.** Extending the queue is a decision with its own conflict semantics — a check-in's identity is `(habit, date)` rather than a field value, so the existing last-value-wins replace rule and the field-level conflict comparison do not describe it. Getting that wrong would produce a duplicate or a lost completion, which is worse than an honest failure.
- **Impact.** Small but real: ticking a habit is exactly the sort of thing done on a phone away from signal, and the answer today is "try again later" rather than "it will sync".
- **Desired future state.** One additional operation in the SHARED queue — `habit_check_in` / `habit_undo`, keyed on `(habitId, date)` and therefore idempotent by the same rule the database already enforces — replayed through the same `/habits/:id/check-in` route, with no second queue and no second authority.
- **Closing condition.** A check-in made offline is queued, survives a reload, replays to the canonical route on reconnect, and produces exactly one completion when the same day is ticked twice offline; `pwa-offline-tasks.spec.ts`'s habit counterpart proves it.
- **Related roadmap item.** [HABITS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-habits-01--habits-and-routines--delivered-2026-08-18); PWA-12.

### ☐ DEBT-156 — The Weekly Review says nothing about habit consistency — P3

- **Current issue.** [HABITS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-habits-01--habits-and-routines--delivered-2026-08-18) deliberately added no Review integration. The Overview step's evidence could truthfully say *"Habits: 8 of 10 scheduled check-ins"*, and the reusable bounded read it would need already exists (`evaluateHabitConsistency` plus `listCompletionsInRange`) — but a Review's period is an arbitrary range, neither a week nor a fixed number of days, so a correct denominator means summing expectations across a schedule-VERSION chain over that range and threading a second aggregation path through REVIEW-02's guided workflow and its own bounded reads.
- **Why it was not built.** Two risks, both stated rather than discovered later. The denominator has to be historically correct or the figure is a lie in the one surface whose whole job is honest retrospection; and a consistency ratio in a Review is one careless sentence away from becoming a score the owner is graded on, which is the manufactured guilt ADR-102 §8 exists to prevent.
- **Impact.** A Review is complete about Tasks, Projects, Goals and Areas and silent about routines. Nothing is wrong; something useful is missing.
- **Desired future state.** ONE bounded read on the Overview step, phrased factually, with a denominator derived from the schedule versions in force during the period — and no verdict attached to it.
- **Closing condition.** A weekly Review's Overview states a habit figure whose denominator is provably the historical expectation (a unit test over a period spanning a schedule change), the step's query budget is unchanged in shape, and the wording assertion in `habit-view.test.ts` is extended to cover it.
- **Related roadmap item.** [HABITS-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-habits-01--habits-and-routines--delivered-2026-08-18); REVIEW-02.

### ◐ DEBT-157 — The E2E partition durations can only be refreshed from a FAILING run — P1 *(re-rated 2026-08-20; mechanism closed by HARDEN-06A the same day, held open for its confirming run)*

> **Read the closing condition below before reading the history.** HARDEN-06A has
> closed the MECHANISM half in full — twelve `results.json` published on run
> `32333645709`, eleven of them from green partitions, and the manifest
> re-derived from the complete set of twelve, and run `32338241602` green on it
> with 1847 of 1847 tests executed — and the entry stays open only for the one
> thing a pull request cannot produce: the same on **`main`**.

- **Current issue.** The gate's split is derived from MEASURED per-spec-file seconds in [`e2e/partitions.json`](../../e2e/partitions.json), refreshed by `pnpm run e2e:partitions:generate --from <results.json>` — and `results.json` is written inside `playwright-report/`, which [`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml) uploads only `if: always() && steps.e2e.outcome != 'success'`. **A green partition therefore uploads nothing**, so the numbers the balance depends on can only ever be refreshed from partitions that failed. The measurements decay silently as the suite grows.
- **Evidence (run 32107056970, `main` @ bcdba66).** Elapsed against the manifest's own budget: p01 19.6 min / 17.6, p02 20.3 / 17.5, p08 21.1 / 17.5, while p09 and p10 — the two `--shard` slices of `responsive.spec.ts` — are budgeted at 9.5 min each and finished in ~11.4 and ~12.7. `worst/mean` is 1.10 as committed and materially worse as run. Separately, four spec files (`identity`, `plan-responsive`, `plan-weekly-planning`, `spine-workspaces`) still carry the pessimistic `DEFAULT_SPEC_SECONDS` of 120 because no run has ever measured them.
- **Impact.** Bounded today and not what made the suite red: V2.3-GATE-01 reproduced and classified all five failing partitions and none of them was a timeout. But p08 at 21.1 min is 84% of Playwright's own 25-minute `globalTimeout`, and the mechanism that would rebalance it cannot be fed. This is the failure mode HARDEN-04/[DEBT-128](#-debt-128--the-e2e-gate-loses-tests-and-reports-green--p1--resolved-2026-08-12) was built to end, returning through the door marked "evidence".
- **Why it was not fixed here.** V2.3-GATE-01 is a truth-restoration pass whose scope guard says not to redesign CI unless imbalance is materially causing failures. It is not. Refreshing from LOCAL timings instead would be worse than leaving it: the manifest's whole authority is that its numbers were measured on named CI runs, and quietly mixing in numbers from a different machine would destroy that without saying so.
- **Desired future state.** `results.json` travels out of every partition, green or red — either as its own small always-uploaded artifact or by lifting it out of the report directory — so `e2e:partitions:generate --from` can be fed by an ordinary green run, and the split is re-derived from real numbers when the suite grows.
- **Closing condition.** A green `main` run publishes a per-partition `results.json`; `pnpm run e2e:partitions:generate --from` is re-run from a complete set of ten; the committed manifest carries a measured duration for every spec file (no `DEFAULT_SPEC_SECONDS` fallbacks) and a `worst/mean` the next run's elapsed times actually agree with.
- **Re-rated P1 on 2026-08-20 — the predicted failure has happened, and it is no longer bounded** ([whole-application audit](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_BUG_AUDIT_2026_08.md#f-03--main-is-red-and-33-e2e-tests-have-never-executed-on-the-merge-commit--p1), F-03). On run `32321840125` (`main` @ f031e0ee, the TASKS-12 merge) partition **p05 hit the 25-minute `globalTimeout`** — 25.0 min against its own 19.4 min budget — and **33 of its 245 tests never executed** (`tasks-dependencies` 3, `tasks-optimistic` 4, `today-keyboard` 10, `ux-01-daily-driver` 10, `visual-system` 6). The entry above says *"four spec files still carry the pessimistic `DEFAULT_SPEC_SECONDS` of 120"*; there are now **ten** — `habits`, `today-task-convergence`, `identity`, `ux-02-plan-habits`, `spine-workspaces`, `plan-weekly-planning`, `tasks-dependencies`, `notifications`, `plan-responsive`, `plan-smart-lists` — and the run measured `tasks-dependencies.spec.ts` at **324 s against its 120 s estimate**, which is p05's overrun on its own. The failing partition has finally uploaded the artefact the refresh needs (`e2e-report-p05`, artifact 9390527305).
- **Mostly resolved by [HARDEN-06A](HARDEN_06A_FINISHING_E2E_GATE_2026_08.md) (2026-08-20).** Three of the four clauses of the closing condition are met, and the mechanism that made the entry inevitable is gone.
  1. **`results.json` travels out of every partition, green or red — proven, not merely wired up.** [`ci.yml`](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml) uploads it as its own small `e2e-results-<partition>` artifact on `if: always()`, beside — not inside — the failure-only report. On run [`32333645709`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/actions/runs/32333645709) **all twelve partitions published one, eleven of them GREEN**, and the manifest was then re-derived from the complete set of twelve. That is the whole of the *desired future state* above, exercised end to end.
  2. **The committed manifest carries a measured duration for every spec file, and no `DEFAULT_SPEC_SECONDS` fallback is load-bearing anywhere in it.** All 111 files have a duration, a test count and a recorded provenance, and the `source` map now holds **one value for all 111 of them** — `ci:32333645709`. No local, normalised or extrapolated figure survives in the file. The ten that had no measurement at all were measured (`tasks-dependencies` **326.7 s against its 120 s guess**), and the stale ones were corrected in both directions (`responsive` 1141.7 s → **1416.6 s**, and 519 tests rather than the 465 the manifest knew of; `project-templates` 312 s → 204.6 s). The manifest's own test estimate was **1595 against a real 1847**.
  3. **The decay cannot recur silently.** `pnpm run e2e:partitions:check`, which `Static` runs, now FAILS while any spec file on disk lacks a measured duration, a test count or a source — asked over the same canonical `listSpecFiles()` discovery the gate partitions on, so there is no second list to forget. It also fails when a partition is budgeted past a ceiling **derived** from `globalTimeout` (`25 min × 0.75 ÷ 1.12`, the last figure being the measured wall-clock cost of a second of test time — 1.114 overall and 1.094–1.131 per partition across all twelve of run `32333645709`) rather than the flat *"under 20 minutes"* the p05 that could not finish satisfied at 19.4 min. Ten partitions became twelve so the heaviest sits at **15.2 min**, 68% of the ceiling, against the 87% it was at.
  4. **The gate finishes, and the re-derived manifest predicts it.** Run [`32338241602`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/actions/runs/32338241602), the first with the committed manifest, collected **1847 tests, executed 1847, failed 0** — **CI Gate green** — across twelve partitions that all started and all finished, the worst at 76% of `globalTimeout`. Against `main`'s 33 unexecuted tests in one partition, that is the outage this entry became. The manifest predicted that run's total to within **0.9%** (175.1 min budgeted, 173.6 min measured), where the previous run's mixed-source manifest was 6% out, and the wall-clock overhead factor came back at **1.113** against 1.114 on the run before — two independent twelve-partition samples agreeing to 0.1%.
- **What is still outstanding, and why the entry is ◐ rather than ☑.** The word **`main`**. Every clause of the closing condition is now met on a PULL-REQUEST run, twice over, but the entry says `main` and that is not a formality: `main` is what gates the branch, and the manifest has never been exercised there.
  - **And the condition's last clause needs restating, because as written it cannot be met.** It asks for *"a `worst/mean` the next run's elapsed times actually agree with"*, and those are two different quantities: the committed **budget** spread is worst/mean **1.04**, while the **elapsed** spread on run `32338241602` was **1.13**, because per-partition wall-clock overhead ranged 1.093–1.134 and runner speed varies on top of it. They were never going to be equal. What the clause was reaching for — and what should be checked instead — is that the AGGREGATE prediction holds and no partition approaches the ceiling. Both are now measurable: 0.9% of aggregate error, and a worst partition at 76%.
  - **Close this entry when** a run on `main` is green with the committed manifest, every partition executes every test it collected, the aggregate budget is within a few percent of the measured total, and no partition exceeds ~80% of `globalTimeout`. **Not before**, and not on a single sample: this entry exists because measurements decay quietly, and closing it on one run would be the same mistake in a smaller font.
- **Related roadmap item.** [V2.3-GATE-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-v23-gate-01--a-trustworthy-v23-baseline--delivered-2026-08-18); HARDEN-04; [HARDEN-06A](HARDEN_06A_FINISHING_E2E_GATE_2026_08.md).

### ☐ DEBT-158 — A Goal measurement journey has never once run, because nothing in the seed is measurable — P2

- **Current issue.** [`e2e/spine-workspaces.spec.ts`](../../e2e/spine-workspaces.spec.ts) — *"records a measurement from the workspace and updates the trio and the chart"* — opens `/goals`, looks for a Goal row carrying a `progressbar`, and calls `test.skip()` when it finds none. MEASURED on the E2E database: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM goal_details WHERE target_value IS NOT NULL` is **0**, so it finds none, every time. The skip is reported by CI as *"1 skipped"* beside the passes.
- **Why it is written that way, and why that reasoning is still right.** The test deliberately drives whichever Goal the workspace is genuinely measuring rather than a fixture id, because the E2E seed and the design fixture are different databases and a journey pinned to one silently stops running against the other. The guard is sound; what is missing is the fixture that would satisfy it.
- **Impact.** REDESIGN-04's measurement journey — record a reading, the trio and the chart move together — has no end-to-end coverage at all, and the gate reports green while it never executes. That is exactly the "green claims are unverifiable" shape [DEBT-41](#-debt-41--the-e2e-suite-is-unreliable-on-main-so-ci-is-green-claims-are-unverifiable--p1--resolved-2026-08-02) was raised for, at one journey's scale.
- **Why it was not fixed here.** Making it run means giving the SHARED seed a measurable Goal, and that seed is read by Today, analytics, the Review evidence and the Goals collection. Introducing a new measurable Goal — and then a measurement written into it by every run of this journey — is a fixture change with a blast radius across partitions, which is not a change to make in the same pass that is trying to establish that the ten partitions are green.
- **Desired future state.** The journey owns its own measurable Goal — created and cleaned up by the spec, in the manner of `e2e/habits-fixtures.ts` — so it runs on every gate without widening what any other spec sees.
- **Closing condition.** The test executes (no `test.skip` path taken) on a full gate run, records a reading, and asserts the trio and the chart moved; the specs sharing its partition are unaffected.
- **Related roadmap item.** [V2.3-GATE-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-v23-gate-01--a-trustworthy-v23-baseline--delivered-2026-08-18); REDESIGN-04.

### ☐ DEBT-159 — Removing two filter chips in quick succession can put one of them back — P3

- **Current issue.** V2.3-GATE-01 fixed the lost update in the live-applying collection CONTROLS: every write now leaves through one function that records what it wrote, so a second choice composes over the first rather than over the committed state the loader has not yet replaced ([`app/shared/collection-layout/use-applied-params.ts`](../../app/shared/collection-layout/use-applied-params.ts)). The chip row is not on that path. Each chip's remove control is an ordinary `<Link>` whose `to` is computed at RENDER time from the applied parameters, so removing chip A and then chip B inside one loader round trip navigates to a URL B computed while A was still applied — and A comes back.
- **Why the chips are links, and why that should not change.** `CollectionFilterChips` states it in its own contract: *"the chips are ordinary links, so they are keyboard-operable, middle-clickable and Back/Forward-correct — the URL is the state."* Routing their removals through the controls' imperative writer would buy this narrow race at the cost of a real, shareable, middle-clickable link, which is a worse trade.
- **Impact.** Narrow and real. The window is one collection revalidation, and both clicks must land inside it on two DIFFERENT chips; a single removal, and a removal after the collection has settled, are both correct. Mitigated in one direction already: the chips now render from the same applied-parameter authority the controls write, so a removal made immediately after APPLYING a filter is correct where it previously was not.
- **Desired future state.** The chip's href is derived from the same in-flight authority the controls use — e.g. the row re-rendering with a pending base while a removal is loading — so the link stays a link and still composes.
- **New evidence, and it widens the entry (HARDEN-06A, 2026-08-20).** The sentence above says a removal made immediately after APPLYING a filter is correct. On a slow machine it is not. `e2e/tasks-collection.spec.ts:298` — *"removes one of two applied filters and leaves the other"*, which removes exactly ONE chip — fails **4 times out of 4, from a freshly migrated and seeded local D1, in isolation** on the HARDEN-06A sandbox: removing the `Priority: P1` chip lands on `/tasks?group=due_state`, with **neither** filter left. The same test passes on CI (run [`32333645709`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/actions/runs/32333645709), partition p02, 173 of 173 executed), so it is environment-dependent rather than intermittent, and the window is the collection's own commit rather than a first removal. Nothing was changed for it here: HARDEN-06A touched only the `Today integration` block of that file, the failing test's code path is byte-identical to `main`, and filter behaviour is outside that slice. Recorded rather than fixed, and the entry's *"mitigated in one direction already"* claim should be read as unproven until it is.
- **Closing condition.** A test in the shape of `e2e/tasks-collection.spec.ts`'s *"combines two filters chosen before the first has finished loading"* — two chip removals with the first revalidation held open — leaves both dimensions removed and neither restored. **And** the single-removal case above passes on a machine slow enough to open the window.
- **Related roadmap item.** [V2.3-GATE-01](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-v23-gate-01--a-trustworthy-v23-baseline--delivered-2026-08-18); SMART-01.

### ☐ DEBT-160 — Only checklist COMPLETION is offline-capable — P3

- **Current issue.** TASKS-13 made ticking and unticking a checklist item an offline mutation, carried by PWA-12's existing queue, receipts and conflict rule (`set_checklist_completed`, with the new `targetId` naming the item — [`app/kernel/offline/offline-mutation.ts`](../../app/kernel/offline/offline-mutation.ts)). Adding, renaming, deleting and reordering a checklist item are ONLINE-ONLY: a transport failure on any of them is reported as an ordinary refusal, exactly as it was before the slice existed ([`app/shared/task-record/use-task-checklist.ts`](../../app/shared/task-record/use-task-checklist.ts)).
- **Why it was not done.** Each of the four needs something the queue's contract does not carry. Adding needs a server-assigned id AND a server-assigned dense position; renaming and deleting address an item whose identity the device may no longer share; reordering contends over the list's WHOLE current order, which is not a single comparable field and cannot be arbitrated by PWA-12's one-field rule. Queueing any of them would mean inventing a second conflict model beside the one that exists — which is precisely what PWA-12's own contract refuses.
- **Impact.** Small and honest. The one act an owner performs repeatedly while working through a Task — ticking a step — works offline; the structural edits, which are made once when the checklist is written, need a connection and say so with their own wording rather than failing silently.
- **Desired future state.** Either a widened offline contract that can carry a client-minted id and a list-shaped conflict, or a documented decision that structural checklist edits stay online — taken deliberately rather than by omission.
- **Closing condition.** Either an E2E journey in the shape of `e2e/tasks-checklist.spec.ts`'s offline pair, adding an item offline and replaying it exactly once with no duplicate; or this entry closed as WON'T DO with the reasoning recorded in [`PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md`](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md#151-what-may-be-changed-offline).
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-13](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-tasks-13--checklists--delivered-2026-08-18); PWA-12.

### ☐ DEBT-161 — A phone Task row shows no checklist progress — P3

- **Current issue.** The shared `TaskRow` draws a compact "2 of 5" beside the title, and `app/styles/task-checklist.css` hides it below `md`. On a phone the step count is reachable only by opening the Task record.
- **Why it was done that way, MEASURED.** On the same page, with and without a checklist ([`measurements.json`](../design/assets/v2-3-tasks-13-checklists/measurements.json)): at 1440 and 1280 a row with the figure is 44px and a row without is 44px — it costs nothing. At 393 and 320 the row is two stacked lines and the title has the narrowest measure it ever gets, so five characters beside it took width off the title and wrapped it: 100px against 81px, nineteen pixels a row, on the surface where density matters most. The item's own brief gave the rule that was followed — if the indicator harms row clarity, keep it to the record.
- **Impact.** A phone owner scanning a list cannot see how far through a Task's steps they are without opening it. Narrow: the record is one tap away and is where the steps are worked through anyway.
- **Desired future state.** A phone row that can carry the figure without costing the title — most likely in the row's quiet second metadata line rather than in the title cell, which needs the shared row's phone composition to place it there rather than a CSS override.
- **Closing condition.** A measurement in `e2e/tasks-checklist-screenshots.spec.ts` showing a 393 and a 320 row WITH the figure at the same height as one without, and `e2e/tasks-checklist.spec.ts` asserting the figure is visible at 390.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-13](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-tasks-13--checklists--delivered-2026-08-18); DS-04.

### ☐ DEBT-162 — The six-column planning board needs a 1440px viewport — P3

- **Current issue.** UX-02 draws Mockup 7's six-column week board from 90rem (1440px) up, and FOLDS it to three columns over two rows below that ([`app/styles/plan.css`](../../app/styles/plan.css)). MEASURED with `node scripts/ux-02-shot.mjs --measure 1`: at 1440 the board is 888px and a day column is 147px; at 1280 the board is 696px, so six columns would be 108px and a Task card's title about 35px.
- **Why it was done that way.** 108px is narrower than the card the board needs, and squeezing it would truncate every title to two or three words — which is the exact failure ADR-101 §10 originally rejected a board to avoid. The fold is the product's standing "recompose, never squeeze" rule, and the three-column form is a good composition rather than a degraded one.
- **Impact.** An owner on a 1280 or 1366 laptop sees the board as 3 + 3 rather than as one row of six. The information is identical and nothing truncates; what is lost is the single-glance week the reference draws.
- **Desired future state.** Six columns at 1280, reached by finding width rather than by taking it off the title — the shell's 216px rail and the page gutters are the candidates, and neither has been measured for this.
- **Closing condition.** A measurement in `scripts/ux-02-shot.mjs`'s output showing six columns at 1280 with a card title at or above the 73px it measures at 1440, and no horizontal document overflow at 1280 or 1024.
- **Related roadmap item.** [UX-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-ux-02--weekly-planning-and-habits-rebuilt-to-the-visual-references--delivered-2026-08-19); [ADR-104](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md).

### ☐ DEBT-163 — A Sunday-start week draws seven board columns and wraps the seventh — P3

- **Current issue.** The board pairs ADJACENT weekend days into one column, computed from the loader's own `isWeekend` so it follows the owner's `firstDayOfWeek` rather than assuming Monday ([`app/modules/plan/PlanWorkspace.tsx`](../../app/modules/plan/PlanWorkspace.tsx), `toColumns`). With `firstDayOfWeek: sunday`, Sunday leads the week and Saturday trails it, so neither has a neighbour to pair with: the week yields seven groups and the seventh wraps to a second row.
- **Why it was done that way.** The alternative — pairing the two weekend days wherever they sit — would draw "Sun / Sat" as the FIRST column of a Sunday-start week, which puts the two days out of order to save a column. Ordering the days truthfully was judged worth an untidy wrap, and the wrap is legible.
- **Impact.** A Sunday-start owner sees six columns and one below them, instead of one row. Correct, and slightly untidy. The default preference is Monday, so it is not the common case.
- **Desired future state.** A weekend treatment that reads well when the two days are not adjacent — most likely a narrower column for each of them rather than a pairing — decided as a design question with a measurement.
- **Closing condition.** A capture at 1440 with `firstDayOfWeek: sunday` showing one row of columns in calendar order, and `plan-responsive.spec.ts` asserting no wrap at that preference.
- **Related roadmap item.** [UX-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-ux-02--weekly-planning-and-habits-rebuilt-to-the-visual-references--delivered-2026-08-19).

### ☐ DEBT-164 — A planning queue row carries two checkboxes — P3

- **Current issue.** A row in "Still to place" draws the shared selection checkbox AND the shared completion circle, because PLAN-01 gave the queue bulk selection over the ordinary Task row ([`app/modules/plan/PlanWorkspace.tsx`](../../app/modules/plan/PlanWorkspace.tsx)). Mockup 7 draws one control per row.
- **Why it was not changed.** Both are useful and they mean different things: one chooses the Task for placement, the other finishes it. Merging them needs a decision about what a single tick on a queue row MEANS — and guessing wrong makes a surface whose purpose is deliberate placement complete work by accident.
- **Impact.** Two adjacent square controls at the head of every queue row, which reads as a duplicate until the owner has used it once. Cosmetic, and only on this one rail.
- **Desired future state.** Either one control with a stated meaning and the other act moved to the row's overflow, or a visual distinction strong enough that the two never read as a pair.
- **Closing condition.** A capture of the rail at 1440 and 393 with one control per row (or two that are unmistakably different), and `plan-weekly-planning.spec.ts` still proving both acts are reachable by keyboard.
- **Related roadmap item.** [UX-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-ux-02--weekly-planning-and-habits-rebuilt-to-the-visual-references--delivered-2026-08-19); PLAN-01.

### ☐ DEBT-165 — A template carries no dates, relative or otherwise — P2

- **Current issue.** A Project template stores no date of any kind, so a project created from one arrives with every task undated and unplanned ([`app/kernel/project-templates/project-template.ts`](../../app/kernel/project-templates/project-template.ts); [ADR-105 §4](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md)). The roadmap item explicitly asked whether "Book venue — due 14 days after the project is created" should ship, and PROJECT-02 answered no.
- **Why it was done that way.** DalyHub already has three date authorities — a due date, the `scheduled_date` that IS the plan (ADR-030), and a recurrence anchor (ADR-062/ADR-085). A relative offset would be a FOURTH: resolved once at instantiation and never re-derivable afterwards, needing its own vocabulary (from creation? from a start date? business days?), its own timezone rule and its own edge cases. PLAN-01 already built the surface where a fresh project gets its days.
- **Impact.** An owner instantiating a twelve-task template plans all twelve from scratch each time, even where the cadence is genuinely fixed ("send the draft on the 10th, present on the 15th"). The create drawer says nothing is scheduled, so it is stated rather than discovered — but it is real work the owner repeats.
- **Desired future state.** A CLOSED offset vocabulary — a whole number of days after project creation, and nothing else — resolved into the existing `due_date`/`scheduled_date` at instantiation, with the owner's timezone deciding the day. Never an expression language.
- **Closing condition.** An ADR fixing the vocabulary and its timezone rule; the offset stored as an integer column on `project_template_tasks`; a kernel test proving a template instantiated in two timezones lands on the owner's day in both; and the create drawer stating the resulting dates before it writes them.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PROJECT-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-project-02--project-templates--delivered-2026-08-19); [ADR-105](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md).

### ☐ DEBT-166 — A template task cannot repeat — P3

- **Current issue.** A template task carries no recurrence rule, so a project made from a template that "always has a weekly check-in" arrives with a single one-off task ([`migrations/0046_create_project_templates.sql`](../../migrations/0046_create_project_templates.sql)).
- **Why it was done that way.** A DalyHub recurrence rule is anchored to concrete dates (`date_kind`, `anchor_day`, and a series whose next occurrence is computed from the last one). A template holds no dates (DEBT-165), so a recurrence rule in a template is not merely undesirable — it is not representable. This one is blocked on that one.
- **Impact.** The owner adds the repeat by hand after creating the project, once per project. Small, and only for templates that genuinely contain routine work.
- **Desired future state.** Once DEBT-165 gives a template task a resolvable first date, a recurrence rule can be carried alongside it and instantiated as a real series.
- **Closing condition.** A kernel test showing a template task with a repeat producing a project task with a live recurrence series whose first occurrence is the resolved date, and no successor generated at instantiation.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PROJECT-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-project-02--project-templates--delivered-2026-08-19); TASKS-12.

### ☐ DEBT-167 — Creating a project from a template requires connectivity — P3

- **Current issue.** PROJECT-02 added no offline mutation: browsing templates works from whatever the PWA has cached, but saving, editing and instantiating all require the network ([`docs/development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md`](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md); [ADR-105 §8](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md)).
- **Why it was done that way.** The PWA-12 replay ledger exists for single, idempotent, narrow Task mutations, and its `operation` set is a closed CHECK constraint for exactly that reason. An instantiation is a compound write of up to ~287 statements whose whole guarantee is atomicity; replaying it offline would mean either giving up that guarantee or reimplementing it client-side.
- **Impact.** An owner on a plane cannot start a project from a template. Given the frequency of the act — a handful of times a month — this was judged a fair boundary rather than a gap.
- **Desired future state.** If it ever matters, the honest shape is a QUEUED INTENT (template id, title, parent) replayed as one server-side instantiation when connectivity returns — never a client-side assembly of the rows.
- **Closing condition.** An offline journey creating a project from a template, coming back online, and proving exactly one project with exactly the right rows — including after a duplicate replay.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PROJECT-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-project-02--project-templates--delivered-2026-08-19); PWA-12.

### ☐ DEBT-168 — A template captures no links and no Knowledge — P3

- **Current issue.** Capturing a project copies its tasks and their checklists, and nothing else: the project's `project.relates_to` links and its Knowledge notes are left behind ([`app/platform/storage/d1/d1-project-template-repository.ts`](../../app/platform/storage/d1/d1-project-template-repository.ts), `createTemplateFromProject`).
- **Why it was done that way.** A link is a relationship between two records that EXIST. Copying "related to the 2026 vendor contract" into a template means either carrying an entity reference that a new project may have no business claiming, or inventing a second kind of soft reference. Neither was worth deciding for PROJECT-02.
- **Impact.** A project whose shape genuinely includes "always linked to the supplier note" has to be relinked once per instantiation.
