- **Desired future state.** A decision about whether a template may hold a reference to a live record at all — and if so, what happens when that record is deleted between saving the template and using it.
- **Closing condition.** An ADR answering that question, and a kernel test proving a template whose referenced record has been deleted still instantiates cleanly.
- **Related roadmap item.** [PROJECT-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-project-02--project-templates--delivered-2026-08-19).

### ☐ DEBT-169 — Dependencies are occurrence-local, so a recurring pair must be relinked every cycle — P3

- **Current issue.** TASKS-12 attaches a dependency to concrete Task OCCURRENCES and never clones it onto a successor ([ADR-107 §7](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-107-advanced-recurrence-widens-a-closed-vocabulary-and-dependencies-are-occurrence-local--one-successor-authority-a-remembered-grid-and-no-relationship-cloning)). An owner whose "Prepare monthly report" genuinely gates their "Submit monthly report" every month re-adds the dependency once a month.
- **Why it was done that way.** An occurrence that has not been created is a prediction, not work, so an edge to one would reference something that may never happen — a series can end, a rule can be removed, an occurrence can be deleted. Cloning would also require guessing which future occurrence of A corresponds to which of B, and the only available mappings ("by date", "by sequence") are each wrong the moment one series is completed late or skipped. Shipping the simple rule and SAYING SO was judged better than shipping a guess.
- **Impact.** One extra interaction per cycle for the small number of recurring pairs that genuinely depend on each other. The product states the rule rather than surprising anyone with it.
- **Desired future state.** If it is wanted, the honest shape is corresponding-occurrence inheritance keyed on SERIES IDENTITY plus sequence, cloned with fresh ids and only when both matching successors can be identified — and it needs a product answer for two series of different cadences before it needs an implementation.
- **Closing condition.** An ADR answering the different-cadence question, plus a kernel test proving occurrence #13 of A blocks occurrence #13 of B and nothing else, including when one series is skipped and when one ends.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-tasks-12--advanced-recurrence--dependencies--delivered-2026-08-19).

### ☐ DEBT-170 — Adding or removing a dependency requires connectivity — P3

- **Current issue.** TASKS-12 added no offline mutation for dependencies or for recurrence rules: reading blocked state works from the cached snapshot, but both writes require the network ([`docs/development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md`](../development/PWA_AND_OFFLINE.md); [ADR-106](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-106-a-task-dependency-is-a-directed-entitylink-not-a-second-join-model--derived-blocked-state-and-cycle--bound-enforcement-inside-the-write)).
- **Why it was done that way.** A dependency's outcome is a property of the GRAPH at the moment of the write — only the server knows whether an edge closes a cycle or crosses a bound — so a queued dependency is an intent the device cannot evaluate and might have to withdraw hours later. A recurrence rule's validity likewise depends on the Task's own anchor date. Building a local dependency engine to decide either would be a second authority for the one thing this programme exists to keep singular.
- **Impact.** An owner offline can complete a blocker (that already queues) but cannot record a new dependency. It also leaves a documented temporary state: the device knows a blocker is complete while the server does not, so the dependent may still read as blocked until the completion replays.
- **Desired future state.** If it matters, a QUEUED INTENT arbitrated server-side on replay — with a refusal the sync surface can state in words when the graph moved underneath it — never a client-side cycle check.
- **Closing condition.** An offline journey adding a dependency, coming back online, and proving exactly one edge — plus a journey whose queued edge is refused because another device closed the cycle first, reported without data loss.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-tasks-12--advanced-recurrence--dependencies--delivered-2026-08-19); PWA-12.

### ☐ DEBT-171 — The recurrence editor states the rule but never the next few dates — P3

- **Current issue.** The shared editor says what a rule MEANS ("The last Friday of every month, moved to the Friday before, 12 times") and never what it will DO ("25 Sep, 30 Oct, 27 Nov…"). For the advanced rules TASKS-12 added, the sentence is harder to verify at a glance than a plain "Every week" was.
- **Why it was done that way.** DalyHub materialises recurrence incrementally ([ADR-062](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-062-intentional-unassigned-tasks-inbox-semantics-and-calendar-recurrence)): exactly one occurrence exists at a time, and a projected calendar is a claim about Tasks that do not exist. `planNextTaskOccurrence` could be iterated to produce a preview, but a preview is a promise, and one that a later edit, a skip or a late completion would silently break.
- **Impact.** An owner authoring "the last Friday, skipping weekends, twelve times" reads a sentence and trusts it, rather than seeing three dates and recognising them.
- **Desired future state.** A read-only "next three dates" line under the summary, computed from the same authority, worded as a projection rather than a schedule ("if nothing changes: …").
- **Closing condition.** A preview whose dates are produced by `planNextTaskOccurrence` and nothing else, with a unit test proving it agrees with what completion actually creates for every rule shape.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-tasks-12--advanced-recurrence--dependencies--delivered-2026-08-19).

### ☐ DEBT-172 — The service-worker budget bounds the worker and its precache manifest as one number — P3

- **Current issue.** `SERVICE_WORKER_MAX_BYTES` in [`e2e/pwa-budget.spec.ts`](../../e2e/pwa-budget.spec.ts) measures `/sw.js` WHOLE — the worker's logic and the list of shell bundles the build substitutes into it. MEASURED 2026-08-19: the logic is 22,925 bytes (`vite-plugins/sw-template.js`, unchanged by TASKS-12) and the manifest is 1,013 bytes / 31 assets on `main`, 1,074 / 33 after TASKS-12 added one resource route. The ceiling was 24,000 with `main` at 23,960 — forty bytes of headroom — so TASKS-12's sixty-five-byte manifest growth failed it.
- **Why it was done that way.** The budget was written for V2.0.1 against a 12 kB worker, where 24,000 was ~2x headroom. The worker doubled over eighteen roadmap items without the number being revisited, and one ceiling over two things that grow for completely different reasons cannot say which one moved.
- **Impact.** A ceiling with forty bytes of headroom stops answering "did anyone triple what a phone downloads?" and starts answering "did anyone add a route?" — which fires on changes that are not regressions and, worse, would stay quiet about real worker growth once someone raises it far enough to stop the noise. TASKS-12 re-baselined it to 27,000 (measured + ~12%) with the numbers recorded in the file, which restores the ratchet but does not fix the conflation.
- **Desired future state.** TWO ceilings: one on the worker's own source (`sw-template.js`, which nothing but a deliberate change to the worker moves) and one on the substituted manifest's byte count — beside `PRECACHE_MAX_ASSETS`, which already bounds the manifest's LENGTH and is the natural place for its size.
- **Closing condition.** Two budgets, each with its own measured value and headroom, and a run proving that adding a route moves only the manifest number.
- **Related roadmap item.** [TASKS-12](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_3.md#-tasks-12--advanced-recurrence--dependencies--delivered-2026-08-19); PWA-10.

### ☐ DEBT-173 — E2E specs assert against the shared workspace's ACCUMULATED state, so re-ordering the suite changes what they see — P2

- **Current issue.** Every partition runs its spec files against one dev server and one local D1 seeded identically, but the specs inside it run in sequence and each leaves its fixtures behind. A spec that asks for *"the first task row"*, or for its own task to be visible in a bounded band, is therefore asserting against *the seed plus whatever every earlier spec in its partition happened to create* — and that set is a property of the SPLIT, not of the product. The split is derived from measurements, so it moves whenever the suite's timings move.
- **Evidence (HARDEN-06A, 2026-08-20).** Three twelve-partition runs, [`32333645709`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/actions/runs/32333645709) (run A, the interim split), [`32338241602`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/actions/runs/32338241602) (run B) and [`32340347468`](https://github.com/acedaly/DalyHub-V2/actions/runs/32340347468) (run C) — B and C on an **identical executable tree**. Every partition of all three FINISHED and executed every test it collected: **36 of 36 partitions, zero unexecuted tests**, so this is not F-03 returning. What varied was which tests passed:
  - **Run B: 0 failures. Run C: 4 partitions red** (p02, p07, p08, p10), each on a different, unrelated test.
  - Three of the four were diagnosed. Two of those three are this entry:
  - `e2e/iphone-daily-driver.spec.ts:185` asserts the overflow sheet's last row sits inside the phone viewport, and measured **853.85 against a 845 limit** — an 8.85 px overrun. It opens the menu of `taskRows(page).first()` on a **bare `/tasks`**, so *which* task, and therefore how many actions the menu offers, is decided by the accumulated collection.
  - `e2e/tasks-journey.spec.ts:379` reopened a P1 task and could not find it in the `Priority 1` band of `/tasks?view=list&system=active&group=priority`. The URL is fully explicit, so this is not the default-view leak HARDEN-06A fixed; the band is bounded and the workspace had accumulated more active P1 tasks than it did on the run before.
  - The third, `e2e/notes.spec.ts:424` (the unsaved-changes navigation guard not appearing), is **not** this entry — it is an ordinary timing race, and it belongs with [DEBT-125](#-debt-125--mains-e2e-suite-is-red-for-reasons-unrelated-to-the-change-that-finds-it--p1--no-longer-broadly-red-and-the-browser-fix-is-holding-the-suite-still-cannot-finish-harden-03-2026-08-12). p10's was not individually diagnosed. Do not fold the two shapes together: one is fixed by giving a spec its own fixture, the other is not.
- **Why HARDEN-06A did not fix it.** It is neither F-03 nor F-11, it is not one defect but a property of ~111 specs, and the repair is per-spec fixtures — the shape `e2e/habits-fixtures.ts` and `e2e/reviews-fixtures.ts` already use. HARDEN-06A fixed the two instances it *caused* (a `main.innerText()` snapshot over a live region, and an owner-level default Tasks view restored only on success) and named this one rather than opening the whole suite in a slice whose stated non-goals include exactly that.
- **Impact, stated precisely.** It does **not** make the gate unable to report — that was F-03, and it is fixed. It makes a green gate *harder to obtain* than it should be, and it means a re-derivation of the split can turn a passing spec red without any product change, which is a bad property for a mechanism that is supposed to be re-derived routinely.
- **Desired future state.** A spec that asserts over "the first row", a bounded band, or a count either creates and cleans up its own fixture, or scopes its assertion to a record it owns by title. No spec's result depends on which other specs preceded it.
- **Closing condition.** The split can be re-derived and the partition assignment materially reshuffled without any spec changing its result, demonstrated by two runs of the same commit under two different derived splits.
- **Related roadmap item.** [HARDEN-06A](HARDEN_06A_FINISHING_E2E_GATE_2026_08.md); the [whole-application audit](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_BUG_AUDIT_2026_08.md) §21, which found the same shape one instance at a time.

### ☐ DEBT-174 — A whole-document autosaving field is not REQUIRED to carry a base version — the contract is per-module, not product-wide — P2

- **Current issue.** THREE modules now write a whole authored document with a base-version precondition folded into the write: Review sections ([REVIEW-02](../roadmap/ROADMAP_V2_1.md#-review-02--weekly-review--delivered-2026-08-05)), the Note body ([AUDIT-FIX-06](#-debt-83--concurrent-note-content-saves-are-blind-last-write-wins--p3--resolved-2026-08-08)) and — since [HARDEN-06B](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_REPAIR_2026_08.md) — a Meeting's agenda and notes. Nothing REQUIRES a fourth to. `useAutosaveField`'s reconciliation contract is opt-in via `serverValue`, the repository precondition is an optional parameter on each repository separately, and no test asserts the shape as a rule over the product.
- **Impact.** It is the exact mechanism by which F-01 happened. The Notes fix was written as *"Notes now follow the same shape [as Reviews]"*, and nothing failed when Meetings shipped a third such field without one — for eleven weeks, during which a Meeting's notes could be silently destroyed by a second writer with no trace and no recovery. A fifth surface that autosaves a document is one PR away from repeating it.
- **What ADR-108 does and does not do.** [ADR-108](../decisions/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#adr-108-three-product-wide-rules-the-whole-application-audit-found-were-per-module-conventions--a-base-version-on-every-whole-document-write-an-owner-day-that-never-travels-without-its-zone-and-maintenance-that-is-not-contact) states the rule, which is why the next author has something to read. It does not ENFORCE it, which is why this entry exists.
- **Desired future state.** A mechanism, not a convention. Two candidates, either acceptable: a shared `useDocumentAutosave` that cannot be constructed without a version and a conflict handler, so the opt-in is structural; or an inventory test that enumerates the whole-document fields (an editor bound to a Markdown column) and asserts each one's repository takes a precondition. The second is cheaper and catches a new surface at the same moment CI would.
- **Closing condition.** A new whole-document autosaving field cannot reach `main` without a base version — demonstrated by adding one without a precondition and watching a test fail, not by inspection.
- **Related finding.** [F-01](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_BUG_AUDIT_2026_08.md#f-01--a-meetings-agenda-and-notes-are-blind-last-write-wins-and-the-overwritten-text-exists-nowhere--p0). **Related roadmap item.** MEET-04; [`SHARED_FORMS.md`](../development/SHARED_FORMS.md).

### ☐ DEBT-175 — The Project record's Tasks tab is the last surface that does not render the shared `TaskRow` — P2

- **Current issue.** `app/modules/projects/ProjectTasksTab.tsx` builds `Card` props by hand. The only three importers of the shared `TaskRow` are `TasksWorkspace.tsx`, `PlanWorkspace.tsx` and `TodayScreen.tsx`. UIX-02 made the Project rows *"the SAME `Card` props Tasks builds"*, which was true then; DS-04 subsequently replaced the Tasks card with `TaskRow` and this tab did not follow. [DEBT-143](#-debt-143--todays-plan-draws-its-own-task-row-not-the-shared-taskrow--p2--resolved-2026-08-17-today-task-01) closed the same fork for Today.
- **Impact.** It is why F-09 was possible: the checklist figure was added to `TaskRow` and the Project tab, being a second implementation, simply did not have it — for the surface an owner works a Project FROM. [HARDEN-06E](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_REPAIR_2026_08.md) made the FIGURE correct (both Project task loaders read the same bounded aggregate, and the row draws it through the shared `checklistProgressLabel`), which closes the defect and not its cause. The next thing added to a Task row will be missing here again.
- **Why it was not done in the repair pass.** It is a real UI change to a record surface, with its own responsive, axe and screenshot consequences, and the audit's own slice plan says to split it out if it grows. Bundling a row redesign into a correctness pass is how a hardening branch stops being reviewable.
- **Desired future state.** `ProjectTasksTab` imports `TaskRow`, and `e2e/helpers.ts`'s `taskRows()` locator finds a Project's tasks too — at which point the comment there stops needing an exception.
- **Closing condition.** The shared row is the only task row implementation in the product, and a fact added to it appears on all four surfaces with no per-surface change.
- **Related finding.** [F-09](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_BUG_AUDIT_2026_08.md#f-09--checklist-progress-is-missing-on-the-one-surface-where-the-steps-matter-most-and-the-projects-task-list-is-still-a-second-task-row--p2). **Related roadmap item.** DS-04; [DEBT-01](#-debt-01--duplicate-card-implementations-per-module--p2).

### ☐ DEBT-176 — The non-secret half of notification settings and calendar sources leaves the export by TABLE rather than by COLUMN — P3

- **Current issue.** `notification_settings`, `notifications`, `notification_deliveries`, `calendar_sources`, `external_calendar_events` and `external_calendar_meeting_links` are absent from the workspace snapshot. Since [HARDEN-06E](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_REPAIR_2026_08.md) the omission is NAMED in `EXPORT_EXCLUSIONS` and documented in [`EXPORT_AND_PORTABILITY.md`](../development/EXPORT_AND_PORTABILITY.md), which is what F-10 was actually about — the silence, not the omission.
- **What is still owed.** Each of those tables holds something that genuinely must not be exported: Pushover credentials, a sealed feed URL, or an operational ledger. But the digest time and its zone, the per-source toggles and a calendar's display NAME are ordinary owner configuration of exactly the kind `owner_app_preferences` and the saved views are already exported as — and they are omitted only because they share a row with a secret. Omitting by column rather than by table would carry them.
- **Impact.** A restored workspace comes back with notifications off, the digest time and its zone gone, the per-source toggles gone and every subscribed calendar gone. The owner is now TOLD that, which is the difference between a documented limitation and a surprise; they still have to set it all up again.
- **Why it was not done in the repair pass.** It adds snapshot collections, which is a schema change with restore, validation and manifest consequences — and it is the same judgement [DEBT-94](#-debt-94--ai-preferences-are-the-one-kind-of-owner-configuration-the-export-snapshot-omits--p3) records for AI preferences, which is still open on its own terms. Deciding both at once, deliberately, is better than deciding this one alone inside a bug-fix branch.
- **Desired future state.** Column-scoped export of the non-secret notification settings and calendar-source names, under `owner`, with the credential columns and the sealed URL omitted by name — resolved together with DEBT-94 so the product has ONE answer to "is configuration exported?".
- **Closing condition.** A restored workspace comes back with the owner's digest time, zone and per-source toggles, and their calendar subscriptions named (unauthenticated, awaiting re-entry of the feed URL) — or a recorded decision that it deliberately does not, consistent with DEBT-94.
- **Related finding.** [F-10](DALYHUB_WHOLE_APP_BUG_AUDIT_2026_08.md#f-10--notification-settings-the-notification-ledger-and-calendar-sources-leave-the-export-silently--p2). **Related roadmap item.** SET-02; NOTIFY-01; CAL-01.

### ☐ DEBT-177 — A completed row does not LEAVE; it disappears on the next revalidation — P3

- **Current issue.** DHDS-08 shipped steps 1–4 and 6 of the canonical completion sequence (the control responds, the ink recedes, the strike draws, the Undo toast). Steps 5 and 6 of the *visual* sequence — the row departing and its neighbours closing the gap — are not implemented. On a surface that removes completed work (`/tasks` in its All-active view), the row is simply absent after the loader revalidates. The mechanism is documented and deliberately unimplemented at `app/styles/motion.css` → "A row LEAVING a surface that removes completed work".
- **Impact.** The smallest of the perceived-quality gaps this phase set out to close, and the one it left: a row vanishing is a discontinuity where a row collapsing would have been an explanation. The Undo toast carries the continuity in the meantime, so nothing is lost or confusing — it is less polished than the rest of the sequence around it.
- **Why it was not done in DHDS-08.** Two reasons, neither of them the animation. **(1) Focus.** The control the owner just clicked is inside the row about to go; holding the row for 260ms while `inert` puts focus on `<body>` for a quarter of a second, and deciding where it should land instead (the next row? the list? the toast's Undo?) is an interaction decision, not a motion one — `AGENTS.md §15` forbids guessing at it. **(2) The surfaces disagree about what completion means.** `/tasks` All-active removes the row; a Completed section keeps it; a Project's history keeps it forever. Animating a row out of existence on a surface that did not remove it would be a lie about what happened, so the departure must be opted into per surface — a wider change than a grammar pass should make alone.
- **Desired future state.** A per-surface opt-in departure: the row's own grid track collapses at `--dh-motion-deliberate`, the neighbours close the gap, and focus is handed to a deliberately chosen target before the row goes. `app/shared/feedback/use-leaving-notifications.ts` is the list-level bookkeeping this would reuse.
- **Closing condition.** Completing a task on a removing surface collapses its row and closes the gap; a keyboard user's focus lands somewhere named rather than on `<body>`; a surface that KEEPS completed work is visibly unaffected.
- **Related roadmap item.** DHDS-11 (drag, reorder and deeper object continuity); [`DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md`](../design/DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md) §21.

### ☐ DEBT-178 — Focus-trapping panels cannot exit, because focus restoration is bound to unmount — P3

- **Current issue.** DHDS-08 published one exit grammar (`data-dh-exit`, `--dh-motion-exit`, `usePresence`) and the toast uses it. The Drawer, Inspector, Sheet and mobile navigation do not: `useDrawerFocus` (`app/shared/drawer/use-drawer-focus.ts`) restores focus to the opener in an **unmount-only** effect, so keeping a panel mounted for its 140ms exit would delay the focus restoration by the same 140ms.
- **Impact.** These four surfaces arrive with the shared grammar and vanish instantly. That is at least *faster* than their entrance, which is the direction DHDS-08 asks for, so nothing reads as broken — but the asymmetry is visible if you look for it, and the shared exit grammar has one consumer where it should have five.
- **Why it was not done in DHDS-08.** `AGENTS.md §15` and DHDS-08 §19 both require that motion never delay or obscure focus. Restoring focus 140ms after a panel closes is exactly that, and the fix is a change to the DS-03 focus contract — decoupling restoration from unmount so it fires when the panel becomes non-interactive rather than when it leaves the tree. That is a focus-management change with its own keyboard and axe consequences, not a motion one.
- **Desired future state.** `useDrawerFocus` restores on an explicit "closing" signal; the four panels take `usePresence`, mark themselves `data-dh-exit` and `inert`, and leave at `--dh-motion-exit` with focus already back on the opener.
- **Closing condition.** Closing a Drawer, an Inspector, a Sheet or the mobile navigation plays the mirrored exit; a keyboard user's focus is on the opener before the panel has finished fading; the existing drawer and inspector focus E2E coverage is unchanged and still passes.
- **Related roadmap item.** DS-03; DHDS-09 (floating surfaces); [`DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md`](../design/DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md) §21.

### ☐ DEBT-179 — Seven E2E tests fail on `main`: a completion checkbox is clicked and never becomes checked — P2

- **Current issue.** `e2e/command-palette.spec.ts` (1), `e2e/today.spec.ts` (4) and `e2e/today-focus.spec.ts` (2) fail against a freshly seeded local database. All seven share one signature: `locator.check()` clicks a task's completion checkbox, Playwright reports the click as performed, the checkbox never becomes checked, and `check()` retries to timeout.
- **How it was established as pre-existing.** DHDS-08 reproduced all seven by checking `app/` out at `157a3f4` (the branch's merge-base) and re-running the same specs against the same reseeded database: the same seven fail, identically. They are not a DHDS-08 regression.
- **Impact.** Seven tests are not protecting what they were written to protect, on the two surfaces where completing a task matters most. Worse, the failure mode is indistinguishable from a real completion defect, so the next agent to touch Today has to re-establish this from scratch — which is what this entry exists to prevent.
- **Why it was not done in DHDS-08.** Diagnosing why an optimistic completion does not settle in these specific fixtures is a different investigation from a motion pass, and it is plausibly a case of [DEBT-173](#-debt-173--e2e-specs-assert-against-the-shared-workspaces-accumulated-state-so-re-ordering-the-suite-changes-what-they-see--p2) (specs asserting against the shared workspace's accumulated state) rather than a product defect at all. Bundling it in would have made a grammar branch unreviewable.
- **Desired future state.** Either the product defect is found and fixed, or the specs are made independent of accumulated workspace state — resolved together with DEBT-173, so the product has one answer to "why did this task-completion assertion fail?".
- **Closing condition.** All seven pass against a freshly seeded database and against the accumulated state a full gate run produces.
- **Two that are NOT that signature, and were investigated separately.** `e2e/tasks-collection.spec.ts:298` **passes in isolation** (9.6s, freshly seeded) and **fails only when run after another spec** — on this branch and on `157a3f4` alike. That is DEBT-173's signature exactly rather than a filter defect, and is distinct from [DEBT-159](#-debt-159--removing-two-filter-chips-in-quick-succession-can-put-one-of-them-back--p3), which is a real ordering bug in the chips themselves. **Confirmed by observation, not just by reasoning:** two consecutive CI runs on the *same branch content* disagreed about it — it failed on `5aade13` and passed on `4d093f2`, with no change between them that touches filtering. A test whose result moves while the code does not is a test reading state it does not own. `e2e/plan-weekly-planning.spec.ts:233` is the row-reveal hit-testing gap, root-caused and recorded as DEBT-180.
- **Impact of leaving it.** With the gate red for unrelated reasons, a genuine regression in a future branch is invisible: it lands in a sea of expected red, and the only way to tell the difference is the base-tree comparison DHDS-08 had to run nineteen times by hand.
- **Related roadmap item.** [DEBT-173](#-debt-173--e2e-specs-assert-against-the-shared-workspaces-accumulated-state-so-re-ordering-the-suite-changes-what-they-see--p2); [`DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md`](../design/DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md) §19.

### ☐ DEBT-180 — The row-reveal contract is unclickable by AUTOMATION: the hidden trigger's own wrapper wins the hit test — P3

- **Current issue.** The shared contextual-action contract (`.dh-action-reveal`, `motion.css`) gives an unrevealed affordance `opacity: 0` **and** `pointer-events: none`, so it cannot be clicked while invisible. Its wrapper — `.dh-overflow-menu`, `position: relative` and exactly the same 32x32 box — keeps `pointer-events: auto`. Measured on `/plan`: at the trigger's own centre, `document.elementsFromPoint` returns `div.dh-overflow-menu`, then `span.dh-taskrow__actions`, then the row. The trigger itself is never in the stack.
- **Impact.** **None for a person.** A pointer user moves over the row, `:hover` flips the affordance to `opacity: 1; pointer-events: auto`, and the click lands. A keyboard user reaches it by focus, which the contract also reveals on. What breaks is AUTOMATION: Playwright's actionability check hit-tests the target point *before* moving the mouse, sees the wrapper, and reports "intercepts pointer events" — so it never performs the hover that would make the element hittable. A deadlock, not a race, so retrying to timeout is the only outcome.
- **Where it bites.** `e2e/plan-weekly-planning.spec.ts:233` ("clearing a plan returns the task to the planning queue") fails on this. Verified pre-existing: the same spec fails identically with `app/` checked out at `157a3f4`, and the `pointer-events: none` half of the contract has been in `base.css` since DHDS-02 — DHDS-08 moved the contract into `motion.css` unchanged. `e2e/motion.spec.ts` works around it by opening the menu from the keyboard, which is the honest path for what that test asserts and is documented there.
- **Why it was not fixed in DHDS-08.** Both candidate fixes are behaviour decisions rather than motion ones, and both are larger than a grammar pass should decide alone:
  1. **Drop `pointer-events: none`.** The affordance becomes clickable while invisible. That is what Todoist and Things do, and it makes the target live where the eye expects it — but it puts an invisible 32px hit area over every row, which can swallow a click meant to open the record. Rule 4 of the contract exists precisely to prevent that.
  2. **Make the specs hover the row first.** Correct — it is what a person does — but it means editing several specs that are not about the reveal at all, to accommodate a contract gap.
- **Desired future state.** A decision between the two, taken once, applied to the contract in `motion.css` and to every consumer at the same time.
- **Closing condition.** `plan-weekly-planning.spec.ts:233` passes without a per-spec workaround, and a click on a row's title still opens the record rather than being swallowed by an invisible affordance.
- **Related roadmap item.** DHDS-02 (the contract's origin); [`DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md`](../design/DHDS_08_MOTION_AND_INTERACTION_GRAMMAR_2026_08.md) §8; DHDS-09 (floating surfaces).

### ☐ DEBT-181 — The Hover Card is the last floating surface outside the shared anchored layer — P3

- **Current issue.** [`app/shared/linked-items/HoverCard.tsx`](../../app/shared/linked-items/HoverCard.tsx) renders its panel as a `position: absolute` `<span>` inside its own trigger (`linked-items.css` → `.dh-hover-card__panel`). Every other floating surface in the product is portalled into the shared overlay layer (`~/shared/anchored`), which is what gives it collision handling, a viewport clamp and immunity from an ancestor's `overflow`.
- **Impact.** A hover card near the right edge of a narrow window, or inside a region that clips its overflow, can be cut or can push the document sideways. There is no measured user report — the surfaces it appears on (`LinkedItemsSection`) are wide — so this is a latent divergence rather than an active defect.
- **Why DHDS-09 did not fix it.** Its dismissal is **pointer containment on the wrapper**: `onMouseLeave` on the element that holds both the trigger and the panel. Portalling the panel makes moving the pointer INTO the card count as leaving the trigger, so the card would close the moment it was reached. Repairing that means giving the card a hover-intent model (a close delay, or a pointer-position test against both rectangles) — a change to the card's interaction, not to its appearance, and outside a floating-surface convergence.
- **What DHDS-09 did do.** The panel now names the anchored LAYER (`--dh-layer-anchored`) rather than a bare `z-index: 20`, which was one of three such numbers in the product and sat below every rung of its own scale. `linked-items.css` is on the short allow-list in `test/unit/floating/floating-grammar.test.ts` for exactly this reason, with the entry pointing here.
- **Desired future state.** `HoverCard` composes `AnchoredSurface` with a hover-intent dismissal that treats the panel as part of the trigger's hover region.
- **Closing condition.** The panel is portalled, `linked-items.css` comes off the allow-list in the floating-grammar test, and a browser test proves the card survives a pointer moving from the trigger into it.
- **Related roadmap item.** [`DHDS_09_FLOATING_SURFACES_AND_CONTEXTUAL_CHOICE_2026_08.md`](../design/DHDS_09_FLOATING_SURFACES_AND_CONTEXTUAL_CHOICE_2026_08.md) §22.

### ☐ DEBT-182 — Tags have no canonical model, so they have no canonical picker — P3

- **Current issue.** Tags are a free-text string list per module (`~/shared/forms/TagsField`, `~/shared/forms/tags.ts`), normalised and de-duplicated but not a first-class entity: there is no tag record, no workspace-wide vocabulary and no rename. Every module that offers tags therefore offers its own suggestion set, and none of them offers search.
- **Impact.** Tagging is slower than it should be and is inconsistent between People, Assets and Notes. It is a real friction rather than a coherence failure, because the *control* is shared even though the vocabulary is not.
- **Why DHDS-09 did not fix it.** A tag picker needs a tag model to pick from, and the DHDS-09 scope boundary rules out introducing a "new tag database model". Building a searchable picker over a free-text list would either invent a vocabulary source (a second model, in the UI layer) or be a combobox with nothing to complete against.
- **Desired future state.** A decision about whether tags are a workspace-scoped entity; if they are, `TagsField` becomes an adapter over the shared `Picker` with a create command, which the picker already supports.
- **Closing condition.** Tags have one vocabulary source, and adding one anywhere in the product is the same interaction.
- **Related roadmap item.** [`DHDS_09_…`](../design/DHDS_09_FLOATING_SURFACES_AND_CONTEXTUAL_CHOICE_2026_08.md) §22; a future People/Assets pass.
